## F-074 Plain-string join keys native; retire the Python execution path [done]
Needs: F-034 · Split out of F-034 (review round 1)
- [x] a plain utf8 join key (single column, then composite), single- and multi-chunk, runs through the native join (`_build_map` / `mx_filter` lookups hash + byte compare) and equals DuckDB; a NULL key never matches (inner, semi and anti)
- [x] the Python execution path is retired for the plans that are native (22/22 TPC-H DataFrame queries)
- known declines to close or keep with a reason: a dictionary column holding NULLs in a string predicate (`pipeline_program.dictionary()` raises "string column has nulls", the leaf filter falls to Python; `tests/test_native_like.py::test_dictionary_column_with_nulls_falls_back_and_answers_like_duckdb`); an aggregate reading a string column with NULLs takes the null-aware Arrow path before the executor (`_aggregate_with_nulls`); SQL Q16/Q7-10/13/15/18/20-22 not fully native (see `scripts/native_coverage.py --sql`); `ILIKE` on plain columns and `isin` of more than 32 values fall to Arrow
- Built (2026-10-17). **Bottleneck**: the join on codes was already native; `encode_keys` made the codes with Arrow `dictionary_encode` + `fill_null` over every row of both sides, and a LEFT join gathered both sides with Arrow `take` (also for int keys); a NULL-bearing column fell off the native gather (`try_gather` declines `null_count`) and a dictionary key was decoded by `cast`. Fixes: (1) new kernel `mx_join_key_codes` in `kernels_aot/joinsemi_cpu.mojo` (`libmxjoin_cpu.so` rebuilt, hashes recorded): one hash-partitioned call encodes the whole key tuple of both sides (utf8, large_utf8, utf8 dictionary, int32/int64/date32, any mix; NULL -> -1, which `join_engine._native_key_codes` turns into side-specific codes); `encode_keys` calls it first when a string key is present (floats, other int widths, MXFRAME_NATIVE=0 keep the old Arrow encoder). (2) `_gather_table` gathers NULL-bearing columns natively without their validity bitmap and puts the NULLs back from NumPy; a negative row number (`null_rows`) is a NULL row, which replaces the Arrow `take` in `_execute_left`; `try_gather(any_device=True)` so a gpu session's join gathers strings natively too. (3) dictionary join keys stay coded to `encode_keys` (the kernel reads them; the old encoder decodes) and are decoded in the output only if the scan deferred them (`_decode_keys`). `tests/test_f074_string_join_keys.py` (79: plain, large_string, dictionary x plain, multi-chunk, composite string+int and string+string, NULL keys, non-ASCII, threaded and skewed sizes; inner/left/semi/anti; cpu + gpu; DuckDB rows + no Arrow compute over 1,000 rows): 77 of 77 fail on main. Suite: `-k "join or sql or tpch"` 6612 passed; full 19713 passed.
- Python-path audit (2026-10-17, `native_coverage.py --sql` at SF 0.17): all 22 DataFrame queries and SQL Q1-Q20, Q22 run with no Arrow compute over 1,000 rows and only native paths (`tests/test_native_strings_tpch.py`); SQL Q21 raises (F-112, correlated `<>`). Fallbacks that stay, with reasons: `large_string` OUTPUT columns and dictionary columns with NULLs still gather with Arrow `take` (the native gather reads utf8 / int32-indexed dictionaries only); float keys and int8/16/uint keys inside a string composite use the Arrow encoder; `_execute_hash_join` / `_legacy` remain the answer for plans `_flatten` cannot take (non-equi residuals gather with `take`); the listed declines below are unchanged (dictionary with NULLs in a string predicate, `_aggregate_with_nulls`, `ILIKE` on plain columns, `isin` over 32 values).

## F-042 SQL front end gaps found in the F-041 review [done]
Needs: none · Pre-existing, found by the F-041 review
- [x] an aggregate without an alias is named `agg_0` (DuckDB: `sum(v)`) and its SELECT position is ignored
- [x] `SELECT kid ... JOIN ... ON k = kid GROUP BY kid` returns the column as `k`
- [x] `GROUP BY ALL` drops the keys
- [x] a duplicate output name (`SELECT x, SUM(v) AS x`) is accepted: is renamed like DuckDB's `duckdb.sql(q).arrow()` / `.df()` does (`x_1`); DuckDB does not raise (`con.execute(q).to_arrow_table()` keeps the duplicate names)
- [x] HAVING is dropped without an error (`... GROUP BY flag HAVING SUM(v) > 25000` gives no Filter); found by the F-046d review
- [x] `COUNT(DISTINCT x)` raises NotImplementedError
- [x] arithmetic on an aggregate (`SUM(v) * 2 AS y`) is not seen as an aggregate: raises "Aggregate.aggs cannot be empty", or the column is silently dropped when another aggregate is present
- built in `sql_frontend.py` only; `tests/test_sql_gaps.py` (166 cases, each checked against DuckDB; unsupported variants raise). Unaliased aggregates take DuckDB's text (`sum(v)`, `count_star()`, `count(DISTINCT v)`); an unaliased expression (`SUM(v) * 2`, `SUM(v + 1)`) raises asking for an alias. HAVING is a Filter above the Aggregate (it may use aggregates the SELECT list lacks, SELECT aliases and group keys). ORDER BY may use an aggregate or an expression over aggregates. `COUNT(DISTINCT x)` becomes `Aggregate(Distinct(Project(keys, x)))` (the native count-distinct path); alone it takes that path; mixed with other aggregates see round 2 (several COUNT(DISTINCT) and `SUM(DISTINCT ..)` raise NotImplementedError). A LEFT JOIN's right key can no longer be referenced (the join drops it; the old code silently read the left key, which has no NULLs); `SELECT s FROM t GROUP BY s` without any aggregate raises. Also checked on SF1 (lineitem / orders, 6 queries equal to DuckDB)
- round 2 (same ticket, from the coordinator): `SELECT DISTINCT` maps to the plan's Distinct (not with GROUP BY / aggregates, nor DISTINCT ON: NotImplementedError); `GROUP BY n` / `ORDER BY n` ordinals like DuckDB; an aggregate in WHERE raises; duplicate output names are renamed like DuckDB (`x`, `x_1`, `x_2`, first free name in SELECT order, also `SELECT a.k, b.k` over a join) instead of raising; COUNT(DISTINCT x) mixed with sum / min / max / count / avg is done by aggregating per (keys, x) and combining the partials per key (no join, so a NULL key stays a group); `t.v, w.v` where both joined tables have a column `v` (the join keeps one copy) now raises instead of returning the left one twice; an ORDER BY name that is a SELECT output name (`SELECT kid ... ORDER BY kid`) uses that column
- review fixes: `COUNT(*)` is `lit(1).count()` (rows) instead of a count of the first column in scope, which was low when that column had NULLs (HAVING COUNT(*) dropped the NULL group, mixed COUNT(DISTINCT) gave 0, a global count was short); the DataFrame API's `col(x).count()` skips NULLs and `lit(1).count()` counts rows on every path, so it shared no cause; the joined-column guard raises only for the qualifier whose copy the join dropped (`t.v` over `t JOIN w` works, `w.v` raises); `ORDER BY .. NULLS FIRST` raises NotImplementedError (the engine sorts NULLs last, which is DuckDB's default, so NULLS LAST works); tests with NULLs in the first column and keys, an all-NULL group and AVG over NULLs
- still open: more than one COUNT(DISTINCT ..) per query; `GROUP BY s` with no aggregate (SELECT DISTINCT is the form that works); unaliased expression group keys need an alias (DuckDB names them `(k + 1)`); `ORDER BY` an alias that names two SELECT columns (DuckDB's pick is unclear, we take the first)

## F-043 SF10: the four queries DuckDB still wins, with general fixes only [todo]
Needs: none · Agreed 2026-10-13 (Abhishek: focus on these, no TPC-H-only tuning)
- [x] Q18 (697 -> ~420 ms, DuckDB 395): too many groups for a partial table per worker -> all workers share the output, updated with atomic add / min / max behind a per-worker 4096-group cache (skewed keys stay local; under 1/16 hits it goes direct); compacted keys are encoded per block (no group id per row, no remap); survivors, HAVING and key decoding in parallel. Non-TPC-H: 20M rows / 5M groups random sum 608 -> ~390 ms, max 869 -> ~500, two keys 1181 -> ~620; skewed 8M rows: uniform 173 -> 61, hot key 22% 150 -> 57, 95% 29 -> 21
- [x] Q4 (224 -> ~120-140 ms, DuckDB 170): runtime join filter: a semi partner more than 2x the fact's rows is filtered through the fact's filtered keys first (skipped when the fact keeps over half its rows)
- [x] Q10 (187 -> ~170 ms, DuckDB 188): with an ORDER BY in the same call, hashed / string-keyed groups are not sorted into key order first; the keys are the sort's last tie-break columns (group-by 88 -> 48 ms)
- [x] Q22 (148 -> ~94 ms, DuckDB 117): native Mojo `str_slice` (new utf8 array) and utf8 `isin` up to 32 values (code points like Arrow)
- [ ] each change helps the general shape (checked on a non-TPC-H case), and no SF1 / SF10 query gets slower

## F-045 ORDER BY ties outside the native executor [done]
Needs: none · Found by the F-043 review
- [x] plans the native executor declines (e.g. date + dictionary-string group keys, or `MXFRAME_NATIVE=0`) break ORDER BY ties in a different order than the native path's key order and than DuckDB; make the fallback's tie-break key order too (matters for F-044's DuckDB checks). Native order: ORDER BY columns, then every group key ascending (strings by bytes). `GraphCompiler._key_tiebreak` appends the keys to a Sort directly above an Aggregate for every non-native path (fused, kernels, GPU, null-key, base compiler); `tests/test_orderby_ties.py`
  Open: unaliased derived keys get no tie-break; with a string key added, sort + LIMIT on fallback paths can't use the native top-k (numbers / dates only), so it ranks every row (correct, slower)

## F-059 ORDER BY ties with a Project between Sort and Aggregate [done]
Needs: F-045
- [x] `groupby(...).agg(...).with_columns(y=2*x).sort(y, desc).limit(10)` differs from DuckDB on both native settings (engine-wide; SQL is not affected because the translator puts the Sort directly on the Aggregate): the tie-break keys must reach a Sort that sits above a Project over the Aggregate. Done in `GraphCompiler._tiebreak_names` / `_key_tiebreak` (follows keys by name/alias through each Project) and an early call in `CustomOpsCompiler` before the materialize barrier, so the native path gets the extended sort too (no Mojo change). A Project that drops a key leaves nothing to tie-break on for that key: the remaining keys still break ties, and ties on the dropped key stay in input order (documented limit); Filter nodes between Sort and Aggregate are walked too. `tests/test_orderby_ties_project.py`

## F-061 Integer overflow past int64 is silent on the exact path [done]
Needs: F-037 · Found by the F-037 review (2026-10-16); high priority: silent wrong results, and F-037 routes more queries here
- [x] `pc.add` / `subtract` / `multiply` in the exact path (`custom_ops.py` ~620) wrap past 2^63 (`a*a*a` with a = 2^30 gives 0, rows dropped); DuckDB raises "Overflow in multiplication of INT64". Use Arrow's `_checked` kernels and raise the same way
- [x] `pc.sum` on int64 and the limb recombine in `np_arrow._int_group_sum` wrap at 2^63 (DuckDB sums int64 into a 128-bit integer): raise, or widen like DuckDB
- [x] tests vs DuckDB for each, both native settings
- Built: `np_arrow.checked_arith` (Arrow `*_checked` kernels, OverflowError "Overflow in multiplication of INT64") at every exact-path `+ - *` (`custom_ops`, `compiler._eval_predicate`); `np_arrow.int_sum` / `_int_group_sum` raise "Overflow in sum of INT64" (a total past int64 raises rather than widening: the SUM column stays int64). Found on the way: when every pipeline declined, `max(a*a*a)` ran in the compiled graph and wrapped to 0. `_aggregate_with_nulls(late=True)` now runs just before the compiled graph and the global shortcuts: `int_sum_inexact(..., preds)` bounds each predicate and aggregate on its own (an uncompilable one cannot hide another; an integer `+ - *` the compiler cannot take counts as unbounded) and sends it to the Arrow path. Group keys, `with_columns` / `select` and sorts on a projection were checked and found to run in Arrow (checked kernels), so they raise too (tests added, each fails on the pre-F-061 code). Known difference: int32 * int32 past int32 but within int64 returns the exact value natively, where DuckDB raises an INT32 overflow. Native declines past 2^53 (F-037). `tests/test_int_overflow.py` (grouped, global, after a join, nulls, GPU).

## F-062 Native merge for partner-row group keys (fused join + aggregate) [superseded]
- Superseded by F-069 (2026-10-16): partner-row group keys take the hashed group path in the fused join (negative and repeated tuples covered by `tests/test_f069_hashed_groups.py`); `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1` on main: DataFrame Q10 `native@cpu`, `try_execute_join:ran`, no declines, no Arrow work. The "no slower fused than unfused" timing goes to the F-058 quiet run
Needs: F-046a slice 3 · From its review (2026-10-16)
- [ ] group keys that are columns of one partner but not its unique key (TPC-H Q10's shape) leave several partner rows per key tuple; slice 3 declines that case (the first version merged them in NumPy, which was per-row Python work and wrong for negative key values). Merge natively in the executor: code the key tuples per partner row (negative and repeated values), then fold the partner-row partials into key-tuple groups
- [ ] tests vs DuckDB with negative and repeated key tuples, asserting the fused path; Q10 no slower fused than unfused on a quiet machine

## F-063 Q17 on device="auto" aborts the process under load [done]
Needs: none · Seen 2026-10-16 in a full-suite run (test_tpch_duckdb, Q17 on auto: exit -6, glibc `pthread_mutex_lock` assertion); passed on rerun. Same family as the rare -11 under load in F-020 / F-022. An abort can't be caught or fallen back from, so it is worse than a wrong answer
- [x] reproduced: `scripts/stress_auto_crash.py --runs 150 --queries 17` (fresh processes, SF1, auto, no extra load, machine load1 10-20): 2 of 150 failed, one `-11` and one `-6` (`tpp.c:83 __pthread_tpp_change_priority` assertion). A synthetic exit-during-warm-up loop gave 2 of 120 and 2 of 150
- [x] core dump (systemd-coredump, read with gdb): crashing thread is `mxframe-gpu-warmup` inside `cuDevicePrimaryCtxRetain` (libcuda, reached by ctypes from the CUDA init); the main thread is in libc `exit()` running static destructors (Arrow thread pool dispose, libcuda teardown). So the process exits while the daemon warm-up thread is still inside the driver; interpreter shutdown does not stop a thread in native code, and `exit()` destroys libcuda's mutexes under it (-6 when a destroyed mutex is locked, -11 otherwise). Not the Mojo runtime, not native executor workers (all idle in pthread_cond_wait). Q17 hits it because it chains two computes, so the 0.1 s idle gap that starts the warm-up lands just before the script ends
- [x] fix: `auto_mode._close_background_gpu_work` (atexit) starts no more background GPU work and joins the warm-up thread, `coproc_fused.close_background` joins resident-upload threads (`_start_thread` refuses to start any once exiting), and the `mxframe-warm` CPU thread. A short script that exits before the warm-up begins waits for nothing; one that exits mid-init now waits for it (up to ~0.4 s, cap 15 s)
- [x] tests (`tests/test_f063_exit_drain.py`): two deterministic ones (exit waits for an in-flight warm-up; none starts once exiting), both fail before the fix; `MXFRAME_STRESS=1` opt-in test, 300 real warm-ups exited at random moments 0.28-0.42 s into CUDA init: 0 failures after the fix (about 1.3% failed before, so 300 runs would show ~4). After the fix `stress_auto_crash.py --runs 200 --queries 17,11,22,1 --load 2`: 200 of 200 exit 0
- [x] reviewer: a full-suite run on a loaded machine should show no more -6 / -11 in test_tpch_duckdb auto cases (F-063 review: 3020 passed at load 8-12, no -6 / -11; 50/50 stress runs clean)

## F-064 Reproducible float aggregates [done]
Needs: F-033 merged · Found by the F-033 review (2026-10-16): silent wrong answers in natural use
- [x] cause: the executor handed morsels to workers through an atomic counter (which worker adds which rows changed per run), and the shared output (many groups, and every fused join + aggregate from 16384 groups) added with atomic float adds (the order of additions changed per run). Q15 written with an uncomputed view (`view.filter(col("tot") == view.scalar(col("tot").max()))`) returned 0 rows in 8 of 8 runs; DuckDB returns 1
- [x] design (round 4, default = main's speed). The defined order: for each morsel (65,536 rows from the first row) a group's rows are added in row order into a sub-total (`nl` lanes, a function of the GROUP COUNT only: 4 up to 1,024 groups, else 1; a global aggregate keeps one lane per SIMD slot), then the sub-totals are added in morsel order. Implementations with the same bits: (1) a table of sub-totals per MORSEL in waves (all tables in one wave when they fit the 128 MB budget; the wave size is the same number `table_ok` checks), morsels claimed dynamically, each wave folded into the output in morsel order; (2) staged (waves of 2 x threads morsels, worker-local staging copied by group range into slots, applied per range in morsel order). **Reproducible** (`compute(reproducible=True)`, `MXFRAME_REPRODUCIBLE=1` as a Python default; plan word 27 bit 1 for `mx_execute`, 15th argument of `mx_pipeline_cpu` for the older pipeline and the coproc CPU share): the defined order everywhere (table path up to 131,072 groups, else staged). **Default**: the defined order only for the global aggregate and fused joins from 16,384 groups (table to 65,536, staged above); every other keyed aggregate and fused joins under 16,384 groups run main's code (restored in `_pipeline`: partial table per worker / atomic shared output), because a table per morsel measured 10-20% slower there. Cheap fixes tried first without effect: one wave, merge chunks of 256 groups, worker state allocated by the worker (in parallel); the cost is the fresh tables (page faults, init, fold), not the setup. A lock-free ring of tables folded in morsel order might close it (not done). The per-call kernels take morsels w, w + n, ... (reproducible for a fixed thread count)
- [x] cost, A/B back to back, quiet machine (load 1-3), 6M rows, 12 threads, min ms over 25 runs, two rounds, main (original libraries) / default / reproducible: keyed 4 groups 4.26, 4.25 / 4.45, 4.46 / 4.44, 4.47; 1k groups 4.76, 4.88 / 4.90, 4.90 / 5.26, 5.16; 2.5k 4.81, 4.94 / 4.99, 4.89 / 5.28, 5.30; 8k 4.85, 4.79 / 5.02, 5.01 / 5.84, 5.80; global 2.56, 2.71 / 2.67, 2.48 / 2.70, 2.53; fused 2k groups 6.51, 5.98 / 6.58, 6.24 / 6.84, 6.70; fused 10k 6.36, 6.55 / 6.89, 6.60 / 7.30, 6.92; fused 16k 6.96, 7.02 / 7.42, 7.24 / 7.99, 7.83; fused 20k 22.73, 22.65 / 8.54, 8.37 / 8.53, 8.32; fused 50k 25.04, 25.17 / 11.98, 12.81 / 11.99, 12.41. Default runs main's code in every row except global and fused >= 16k; its +0-5% there (0.1-0.4 ms, e.g. keyed 4 groups 4.26 -> 4.45) is the extra per-call work (noise (the F-064 review measured the scalar-sharing plan walk at ~6 µs and saw main vs F-064 libraries within noise under the same Python)) or noise, medians within 0-3%. Reproducible: +0 (global), +3-8% (4 to 2.5k groups), +20% (8k keyed), +5-12% fused under 20k, faster than main fused above 16k. The earlier table-path-by-default numbers were +7% (4 groups) to +20% (8k keyed, fused 2k-10k)
- [x] round 3 (review): (1) the flag no longer goes through `os.environ`: `native_exec._reproducible` is a per-thread `ContextVar` set by `compute(reproducible=...)`, `native_exec.reproducible()` reads it (else `MXFRAME_REPRODUCIBLE`, read-only), and it is sent as plan word 27 bit 1 into `_pipeline(..., repro)`; no `getenv` in Mojo. Test: two threads in opposite modes at once, each keeps its semantics. (2) `_plan_key` returns None for the WHOLE key when any part can't be keyed (arrays are keyed by dtype, shape and bytes): views differing only in `isin(np.array([1,2]))` vs `isin(np.array([3]))` no longer share (test). (3) the table path folds a wave of tables (inside 128 MB) into the output in morsel order, wave by wave: reproducible mode has no row limit (test: 24M rows, 8k groups, 6 sums, 10 runs, 0 groups differ); the limit is the table memory: 3 x threads x aggregates x groups x 8 / 2 + threads x groups x 8 bytes <= 128 MB, i.e. about 50 sums at 8,192 groups on 24 threads, beyond which the default takes the faster path. (4) SQL TPC-H Q15 is a plain strict test now (no xfail); a CTE used twice in FROM (a self-join on a float sum) is still computed twice: test at 5,000 groups, documented above 8,192. (5) legacy `LEGACY_MERGE_GROUPS = 16384` for the restored merge, mangled comments fixed, dead lane half and dead fused term removed. (6) sharing is limited to plans that end in an aggregate (small output; the shared plan is computed in full, without the optimizer's pruning and pushdown; documented). (7) test that `reproducible=False` overrides the environment variable
- [x] (superseded by round 4: default fused joins use the table path only from 16,384 groups) fused joins in the default mode used the table path up to 65,536 groups (`FUSED_GROUPS`; measured fused 6M rows, min ms, original / table / staged: 10k groups 7.2 / 8.0 / 8.7, 16k 7.9 / 8.8 / 9.8, 50k 26.3 / 13.2 / 20.4, 200k 25.3 / 23.3 (staged) / 21.7), staged above that; keyed group-bys keep `CROSS_GROUPS` = 8,192
- [x] user-visible class fixed at plan level: a `scalar()` subquery over an aggregate plan the outer plan also contains (Q15 `view.filter(tot == view.scalar(tot.max()))`) shares ONE materialized result (`lazy_frame._resolve_scalars`: the shared plan, found by structural key + identity of in-memory tables, is computed once per `compute`, the subquery aggregates that table, the outer plan reads it). At 200k groups in default mode (the legacy path): 13 of 20 without sharing (measured), 20 of 20 with. SQL Q15 (CTE + scalar subquery over it) takes the same route
- [x] guaranteed in reproducible mode: same input table (same chunking, filter) -> same bits for the native CPU paths, whatever the thread count, the other aggregates, table or staged path (both equal a NumPy implementation of the defined order). Not guaranteed: equality with a sequential or another engine's sum; GPU float atomics (F-067); the per-call kernels across thread counts; a different chunking changes how blocks (and lanes) fall
- [x] cost, rough and noisy (6M rows unless stated, 24 threads, load 6-12, original library vs default vs reproducible, two alternating rounds; min ms; CROSS_GROUPS then 16,384 for these runs): keyed 4 groups 5.3 / 4.9 / 5.4, 800 groups 5.6 / 6.0 / 7.7 (round 2: 5.9 / 5.9 / 6.4), 10k groups (2 aggs) 5.9 / 6.7 / 8.9 (round 2: 6.2 / 6.8 / 7.3), 16k groups 6.0 / 7.7 / 11.0 (round 2: 6.7 / 8.2 / 8.3: the crossover, so `CROSS_GROUPS` was set to 8,192 afterwards: 10k and 16k groups now run the previous code by default), 60k groups (1 agg) 7.1 / 6.8 (legacy) / 15.2 (round 2: 7.7 / 7.1 / 12.2), global 3.0 / 2.9 / 4.1 (round 2: 3.0 / 3.5 / 3.2), fused join 10k groups 7.8 / 7.9 / 8.1, fused join 200k groups 27.1 / 24.2 / 23.6 (faster in both modes), Q18-shape 8M rows / 2M groups 64.1 / 65.3 / 85.0 (round 2: 66.7 / 63.3 / 76.8), same with a 22% hot key 67.0 / 64.0 / 78.9 (round 2: 67.8 / 64.6 / 77.2), F-043 shape 20M rows / 5M groups 256 / 264 / 313 (round 2: 272 / 268 / 280). So default is within noise of the original everywhere (the 8,192-group table path was not re-measured after the change; at 10k groups it cost +0.5-0.7 ms), reproducible mode costs +10-20% on the many-groups aggregates and +2-8 ms on 16k-131k groups; to confirm on a quiet machine
- [x] checks: F-033's uncomputed-view Q15 test strict and 20 of 20; the same shape at 200k groups in default mode 20 of 20 (the plan-level sharing); SQL TPC-H Q15 strict; `tests/test_reproducible_sums.py` (6M rows, 30 runs, 0 groups differ bitwise): default mode for the regimes it covers (global aggregate, fused join 20k and 200k groups); `reproducible=True` for single-table keyed 8k groups, fused join 10k groups, many groups (3M), `tot` independent of the other aggregates at 300 / 800 / 8k groups, 4 / 8 / 16 threads at 300 / 10k / 200k groups, no row limit (24M rows, 8k groups, 6 sums); table path (5k, 10k, 60k groups) and staged path (200k) equal a NumPy reference of the defined order bit for bit; the flag with `MXFRAME_NATIVE=0` (older pipeline, 200k groups: equals the reference); `MXFRAME_REPRODUCIBLE=1` equals `reproducible=True`, `reproducible=False` overrides it; two threads in opposite modes; plan keys with array literals; a CTE self-join with `reproducible=True`
- [ ] open: reproducible mode as a default once the many-groups path is as fast (the staged apply costs 10-20%); GPU float atomics: F-067

## F-065 SQL subqueries [done]
Needs: F-033 · From the F-033 review: SQL-phrased Q11, Q15, Q18, Q21, Q22 get none of the native subquery work
- [x] scalar subquery in WHERE / HAVING / SELECT expressions maps to `LazyFrame.scalar` machinery (`Expr("scalar", plan)`); a correlated comparison in WHERE decorrelates to a join with the per-key aggregate (COUNT refused: the count bug); `x IN (SELECT ...)` / `EXISTS` are semi joins (correlated ones with the equalities as keys), `NOT IN` / `NOT EXISTS` anti joins; NOT IN keeps DuckDB's NULL rules (a NULL in the subquery: no rows; empty subquery: every row, NULL key too) through one scalar over the subquery (empty / no NULL / some NULL) and `x = x` as the NULL test. `WITH` and derived tables. `tests/test_sql_subqueries.py` (108 DuckDB-checked queries incl. NULL and empty cases, 20 forms that must raise)
- [x] forms not supported raise naming the form (IN / EXISTS under OR / NOT / CASE / SELECT, correlated scalar outside a WHERE comparison, correlated `<>` / `<`, correlated NOT IN, UNION, RECURSIVE, ...); an aggregate in the outer WHERE keeps "aggregate functions are not allowed in WHERE"; aggregates inside a subquery are no longer taken for outer aggregates
- [x] what the specification's SQL text needs besides subqueries, added with DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_sql_tpch_forms.py`): comma joins (keys from WHERE, also a key every OR branch has: Q19; the same table twice under aliases), `DATE` / `INTERVAL` constants, `EXTRACT(YEAR ...)`, `SUBSTRING`, `LIKE '%x%'`, exact constant arithmetic (`0.06 - 0.01` is 0.05, not 0.04999...)
- [x] SQL versions of the 22 TPC-H queries (`scripts/tpch_sql/queries.py`, the specification's text and default parameters) run through `mx.sql` and compared with DuckDB (`scripts/tpch_sql/run.py`; `tests/test_sql_tpch.py` at SF 0.017): SF1, 22 queries: **17 run and equal DuckDB** (Q1, 3-12, 14, 17-20, 22), **Q15 runs and is flaky** (equal on some runs, 0 rows on others: its CTE is computed twice and `total_revenue = (SELECT max(total_revenue) ...)` compares two float sums, F-064), **4 raise**: Q2 (`LIKE '%BRASS'`: no suffix match), Q13 (a `NOT LIKE` in a LEFT JOIN's ON), Q16 (`LIKE '%Customer%Complaints%'`), Q21 (correlated `<>`)
- [x] found by the SQL Q18: a group-by on 5 or more keys kept four (the Mojo composite kernel has four slots; the fifth key's values came back as one value for every group, silently); fixed (`_build_group_ids_multikey_mojo` declines more than 4 keys), `tests/test_group_five_keys.py`
- [ ] `scripts/native_coverage.py --sql`: **SQL 8/22 fully native** (Q1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 11, 12, 19) against **17/22 for the DataFrame versions**. Not native, with the reason: Q8, Q14, Q17 (a Project over an Aggregate, SQL's arithmetic on aggregates: `Project(Aggregate(join))` declines "expression not supported by the bytecode compiler", the DataFrame versions compute the aggregate and the arithmetic in two computes), Q10, Q18 (the specification groups by float / wide keys: "group key type float ... not in M0"), Q7, Q9, Q20, Q22 (as the DataFrame versions: M4 strings, renamed leaves), Q15 (equality of two float sums in Arrow), and Q2, Q13, Q16, Q21 raise. Next: the executor taking a Project (or a division) over an aggregate natively, float group keys, and the missing SQL forms above

## F-067 Reproducible float aggregates on the GPU and across a CPU+GPU split [done]
Needs: F-064 · From the F-064 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] cause: `pipeline_gpu.mojo` modes 1 (a shared float64 table per block, shared atomics, folded into the output with global atomics) and 2 (per-row global atomics) added in an order that changed per run: 200 of 200 groups (mode 1) and 70,803 of 100,000 (mode 2) differed between 12 runs of 2M rows on `device="gpu"`; a forced 50/50 split differed in 200 of 200 and 14,631 of 20,000 groups. Modes 0 and 3 (a partial per thread, block rows merged in block order) were already deterministic (pinned by a test). The split's own merge was already fixed: `run_router` sorts partials by first row and the static share puts the GPU on the first rows
- [x] fix: new mode 4 in `pipeline_gpu.mojo`, used when `reproducible` (a 21st argument of `mx_pipeline_gpu`, read like the CPU's: `native_exec.reproducible()`, passed explicitly into the router's worker threads) replaces modes 1 and 2: per tile each row's group id goes to shared memory, the thread owning group g (g % 256) walks the tile's rows in row order and adds the matching ones to the block's shared table, the table is written to a row of `scratch`, `merge_kernel` adds the rows in block order. Holds (slots + 1) x groups <= `REPRO_MAX_NS` = 4,096 (about 1,300 groups with two sums) and what fits next to the register file; beyond that `mx_pipeline_gpu` returns -6 (`GpuUnsupported`) and the CPU's defined order runs (path `fused_pipeline_cpu`). Default mode is unchanged (atomics). Mode choice moved into `_choose_mode`, also exported as `mx_pipeline_gpu_mode`, which `_split` asks (`pk.gpu_reproducible_fits`) before sharing rows: a GPU share declined and redone on the CPU would have added the rows in a different order than the CPU alone (found by the 20,000-group split test, 17,066 groups differing between the first and later runs, because the failure also evicted the resident copy)
- [x] `auto` with `reproducible=True` (review): `_choose_device` returns cpu, `native executor (reproducible: cpu defined order)`, before any calibration-driven choice (the split fraction moved with every `observe`: 12 bit patterns in 15 runs; a drifting estimate could also flip gpu and cpu). The GPU kernel is for `device="gpu"`, a split for an explicit `gpu_fraction`. Test: `test_auto_with_reproducible_runs_the_cpu_defined_order_and_says_so` (splits without the flag, path `native` with it; fails with the check disabled)
- [x] `auto` and the Q15 shape: no code needed. F-064's `_resolve_scalars` computes the shared aggregate once, with the compute call's device, and the subquery and the outer plan read that one table, so they cannot land on different devices or bits. Proof: `test_a_view_compared_with_its_own_maximum_runs_its_aggregate_once[gpu, auto]` counts nested computes over the 2M-row table (exactly one, on the requested device, 5 times of 5, one row each); it fails with `_scalar_inputs` stubbed out. Not covered: a CTE used twice in a FROM (computed twice, F-064)

Fail on main (shown 2026-10-16): `test_gpu_group_by_gives_the_same_bits_every_run[200]` (200 of 200 groups differ), `[100000]` (70,803 of 100,000), `test_forced_split_gives_the_same_bits_every_run[200]` (200 of 200), `[20000]` (14,631 of 20,000), `test_a_split_merges_the_gpu_share_then_the_cpu_share_in_row_order` (last bit). ## F-068 Arithmetic over an aggregate runs natively [done]
Needs: F-065 · From F-065 (2026-10-16): the main reason SQL-phrased TPC-H is 8/22 fully native vs 17/22 for the DataFrame versions
- [x] a Project (or division, CASE) over an Aggregate (SQL `100 * SUM(a) / SUM(b)`, Q8 / Q14 / Q17) runs the aggregate natively and the small per-group arithmetic after it, instead of declining the whole plan ("expression not supported by the bytecode compiler")
  - Built: the root cause was ordering, not the arithmetic: `_compile_and_run` materialized the joins before it peeled a Project / Filter / Aggregate off the aggregate's result (`_materialize_barrier`), so the fused join aggregate (`try_execute_join`) never saw `Project(Aggregate(join))` and the join result was decoded to plain strings. `CustomOpsCompiler._aggregate_consumers_first` now runs that barrier first (sort / limit kept above it, group-key tie-break added as before), so the aggregate goes through the single-table, fused-join and star paths and the arithmetic runs on the G-row result. That finishing step is `custom_ops._np_float_project`: `+ - * /` over float64 columns without nulls and numeric literals in NumPy (IEEE like DuckDB: x / 0 = inf, 0 / 0 = NaN, empty global aggregate = NULL), no Arrow compute; integers, strings and CASE keep the Arrow path (checked kernels, F-061). SQL `/` of an int64 aggregate casts to float64 with `safe=False` like DuckDB (a sum past 2^53 rounds instead of raising ArrowInvalid). Found on the way: `EXTRACT(YEAR)` (`year_days`) was not in the bytecode compiler, so Q8's key declined: `_Builder._year_days` compiles it as `y0 + sum(day >= first day of year)` over the years the column's data spans (one cached min / max pass; nulls decline; so does a span of more than about 60 years (each year adds a constant, the pool holds 64: 1900-1999 gives "too many constants", reported as "expression not supported") and a span within one calendar year (a constant expression: "no columns")). `tests/test_agg_arithmetic.py` (21, vs DuckDB with hook-outcome path asserts; 9 fail on the old code, the other 12 pin behaviour the single-table path already had: ratios, constants, CASE, zero divisors, HAVING, ORDER BY ratio + LIMIT, ties).
  - Known: `sum(int64) + 1` past 2^53 still raises ArrowInvalid (the SQL front end turns the literal into 1.0, `_float_expr`); only `/` is lossy-by-design like DuckDB
- [x] SQL Q14 and Q17 fully native (`scripts/native_coverage.py --sql`), equal to DuckDB. Met. **SQL 8/22 -> 10/22** (Q1, 3, 4, 5, 6, 11, 12, 14, 17, 19); DataFrame coverage 17/22 unchanged. Q8 moved to F-071 below: its aggregates already run in `try_execute_join` and equal DuckDB, but one Arrow `dictionary_encode` of 10,000 rows (the supplier x nation partner's `n_name`) remains

## F-069 Float and wide group keys in the native executor [done]
From F-065: SQL Q10 and Q18 group by float / wide keys as the specification writes them, and the executor declines
- [x] float32 / float64 group keys (exact bit equality; every NaN one group, -0.0 with 0.0, NaN after +inf as DuckDB) and key tuples wider than direct addressing take a partitioned hash aggregate (`kernels_aot/hashagg_cpu.mojo`, design in docs/design/mojo-executor.md "Hashed groups") in the single-table executor and the fused join v3 (float partner columns, wide fact / partner keys, Q10's per-customer keys). Output in key order; NULL in a key declines; DISTINCT over float keys declines early. `tests/test_f069_hashed_groups.py` (28, vs DuckDB, path asserts via `nx.last_group_path()`; all failed on the old code), old tests that pinned the declines updated.
- [x] reproducible mode follows F-064's defined order (bit-identical across runs and thread counts, equal to the NumPy reference)
- [x] SQL Q18 fully native; SQL Q10: equal to DuckDB at SF0.17 with no native decline, but 4 Arrow dictionary_encode calls over the customer string columns remain (not fully native by `scripts/native_coverage.py`) -> F-076 (test renamed to what it proves)
- [x] memory: records grow with rows, +630 MB at 10M rows / 1M groups (Arrow +140 MB); frees per partition, group-sized arrays, and a guard at a third of MemAvailable that declines to Arrow (design doc "Memory bound"; `tests/test_f069_hashed_groups.py::test_a_record_budget...`). Spilling / early folding is the remaining bound.
- [x] not slower than the faster of the old executor and Arrow, within noise (`scripts/bench_hashed_groups.py --rows 2000000 --repeat 3`, median, loaded machine, ms new / old executor / Arrow): float key 50 groups 5 / 17 / 20; 5k groups 6 / 17 / 15; 1M groups 92 / 141 / 130; wide int64 pair 1M groups 272 / 3398 / 344; 3 wide keys 5k groups 10 / 36 / 156; float + wide key with filter 75 / 297 / 375; fused float partner key 32 / 31 / 37; fused wide fact keys 220 / 3960 / 351. The first run (6M rows) showed 22 s for the wide pair: phase timing (2M rows, 2M groups, ms) hash aggregate 158 (local+flush 114, merge 22, copy 21), key-order finishing 7213, output 97. Root cause: the key-order sort of the groups (a single-threaded merge sort, two rank sorts plus a tuple sort, each compare reading through `order` at random). Hashing was never the problem (keys go through the splitmix finalizer, 64 partitions of about equal size, tables sized to their records). Fix: key words gathered into contiguous records and sorted by a parallel merge sort (`_sort_records`; `_psort` for the ORDER BY paths): finishing 7213 -> ~160 ms; float key 1M groups finishing 902 -> 36. No decline guard needed.

## F-070 NaN compares like DuckDB [done]
From the F-068 review (2026-10-16): pre-existing silent wrong answer
- [x] DuckDB orders NaN above every value and treats NaN = NaN as true; MXFrame drops a NaN group in HAVING (`HAVING 0/0 > 1`) and in a WHERE over an aggregate result. Make comparisons on float columns follow DuckDB's NaN rules on every path (native bytecode, Arrow fallback, GPU), and sorts put NaN where DuckDB does
  - Built, per path (probed with DuckDB first: `x > 1`, `x >= 1`, `x <> 1` true for NaN, `x = 'nan'` matches, `x <> x` false, NaN above `inf`, MIN skips NaN, MAX is NaN, DESC puts NaN first, NULL last either way):
    - bytecode (`pipeline_program.py`, `kernels_aot/pipeline_cpu.mojo`, so also the native executor's filters, aggregates and fused joins): a compare instruction carries `NAN_AWARE` in its last word (CMPC: bit 8 of its compare code) and the kernels add branch-free masks (`x.gt(y) | (~x.eq(x) & y.eq(y))`, ...). It is emitted only where NaN can tell the difference: never for integers, and against a constant that is not NaN only for `x > c`, `x >= c` (and `c < x`, `c <= x`); `x < 1.0`, `x = 1.5` run the old instructions. BETWEEN stays the integer instruction (float BETWEEN is two compares); a NaN literal is allowed in programs (it was "unsupported"); `isin` of floats is the same compare chain
    - GPU (`pipeline_gpu.mojo`): the same rules in `_cmp_f` / `_cmp_d`, one flag word
    - native HAVING (`executor_cpu.mojo` `_cmp`): NaN-aware (aggregate outputs are float64)
    - Arrow fallback (`compiler.py` `_PC_CMP_OPS`, used by both Arrow evaluators): the six compares are NaN-aware with Kleene logic, so NULL stays NULL; `np_arrow.is_in` (SQL `IN`): NaN matches NaN, -0.0 matches 0.0 (Arrow's hash lookup told the zeros apart), and NULL gives NULL (`NOT IN` kept the NULL rows: a separate silent wrong answer, fixed because the requested NULL x NOT IN test hit it)
    - MIN / MAX in the null-aware Arrow aggregates (`np_arrow.group_aggregate`, `float_extreme`, the global numpy path): MAX is NaN when the group holds one, MIN skips NaN (the bytecode path already did, with a hidden NaN count)
    - sorts: the sort key encoder (`_encode_sort_key`) canonicalizes NaN (a negative NaN from `inf - inf` and a positive one ranked as two values, so ties on NaN lost their tie-break); the native HAVING/top-k already declined on NaN sort values
    - SQL front end: `CAST('nan' | 'inf' | '-inf' AS DOUBLE | FLOAT)` is a float literal (also in an `IN` list); DataFrame code passes `float("nan")`
    - kernels rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh` (cpu, exec, gpu), hashes recorded by the script
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests with expected answers built from a native DuckDB table (a registered Arrow table behaved differently in the review's probe), both native settings
  - `tests/test_f070_nan_compares.py` (4306): float32 and float64 x with and without NULLs x cpu and gpu x native on and off; WHERE (aggregate, grouped, plain row filter, CASE WHEN), HAVING (grouped and global), WHERE over an aggregate result, MIN / MAX, sorts, `>`, `>=`, `<`, `<=`, `=`, `<>`, BETWEEN, IN (also 19 values, which the programs decline), NOT, AND / OR, NaN / +-inf / -0.0 / NULL data and constants, pinned answers, LazyFrame literals, the CAST literal, a Parquet file with NaN row groups, and the instruction flags (integers untouched). Each asserts the path that answered (`last_compile_provenance`, the native filter). On the parent commit 3207 of them fail (every test function fails there); 50 of the 265 query cases never fail on it because the old and new rules agree (`x < 1.0`, `x = 1.5`, `x between 0.0 and 2.0`, sorts without payload variants, ...): they pin what must not change
  - also run (not committed): a 1312-query fuzz against DuckDB at 5,000 and 200,000 rows with a 9-value palette and 10% NULLs, and 88 float-partner join queries: 0 differences
- [ ] float filters not slower: the compare change adds 3-4 SIMD ops to `x > c` / `x >= c` on float columns and to float column-vs-column compares, none elsewhere. Not measured (shared machine). On a quiet machine (load < 2 for 5 min): SF10 Q6 (float32 `l_discount`, `l_quantity` compares: `x < c`, `x <= c` unchanged; `l_discount >= c` is the flagged one) and a float `x > c` count over 100M rows, fresh process each, median of 7, this tree vs `git archive HEAD~N` of the parent in a second directory, alternating; `MXFRAME_NATIVE=1` and `0`, `device="cpu"` and `"gpu"`
- Found, not fixed (outside this ticket): (1) `fused_pipeline_cpu` (`MXFRAME_NATIVE=0`) on 200,000 rows reads the denormal 5e-324 as 0 (`x > 0.0` counts 0 of 100,000; native and a 20-row table are right: repro `N=200000`, `where x > 0.0`); (2) `ORDER BY` a column that is not in the select list raises `KeyError: Field "x" does not exist`; (3) window `min` / `max` (`custom_ops` window path) use `arr.min()`, which propagates NaN; (4) the MAX-graph GPU predicate path (`_CMP_OPS`, only on datacenter GPUs, unreachable on this machine) still compares IEEE; (5) join keys: NaN = NaN in DuckDB, not checked here (F-077 is on float group keys)

## F-071 SQL Q8 fully native [done]
From F-068: Q8's aggregates run natively but the nested supplier x nation partner star decodes `n_name` (one Arrow `dictionary_encode` over 10k rows); at SF10 the partner (100k rows) exceeds `_DICT_MAX`
- [x] the nested partner star keeps dictionary codes for columns the fused aggregate reads (drop the stashed partners if the fused call declines); SQL Q8 fully native at SF1 and SF10 per `scripts/native_coverage.py --sql`
  - Built: two causes. (1) Q8 scans `nation` twice, so the join renames the second copy's `n_name` to `n_name_right` and the plan reads that name: `io_parquet.resolve_plan` found no reference to `n_name`, so it decoded the column up front (plain strings; the 25 names became 10k strings in the supplier x nation partner, then re-encoded by `try_execute_join`). `deferrable` and the keep test now look through the rename (`hit`, via `_renamed_ref`, as column pruning already did). (2) `fused_join_aggregate` widens the star's `coded` set to every deferred dictionary column of the non-fact leaves: the aggregate reads them as dictionaries whatever it does (CASE, group key, predicate; the bytecode compiler takes dictionaries), so the partner build keeps int32 codes (nested stars too). If the fused call declines, the stash drops those partners (the old path rebuilds with `setup`'s own `coded`: strings). `_DICT_MAX` limits distinct values of the dictionary (25 here), not partner rows: SF10's 100k-row supplier needed no change. SQL Q8 fully native at SF1 and SF10 (no Arrow compute over >= 1000 rows); SQL coverage 10/22 -> 11/22. `tests/test_nested_partner_codes.py` (6, vs DuckDB, path + partner-type asserts: CASE, group key + CASE, predicate inside a CASE, partner past `_DICT_MAX` rows, a table joined twice, decline hands strings to the old path; the first five fail on the old code, the last fails with the stash drop removed)
- [ ] no DataFrame query slower; equal to DuckDB (timing on a quiet machine: not measured here)

## F-072 MIN / MAX of a dictionary string column [done]
Found by the F-071 review (2026-10-16); fails the same on main before F-071
- [x] `select k, max(s) from a group by k` with `s` dictionary-encoded raises KeyError in the bytecode compiler (`nodes[args[0]]`), single table, no join; plain-string partner columns fail too. MIN / MAX over strings (byte order like DuckDB) on every path; native where the dictionary allows (order the dictionary once, then min/max of ranks)
  - Built (`custom_ops._string_minmax`, `_rank_strings`; no Mojo touched): before any path runs, an `[Sort Limit] [HAVING] Aggregate` with `min` / `max` of a string column (dictionary, plain, multi-chunk with a dictionary per chunk: unified by `arrow_dict.encode_key`) ranks the column's distinct values once in byte order (`sort_indices` on the dictionary, which is DuckDB's order: '' first, 'Z' < 'a', 'e' < 'é' < '日本'), adds an int32 rank column (NULL stays NULL) to the Scan that holds the column (so a partner column goes through the fused join as an int partner column), runs the aggregate on the ranks through the existing paths, and turns the ranks back into strings (`take` of the sorted values; NULL stays NULL). HAVING / ORDER BY / LIMIT run above the string result. A name no leaf holds exactly once (a join's renamed `s_right`, both sides having `s`, a Project in the way) is ranked on the materialized input instead. Results are strings for dictionary columns too. Paths (asserted in `tests/test_string_minmax.py`, 21 tests, all equal DuckDB; 14 of them raised KeyError / ValueError on main): single table grouped and global, filter: `native`; partner column: `fused_join: True`; renamed column: `native` after the join; a column with NULLs: `arrow_null_semantics` (the executor declines NULLs; an all-NULL group gives NULL), not native yet. Also checked by hand: `device="auto"` native, `device="gpu"` fused_pipeline_gpu.
  - Open: a nullable dictionary column is decoded to plain strings by `encode_key` before ranking (per-row string work); native NULL handling for string min / max follows the executor's NULL support; a GPU session runs the aggregate on a CPU compiler when the column has more than `_GPU_EXACT_RANKS` (2^24) distinct values, since GPU paths carry ranks as float32 (test lowers the constant); the rank column costs one `take` over all rows even when a filter keeps few (rank the surviving codes if that shows up)

## F-073 Random query seed 7129 returns rows out of ORDER BY order [done]
Found by the F-071 review: `scripts/bench_random` SF1 seed 7129 (single table, ORDER BY AVG(l_tax) DESC, ...) is WRONG "not in ORDER BY order" every run, on main before F-071 too. A silent wrong answer until shown otherwise
- [x] reproduced (`scripts/bench_random` seed 7129, SF1: `SELECT l_shipinstruct, l_suppkey, AVG(l_tax) a0, COUNT(l_quantity) a1, SUM(l_discount) a2 ... GROUP BY l_shipinstruct, l_suppkey ORDER BY a0 DESC, l_shipinstruct, l_suppkey`, 30,000 groups). Finding: **(a), a checker bug, not an engine bug.** MXFrame's output equals DuckDB's bit for bit on all 30,000 rows (every column, exact `==`) and is strictly in ORDER BY order by its own values (0 violations; DuckDB's too). So there are no near-tie differences between the engines here either. Cause: `run.compare` called two rows tied when the sort floats were within TIE (1e-6, absolute for values near 0.03) and then demanded the NEXT sort key in order, so inside any run of averages ~1e-6 apart (30,000 groups sit that densely) the ascending key order was "violated" by rows that are correctly sorted by value (e.g. rows 57 / 58: a0 0.0461849701 > 0.0461842096, keys 'DELIVER IN PERSON' then 'COLLECT COD'; 11,989 false violations). Near-equality is not transitive and is no tie.
- [x] fix in `scripts/bench_random/run.py` (`out_of_order`): the first sort column on which two adjacent rows are not exactly equal decides; a float pair within TIE there is a near-tie (engines summing in a different order differ in the last bits): either order is accepted and later columns are not consulted. Exactly equal values still demand the key order; a clearly wrong direction is still WRONG. Documented in `out_of_order`'s docstring. `tests/test_bench_random_compare.py` (4: seed 7129's rows, exact ties need key order, clear violation, last-bit swap; the first and last fail on the old checker)
- [x] scan: seeds 0-299 plus 7129 at SF1, MXFrame vs DuckDB, correctness only (no timing; each query in fresh processes, ~25 min at load 16-18). Old checker: 6 WRONG "not in ORDER BY order": 110 (topn, `ORDER BY c_acctbal DESC, o_orderkey`), 149, 213, 277, 285 (agg / join, ORDER BY a float aggregate then keys) and 7129; new checker: 301 of 301 ok, no error. So the random suite's "100/100 equal" claims were seeds that don't contain such dense float orderings; no engine ordering defect found. No engine code changed, so the full suite was not rerun

## F-060 Exact integers without a pre-pass; integer SUM is int64 everywhere [done]
Needs: F-037, F-046a slice 3 merged (same Mojo files) · From the F-037 review
- moved to F-060b: replace F-037's min/max pre-pass (int32*int32, int64*int64 and every int64 SUM read the data once per query) with a runtime overflow flag in the bytecode interpreter: compute, flag any |result| past 2^53 per morsel, and rerun on the exact path only when flagged. **Half done:** the SUM no longer asks for any bound (a plain `sum(int64_col)` reads nothing before the kernel runs; the int64 column range check that guards the float64 loads, F-032, is what remains); the `+ - *` bound in `_Builder._arith` still reads a column's min / max once per query when the column types alone cannot prove 2^53 (int32 * int32, any int64 product). The interpreter flag for that (`_binop` + `_run_ops` on the CPU, `_run` on the GPU, then dropping `col_bound(tight)` and `Inexact`, and routing every integer `+ - *` that reaches the late stage to Arrow) is the follow-up: F-060b
- [x] integer SUM returns int64 on every path regardless of size or nulls (native executor, fused pipeline CPU and GPU, CPU+GPU split, Arrow NULL-aware path, streamed), the value DuckDB returns; the type depends on the input type only. Decision (probed on DuckDB 1.5.1): DuckDB's `sum` of every integer type, `TINYINT` to `UBIGINT`, is `HUGEINT` (`decimal128(38, 0)` in Arrow), not the integer type; the engine keeps int64 and **a total past int64 raises** `OverflowError: Overflow in sum of INT64` (a decimal128 / int128 result would change the type of every integer sum and of every expression over it, and needs 128-bit accumulation on the GPU); documented in docs/api.md ("Integer SUM and AVG")
- [x] `mean` of large int64 values: integer accumulation, not float64 (`imean` outputs: the kernels add int64 and the executor divides; the Arrow path adds with `int_sum` / `_int_group_sum`)
- moved to F-060c: integer `min` / `max` on the exact path return float64 (lossy past 2^53): return int64 (found by F-061). Not done: the list for this build was SUM; `max(int64 col)` past 2^53 still returns a double or raises on the native paths (F-060c)
- [x] `pipeline_program._int_arith` treats an uncompilable expression as unbounded only when all its columns are integers; one mixing a string or float column with an overflowing int product might still reach the compiled graph (no repro found by the F-061 review): **unchanged** (the late stage `int_sum_inexact(aggs, table, preds)` still bounds each predicate and aggregate on its own); the full closure is the runtime flag of the first bullet
- [x] sums whose expression the compiler can't take keep the old float path today (`int_sum_inexact` returns False): covered. `custom_ops._arrow_aggregate_reason(late=True)` sends any `sum` / `mean` of an integer expression (`pipeline_program.has_int_sum`) that gets past the native executor and the fused pipelines to Arrow's checked int64 sum; the compiled graph and the global shortcuts never see an integer sum
- Built (2026-10-16). Kernels (Mojo): new bytecode op `OP_AGG_ISUM = 53` (`kernels_aot/pipeline_cpu.mojo`, `hashagg_cpu.mojo`, `executor_cpu.mojo`, `pipeline_gpu.mojo`). An integer sum's slot is a float64 word that holds an int64 (`_ibits`); every place that added a float64 now adds an int64 for that op: the global SIMD lanes, the grouped lanes, the shared output's atomic `fetch_add` and its per-worker cache, the legacy per-worker merge, the table of sub-totals and its merge, the staged path's bins, the hashed groups' records and partition merge, and on the GPU mode 0 (per-thread), 3 (per-thread in shared memory), 1 (shared-table atomics, flushed with a global atomic), 2 (global atomics), 4 (ordered, F-067), the warp reduce and the block merge. F-082's `par` / `par_n` / `sync_par` wrappers are untouched. **Overflow:** every int64 add checks `((a ^ s) & (b ^ s)) < 0` and sets a flag cell (`Prog.ovf`; `ovf_a` on the GPU entry point, one more argument of `mx_pipeline_gpu`); when it is set the call returns `E_OVERFLOW (-21)` (hashagg: -7), Python declines (`SumOverflow` in the pipeline paths, "executor declined" in the executor) and Arrow's checked sum answers: exact when only a running total passed int64 and came back (rows of both signs), `OverflowError` when the final total does. Executor outputs: `O_ISUM` (int64 words, exact) and `O_IMEAN` (float64); an `ORDER BY` or `HAVING` over an integer sum with a value past 2^53 declines (`E_INEXACT`: the executor sorts and compares doubles) and Arrow answers. Rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh cpu` and `gpu` (hashes recorded). Python: `pipeline_program` compiles integer `sum` / `mean` to slot kind `isum` (outputs `isum` / `imean`), `Partials.ints`, `checked_int_add` in `merge_partials`, `finalize`; `native_exec` (`_OPS`, `_wrap_result`, `range_partials` for the CPU share of a split); `pipeline_exec` types the result and declines on `SumOverflow`; `pipeline_kernels` (`E_OVERFLOW`, the GPU flag cell); `custom_ops` / `np_arrow` (Arrow path: integer sums always int64, integer `mean` = exact int64 total / count); `sql_frontend._is_int_expr` treats `SUM` of integers as an integer (`sum(i) + 1` stays int64). **Removed:** `pipeline_program.SumProbe` / `sum_probe` / `_probe`, the sum bound and its `Inexact` in `_compile`, `within_exact(scale)`, `int_sum_inexact`'s sum branch, `streamed._Spec.int_sums`, the probe plumbing in `_Runner` / `_State` and the end-of-merge cast to double (streamed and in-memory types are int64 by construction).
- Tests: `tests/test_f060_int_sum.py` (188: every integer width, grouped and global, cpu / gpu / auto, native on / off, expressions, filters, NULLs, empty input, `avg`, overflow, SQL, streamed at three budgets, all against DuckDB's exact values with the result type asserted), `tests/test_f060_int_sum_kernels.py` (87: each kernel against DuckDB's totals past 2^53 with both signs: native direct / hashed, the older pipeline (per-worker partials, shared atomics at 1M groups, table path, staged path in `reproducible=True`), the fused join, the GPU's five modes; overflow on each; the largest total that fits; an `ORDER BY` / `HAVING` on totals 2^54 + k). On `main` (its code exported to a scratch directory with the new tests added): 122 of 273 fail (types are doubles; the overflow tests already passed from F-061). Updated, intent kept: `test_f056_streamed.py` (the "near the limit is a double" and "small sum is a double" tests now assert int64 at every budget; the SumProbe "never hears from a batch" test is deleted with the probe), `test_f056_step3a.py` (the `_State` signature), `test_int_arith_2_53.py` (integer sums compile whatever rows x max is; their slot is `isum`), `test_f057_coproc.py` (a full split check no longer sizes an integer sum: no `_minmax` / `col_bound` scan).
- Full suite once on the F-060 build, output in `/tmp/f060_full.txt`: 16,431 passed, 6 failed (all six pinned the old double type or the probe; fixed as above and rerun green), 20 minutes at load 8-9 on the shared machine.
- [ ] **Quiet-machine check (not run: the machine is shared, no benchmarks).** int64 accumulation replaces float64 accumulation and every add is overflow-checked, so speed may change. On an idle machine (load under 2, nothing on the GPU): `pixi run python scripts/bench_int_sums.py --sf 1 --label before` on `main`, then `--label after` on this branch (and `--device gpu`); it runs integer-column variants of TPC-H Q1 (four int sums, 4 groups), Q6 (one global int sum) and Q18's inner query (an int sum over 1.5M groups), one fresh process each, min and median of 7. Accept when each query is within 5% of "before". The TPC-H files hold float32 for the money columns, so the usual float Q1 / Q6 / Q18 sums are untouched by this ticket and their numbers must not move; if the shared-output atomics (Q18) or the GPU's mode 2 are slower, the checked add there is the first suspect.
- Follow-ups found, not done: F-060b (the interpreter overflow flag, first bullet), F-060c (integer `min` / `max` exact and int64), window sums (`expr.over(...)`, `cum_sum`) are float64 (documented in api.md); `int32 * int32` past int32 still returns the exact value natively where DuckDB raises an INT32 overflow (F-061 note).

## F-046 Close the CPU gaps to DuckDB (next release) [doing]
Needs: none · Agreed 2026-10-13 (Abhishek: for the next release, on the strong baseline). General fixes only; every slice measured on TPC-H (stored and shuffled) and the random suite, and must not slow any other query. MojoFrame (illinoisdata/MojoFrame, arXiv 2505.04080) checked: its techniques (integer ids for low-cardinality strings, tuple-hash group-by, factorize-then-join) are already covered here; the slices below follow the database literature instead
- [ ] F-046a joins: profile the random joins (0.64-0.86x DuckDB) and TPC-H Q3 / Q5 / Q8 / Q12 / Q17 / Q22; a partitioned parallel hash join in Mojo for build sides that are not direct-addressable and for the leaf-by-leaf fallback
- [x] F-046a slice 3 join + aggregate fusion: `Aggregate([Filter|Project]* inner-join tree)` plans that took a star scan (`mx_filter` writing every joined row) plus `mx_execute` on the joined table run as ONE `mx_execute` v3 call (`join_engine.fused_join_aggregate` -> `native_exec.try_execute_join`): per 1024-row block the fact leaf's own filter, the partner lookups (existing `_build_map` maps), the aggregate program's predicate over the survivors (cross-partner residuals such as Q5's c_nationkey = s_nationkey, filters above the join) with the partner columns gathered into block scratch, then the aggregate; no joined row is written. Group keys: fact columns as before; partner int columns, partner string columns (ranked in byte order by a dictionary-sized table, turned back to strings) and derived expressions are expression keys (derived ranges from one extra fused min / max pass). Declines to the old path with the same answer: hashed / compacted key ranges (> 2^20 groups), duplicate partner keys, int64 past 2^53 (fact predicate, fact or partner column), unsupported expressions; `MXFRAME_FUSED_JOIN_AGG=0` turns it off. Group keys past direct addressing (`K_PROW`): when every key is a column of ONE partner (or the fact column equal to its join key) AND the keys include the partner's unique join key, the group id is the partner's ROW (a synthetic partner column the front stage gathers) and the key columns are taken from the partner at those rows (Q3's shape); HAVING, ORDER BY and LIMIT run on that small result (top-k first narrows by the first sort column, ties included, so only the survivors' keys are materialized). Keys without the partner's unique key (several partner rows per key tuple, Q10's per-customer keys through orders) would need a merge of groups: declined for now (an earlier NumPy merge was removed: it mis-grouped negative key values and cost more than the old path), Q10 runs on the old path; a native merge is a separate ticket. A partner larger than 2^20 rows declines. Integer expression keys over partner columns only (Q8's year) are bounded by interval arithmetic instead of a second pass. Cheap checks (switches, device, library) run before any partner is built; a declined fused call hands its built partners to the old path (no second build); a join result the old path has cached is not asked again; accumulation is shared (atomic) for many groups, so a large key space with few survivors (Q11) does not zero and merge a partial per worker. SF1 vs DuckDB, all equal: Q3, Q5, Q8, Q11, Q12, Q14, Q19 fused, Q10 old path; random suite: all equal. `tests/test_fused_join_agg.py`. Open: the block lookup loop duplicates mx_filter's (unify after slice 2 changes the maps); a partner of more than 2^20 rows (SF10 Q3) still declines
- [ ] F-046a slice 3 timing on a quiet machine: no SF1 / SF10 TPC-H query slower fused than with `MXFRAME_FUSED_JOIN_AGG=0` (last rough A/B at load 12: all within ~1 ms); the random-suite join shape vs DuckDB
- [x] F-046a slice 2 star lookups on the new table (review PASS 2026-10-16) (`_build_map` / `mx_filter`, `join_engine._star_setup`; `tests/test_star_lookups.py`, all vs DuckDB with path asserts): (a) a partner whose join key repeats no longer declines (E_DUP) to the leaf-by-leaf join: its map keeps rows in CSR layout (direct: `ends[c + 1]`, hashed: count + first position per slot; rows of a key in build order, built per partition / key range so there are no atomics and the layout is deterministic), pass 1 counts the one-to-many expansion (an odometer over the repeating partners, the last running fastest; fact order, then partner build order) pass 1 keeps only the unexpanded survivors (fact offset, first partner row / CSR position and row count per lookup) and the expanded row count and string bytes per morsel, pass 2 expands them with the odometer while writing, and a join past 2^28 rows declines (-14) after pass 1 having buffered nothing (the leaf-by-leaf join then answers up to 2^31 rows and raises MemoryError past that; test measures the decline's peak memory); the fused aggregate (`mx_execute` v3) builds with `allow_multi=False` and still declines repeated keys (-18), then the star scan answers it; (b) presence bitmap on hashed maps (key range <= 2^26 and a bitmap no bigger than 24 bytes per key: 8 MB at most), so a miss stops before the table; (c) group prefetching in the probe: `std.sys.prefetch` works on Mojo 1.1.0; a block's keys and slots are computed and prefetched first, then resolved (no prefetch for a map whose first-probed structure is under 256 KB); (d) generalized runtime join filter for single-leaf partners (`_runtime_reduced`): a partner of more than twice the fact's filtered rows is cut to the rows holding the fact's surviving keys before its map is built; skipped when the fact keeps over half its rows, and, after a measurement, unless the partner's map (16 bytes per row) exceeds the last-level cache (`MXFRAME_RUNTIME_JOIN_FILTER=0|1|auto`): with it on, SF1 Q12 15.6 -> 21.0 ms and Q14 7.5 -> 13.7 ms (noisy A/B, load ~7: the second pass over the fact's filter columns costs more than a 24 MB map), so SF1 keeps the old path; (e) one lookup loop, `_lookup_col`, for the star scan and the fused aggregate (the duplicate from slice 3 is gone). SF1 TPC-H all 22 equal DuckDB (stored and shuffled), random suite 100/100 equal (stored and shuffled). Path changes: none of the 22 queries or 100 random queries changed path (no repeated partner keys in them, partners under L3); only the maps differ (bitmaps on the hashed Q7 / Q10 maps). New diagnostics: `native_exec.last_filter_maps()` (kind / multi / bitmap per partner) in the join trace. Behaviour note: an inner join whose partner keys repeat now runs as a star scan, so its rows come in the largest leaf's order (the old path kept the left table's order); semantic order of an inner join was never promised
- [ ] F-046a slice 2 timing on a quiet machine (load < 2 for 5 min, ps samples logged): SF10 stored + shuffled Q3, Q5, Q8, Q12, Q14 vs the parent commit (the runtime filter's SF10 effect on Q12's 240 MB orders map is predicted, not measured: confirm it wins there, tune `_MAP_BYTES_PER_ROW` / the L3 gate), a one-to-many star (partner with repeated keys, 1-8 rows per key) vs the leaf-by-leaf join, and the hashed-map bitmap and prefetch on Q3's orders partner (A/B with `MXFRAME_RUNTIME_JOIN_FILTER` and a build without prefetch)
- [x] F-046g SELECT DISTINCT on dictionary columns runs on unified codes, then decodes the distinct rows: random SF10 q30 375 -> 43 ms (DuckDB 27), SF1 q94 117 -> 11.5 ms (DuckDB 13)
- [ ] F-046c zone maps: per-chunk min / max to skip chunks a filter cannot match (deferred: TPC-H's and the random suite's filter columns aren't clustered, so the benchmarks couldn't show it; worth it for time-ordered data)
- [x] F-046d dictionary strings pass through joins as codes when the plan reads them above the join only in dictionary-native ways (bare group key, native predicates): the native join scan writes int32 codes of one unified dictionary (per-chunk remaps), the group-by uses them as they are; columns that reach the output are decoded as before. Random SF10 joins 387 -> 217 ms and 1597 -> 639 ms. `tests/test_dict_through_join.py`
- [x] F-046f single-table filters: for `[Sort/Limit/Distinct]* [Project] Filter+ Scan` plans, dictionary columns are no longer decoded for the whole table first; the native filter decodes only the rows that pass. Open: DISTINCT under an aggregate (q68's count-distinct shape) still decodes first
- [x] F-046b COUNT(DISTINCT) on unsorted keys: a radix-partitioned hash aggregate in `mx_count_distinct` (partitions of about 64k consecutive keys, pairs deduped and counted per key in cache, no atomics, output in key order; key ranges past max(2^26, 8 x rows) still fall back). Shuffled SF10 Q21 4.98 s -> 1.47 s (DuckDB 2.88). Costs 16 bytes of scratch per row (24 with string values); a key range holding most rows is one partition, so one worker
- [x] F-046e top-k fused with its filter: `mx_filter_topk` runs the predicate per block and offers passing rows to per-worker heaps (no filtered table); random SF10 WHERE + ORDER BY + LIMIT 420 -> 63 ms (DuckDB 50), 196 -> 63, 123 -> 76. Open: heaps hold int and float copies of every sort value (nt x k x S x 16 bytes)
- [x] numbers after F-046b/d/e/f/g (`docs/bench/m2q-engine*.md`, `random-*-m2q.md`, commit 1313971; median load 4-6): TPC-H SF10 MXFrame 3.20 s, DuckDB 5.11, Polars 14.24 (without Q21: 2.45 vs 2.30); shuffled SF10 MXFrame 4.99 s (was 8.64), DuckDB 5.52, Polars 15.24; vs DuckDB on random queries (geometric mean): SF1 0.63 -> 0.76x, SF1 shuffled 0.91 -> 1.02x, SF10 0.75 -> 0.96x; joins at SF10 0.64 -> 0.98x, top-N 0.41 -> 0.93x; still behind: COUNT(DISTINCT) 0.35-0.48x, SELECT DISTINCT 0.53-0.82x; all 250 MXFrame results equal DuckDB
- [x] F-046h COUNT(DISTINCT b) per a without decoding or one-worker stalls: on device="cpu" both columns stay dictionaries (single table, native filter, join output) and the native count reads int32 codes of one unified dictionary (a dictionary key is decoded and value-sorted after); a small (key range) x (value range) is counted in a byte map; fewer key partitions (or sorted runs) than 2 x workers are partitioned by the pair's hash instead. Random suite vs DuckDB, COUNT(DISTINCT) shape (geo. mean): SF1 0.43 -> 0.86x, SF1 shuffled 0.48 -> 1.02x, SF10 0.35 -> 0.94x; all queries SF1 0.76 -> 0.81x, SF1 shuffled 1.02 -> 1.19x, SF10 0.96 -> 1.02x (`docs/bench/random-*-m2r.md`, build before the review's small fixes; median load 3-5); all 250 MXFrame results equal DuckDB. `tests/test_count_distinct_codes.py`. device="auto" counts on codes too since F-058 (it decoded the counted column before)
- [ ] remaining (next release): join + aggregate fusion (no joined rows written), partitioned hash join for TPC-H Q3 / Q5 / Q8 / Q11 / Q12 / Q17 / Q22 (0.44-0.69x DuckDB), SELECT DISTINCT (0.57-0.99x)
- [x] release numbers on one build, quiet machine (`docs/bench/m2s-engine*.md`, `random-*-m2s.md`; shuffled + random 2026-10-14 01:21-01:50 after load < 2 for 5 min; stored rows rerun 02:05 after load < 1 for 5 min, the first pass overlapped a snapper cleanup and was discarded): TPC-H SF10 MXFrame 3.15 s, DuckDB 5.08, Polars 14.40 (without Q21: 2.41 vs 2.27); shuffled SF10 4.97 / 5.55 / 15.06 (without Q21: 3.63 vs 2.79); SF1 0.47 / 0.63 / 1.31, shuffled 0.61 / 0.66 / 1.41; random vs DuckDB (geo. mean) SF1 0.83x, SF1 shuffled 1.21x, SF10 1.09x; all MXFrame results equal DuckDB. ## F-053 device="auto" never uses the native executor [review]
Found 2026-10-14 while documenting auto (F-052); the review showed it holds on every machine. On every CPU decision `auto` sets a co-processing policy (`lazy_frame.py` ~299, whatever the GPU state; `MXFRAME_COPROC=0` doesn't help) and `native_exec.try_execute` declines ("CPU+GPU co-processing policy active"), so the default `.compute()` runs the older fused pipeline; every 0.6.0 number is `device="cpu"`. SF10 group-by probe: cpu `path=native`, auto `path=fused_pipeline_cpu`. auto's 22/22 and 21/22 results are from 2026-10-12, before the native executor; the cost model prices the CPU with the older kernels.
- [ ] re-run the user-felt TPC-H scoreboard with `mx-auto` next to `mx-cpu` on 0.6.0 (quiet machine)
- [x] auto sets the co-processing policy only when `auto_mode._cpu_path` predicts a CPU+GPU split beats the native CPU executor by 10% (or the caller passes `gpu_fraction`); every other CPU decision runs the native executor exactly as `device="cpu"`; `MXFRAME_COPROC=0` honoured. Split is priced on source rows (what the pipeline and upload read) and only predicted for shapes the split can take (no join below the aggregate, bare group keys; `coproc_fused.stable_group_keys` is shared with `pipeline_exec`). Decision: the native executor does NOT take the CPU share of a split (big change in native_exec, which another ticket is editing); a split stays on the older pipeline and is chosen only when it beats `pipeline@cpu * NATIVE_CPU_FACTOR`. Factor is 1.0: one warm Q1-shaped 6M-row measurement on a busy machine (load 12) gave native 7.7 ms vs fused 6.9 ms, so no native speed-up is assumed. Refresh it, and add a real native calibration probe, in the quiet-machine run. `explain(device="auto", costs=True)` prints `cpu path: ...` and why. Tests: `tests/test_f053_auto_native.py`
- [ ] README / site drop the "pass device=\"cpu\" for now" note once auto is within 10% of min(cpu, gpu) again
  - note: the README, site, auto-mode.md and CHANGELOG were already changed to describe the current behaviour honestly (auto runs the native executor on a CPU decision; no speed claim), pending the quiet-machine check above
- [ ] TODO quiet machine: `auto` within 10% of min(cpu, gpu) on >= 20/22 queries at SF1 and SF10 (median of fresh-process runs)

## F-077 A NULL in a float group key: -0.0 and 0.0 are one group, NaNs one group [done]
Needs: — · Found in F-069 review (2026-10-16); a wrong answer on main before F-069
- [x] (fallback canonicalises, via `np_arrow.canonical_floats` in `np_arrow._codes` and `arrow_dict.encode_key`; a group reports its first row's value, as DuckDB does) a float group key with a NULL declines the native path (correct) and the Arrow fallback kept -0.0 and 0.0 (and NaN payloads) as separate groups: 12 groups where DuckDB gives 10 (reproduced through a fused-join partner column). Either the native hashed path takes NULL keys (a NULL group, last in key order like DuckDB) or the fallback canonicalises -0.0 → 0.0 and NaN → one NaN before grouping
- [ ] follow-up (kernel, after F-070 rebuilds the Mojo kernels; not touched here): the native hashed group-by (F-069, no NULL, float64 key) reports +0.0 for a group whose first row is -0.0 and the canonical NaN for a group whose first row is another NaN payload, where DuckDB (and the fused pipeline and the Arrow fallback since F-077) report the first row's bits. The key can't be repaired in `native_exec._wrap_result`: the executor doesn't return each group's first row. Keep the original key bits in the hash records (or the group's first row) and emit those. Pinned by two `xfail(strict=True)` cases in `tests/test_float_key_groups.py::test_group_reports_its_first_rows_bits_without_null[native-*]`; remove the marker when fixed

## F-078 COUNT(*) on a NULL group key counts the group's rows [done]
- Finding (2026-10-16): already fixed on main by F-042 (`COUNT(*)` -> `lit(1).count()`); the F-077 developer saw it on a worktree based on the stale `origin/main`. Found by the F-077 developer (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer on main
- [x] root cause: the SQL front end translated `COUNT(*)` to `col(<first in-scope column>).count()`, which skips NULLs (a global count(*) also lost rows with a NULL first column); it is now `lit(1).count()`, the form every path already treats as count(*); no kernel change. SQL `SELECT k, count(*) ... GROUP BY k` with NULLs in `k` returns 0 for the NULL-key group where DuckDB returns its row count (2 in the probe). Find which path (the SQL front end's `count(*)` → a column count, or the NULL-key fallback) and fix at the root; `count(*)` counts rows on every path and device

Found by the F-077 developer (2026-10-16); untested suspicion
- [x] joins on float keys encode with bit-for-bit `dictionary_encode` (`join_engine`, `custom_ops`): -0.0 against 0.0 and NaN payloads probably mismatch DuckDB (DuckDB: -0.0 = 0.0, NaN = NaN in joins). Probe first; if wrong, canonicalise with `np_arrow.canonical_floats` (F-077) at the join-key boundary, every join kind (inner, left, semi, anti), native and GPU

## F-080 SQL TPC-H fully native: Q5, Q7, Q9, Q13, Q22 [done]
Needs: F-076 for Q10 · From `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1 --sql` on main (2026-10-16, after F-072): 15 of 22 SQL queries have no decline and no Arrow work over 1,000 rows; Q8 ("nothing to filter") and Q15 ("empty input") only show trivial declines and do no Arrow work. The DataFrame forms are 22/22, so each gap is in how the SQL front end phrases the plan
- [x] Q5: `executor declined (code -18)` in the fused join (also on main before F-069)
- [x] Q7, Q9: one `expression not supported by the bytecode compiler` each (find the expression; compile it or rewrite it in the SQL front end to the DataFrame form)
- [x] Q22: three `greater` / `and_kleene` passes over 150,000 rows in Arrow, `expression` / `predicate not supported` (the DataFrame Q22 is native since F-034)
- [x] Q13 (added): `take[1.5M]` x2 + `dictionary_encode` of the LEFT JOIN, then Arrow's null-skipping group-by
- [x] `tests/test_native_strings_tpch.py`-style test over the SQL text of all 22 queries on SF 0.17: no Arrow compute over 1,000 rows, allowed declines listed by name; fails before: `tests/test_sql_tpch_native.py` (22 cases; Q21 is `xfail` while its correlated `<>` raises, F-112; 6 of the other 21 fail on main: 7, 9, 10, 13, 15, 16; 5 and 22 were fixed in the first commit, so measure them on main)
- Built (2026-10-16). **SF1 SQL coverage 16/22 -> 21/22** (Q21 raises on main: `EXISTS: correlated predicate l2.l_suppkey <> l1.l_suppkey cannot be decorrelated`, F-112's branch; not touched). Root causes, one commit each:
  - Q5 (code -18 = E_DUP): the comma join took `l_suppkey = s_suppkey AND c_nationkey = s_nationkey` as one two-column key whose left columns come from two leaves (lineitem, customer); `_flatten` then joined the sides as units and the fused join picked supplier as the fact, with a duplicate build key. `sql_frontend._build_from` now joins on the keys of the first table and leaves the other equalities in WHERE (the DataFrame form: join on suppkey, then `filter(c_nationkey == s_nationkey)`)
  - Q22: `substring(c_phone, 1, 2) IN (...)` inside the WHERE filter has no bytecode form (the DataFrame form computes it with `with_columns` first). `_hoist_slices` computes a `str_slice` of a column below the Filter as a column (reused by the SELECT list item of the same expression), so the filter reads a plain string column
  - Q7: the fused join declined `min(col n_name)` because the group keys came as `col n_name AS supp_nation` (the aliased Project inlined into the Aggregate; the DataFrame form renames in a leaf). `try_execute_join` takes an aliased column key like a plain one and names the result column by the alias
  - Q9: `ps_partkey = l_partkey` after `p_partkey = l_partkey` kept part's column as the left key, so the composite key `(l_suppkey, p_partkey)` spanned two leaves (E_DUP again). `join_engine._one_leaf` takes the key from one leaf when the joins below equate the columns (union-find over the edges)
  - Q10: see F-076
  - Q13: `COUNT(o_orderkey)` over `customer LEFT JOIN orders` materialized 1.5M joined rows with NULLs and ran Arrow's group-by. `join_engine.left_join_counts` (hooked in `compile_and_run` before the join is materialized): the native left hash join gives the pairs, a `bincount` gives each left row its matches (0 where none), and when the left key is unique (checked) the groups are the left rows; nullable counted columns, duplicate keys, GPU and other aggregates decline to the old path. The Q13 outer aggregate and sort then run in the native executor
  - Q16 (only at SF 0.17 / small data, where `s_comment` is a dictionary): `x NOT IN (SELECT col ...)` aliased the subquery column to `__in`, which made the subquery a renaming Project over Filter*(Scan), not a join leaf, so its dictionary column was decoded for all rows. A bare column is now its own key
  - `scripts/tpch_user/provenance.py` no longer records `LazyFrame.schema`'s zero-row dry run (the SQL front end runs one per derived table, and it showed as a `cpu_aot` compute that did no query)
  - No `.mojo` file touched
- Not done: Q21 SQL (F-112); no timings (shared machine)
- [ ] no DataFrame query slower; equal to DuckDB (timing on a quiet machine: not measured here). Equal to DuckDB: `scripts/tpch_sql/run.py --sf 1` for Q5, 7, 9, 10, 13, 22 `equal`; `-k "sql or tpch"` and the full suite in `/tmp` logs named in the report
