## F-082 Denormal floats are not flushed to zero [done]
Found by the F-070 developer (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer
- [x] cause: Mojo's `parallelize` / `sync_parallelize` run every task with FTZ + DAZ set (MXCSR 0x9FE0, read with `stmxcsr` inside a task; main thread 0x1FA0). Not a compile flag, not a float32 conversion, not fast-math: any kernel that fanned out over threads read 5e-324 (and float32 1e-45) as 0. `fused_pipeline_cpu` fanned out from ~200,000 rows; the native executor, hash aggregate, group keys and joins from larger inputs (native: 20M rows `x > 0.0` counted 0 of 10M). The GPU kernels (float64 and float32), Arrow and the single-thread paths were right
- [x] fix at the root: `kernels_aot/_fpenv.mojo` (`par`, `par_n`, `sync_par`) resets MXCSR to 0x1F80 at the start of every task; every `parallelize` / `sync_parallelize` in executor_cpu, pipeline_cpu, hashagg_cpu, groupkeys_cpu, joinsemi_cpu and kernels_aot now goes through it. Rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh` (cpu, keys, join, aot) and hashes recorded. Rule: never call `parallelize` directly in kernels_aot
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_f082_denormals.py`): 5e-324, 2.2e-308 / 2, -5e-324, float32 1e-45, 1.1e-38, -1e-45 mixed with zeros and normals over 200,000 rows; filters (count, sum, grouped, 5000 groups), sums, min / max, row filter, float64 and float32, with and without NULLs, cpu native on / off and gpu; 68 fail on main's libraries (all cpu), all pass now
- [ ] quiet-machine speed check (not run: shared machine): the wrapper adds one `ldmxcsr` per task (and per chunk for `par_n`). Compare SF1 / SF10 Q1, Q6, Q18 and the group-key / join micro-benchmarks before and after F-082 in fresh processes (median of several, load < 2); expect no change
- [ ] follow-up (found while testing): the SQL front end fails on a scientific literal without a dot (`x > 1e-310` raises `ValueError: invalid literal for int()`; `sql_frontend.py` `lit(int(v)) if "." not in str(v)`), and a float32 column compared with a float64 literal casts the literal to float32 (`x between -1e-30 and 1e-30` on float32 keeps the row holding float32(1e-30), DuckDB compares as double and drops it)

## F-084 Window functions: NULL values, NaN ties and DESC order like DuckDB [done]
Found by the F-081 review (2026-10-16); silent wrong answers on main before F-081
- [x] window min / max turn values to float64 first, so a NULL value becomes NaN: `max` over `[NULL, 2, 5]` gives NaN (DuckDB 5), an all-NULL partition gives NaN (DuckDB NULL); min skips NULL only through the NaN rule. Aggregate over the valid values, NULL when none
- [x] `_window_rank` ties rows with `==`, so tied NaNs rank apart: rank of `[nan, 1, nan, 2]` is `[3, 1, 4, 2]`, DuckDB `[3, 1, 3, 2]` (rank and dense_rank); tie through the canonical codes (`np_arrow.canonical_floats`)
- [x] ORDER BY ... DESC inside a window puts NaN last (Arrow `sort_indices` puts NaN at the end both ways); DuckDB treats NaN as the largest value, first under DESC (row_number, rank, running sums differ)
- [x] window results keep integer dtype where DuckDB does (int max returns 4.0 where DuckDB returns 4); decided: stay float64, documented in docs/api.md (DuckDB gives int64 rank, int min/max, decimal sum)
- [x] window sum, mean and cum_sum turned a NULL value into NaN (silent wrong answer, added at review): skip NULLs, NULL when none valid, running sum NULL until the first valid value, NaN among valid values propagates; `count` is not a window op here
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests for each; fail before

Found by the F-079 developer and the F-083 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] `WHERE x IS NULL` / `IS NOT NULL` (and in SELECT, CASE, HAVING, subqueries, joins' ON) raises `NotImplementedError: Unsupported SQL expression type: Is` in the SQL front end; map to the existing `is_null` / `is_not_null` expressions (native filter where the bytecode has a validity test; Arrow otherwise). Now a query that reads `u.k` (or a bare right-only key) makes the right side carry a copy of it (`__rk_<alias>_<key>`, a name no table has), NULL where unmatched; column resolution maps `u.k` to it, so `IS NULL`, `IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM NULL` and `SELECT u.k` agree with DuckDB. `USING (k)`: a bare `k` stays the left column. `SELECT *` hides the copy. `Expr.signature(alias=True)` puts the output name in plan cache keys (join result cache returned the previous join's column names). `IS NULL` conjuncts on the right table in `JOIN ... ON` filter the right side first; constants-only tests (`1 IS DISTINCT FROM 2`) raise a clear error.

## F-087 SQL OFFSET is ignored [done]
Found by the F-083 review (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer on main
- [x] `ORDER BY c, i LIMIT 3 OFFSET 2` returned the first 3 rows (OFFSET dropped). `Limit` has an `offset` (default 0; `LazyFrame.limit(n, offset=0)`); OFFSET without LIMIT keeps every later row. `CustomOpsCompiler.compile_and_run` runs a top-level OFFSET window as `Limit(offset + n)` (so Sort + Limit still reach the top-n heaps, the native filter top-k and the aggregate top-k with k = offset + n) and cuts the first `offset` rows off the result; an OFFSET window under another Sort / Limit or under a Filter / Project / Aggregate is answered by the generic `slice(offset, n)` (`compiler.py`); only Sort / Limit / Distinct / Tail chains are resolved first by `_resolve_inner_offsets`. Correlated subqueries still reject OFFSET
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_sql_offset.py`): OFFSET with and without LIMIT, ORDER BY ties broken by a unique key, hidden sort key, group-by, DISTINCT, join, FROM subqueries, CTEs, IN / NOT IN subqueries (also the hidden-key form), OFFSET past the end, LIMIT 0, no ORDER BY, DataFrame `limit(n, offset)`, a gpu case; top-n structure (sort `topk` and heap k = limit + offset); 53 of 62 failed before

## F-094 SQL modulo operator [done]
Found by the F-087 developer (2026-10-16)
- [x] `i % 2` is unsupported in the SQL front end; map `%` (and `MOD(a, b)`) to the engine's modulo with DuckDB semantics (sign follows the dividend, NULL on zero divisor? probe DuckDB: it errors / returns NULL), native filter where the bytecode has it
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: int / float, negative operands, zero divisor, in WHERE / SELECT / GROUP BY; fails before
Done: DuckDB probed (sign follows the dividend; integer `% 0` is NULL, float `% 0.0` is NaN; NULL in, NULL out; NaN stays NaN; `x % inf` is x). New `mod` Expr op (`np_arrow.modulo`, fmod) on the Arrow path: the arrays decide integer or float arithmetic (integers keep their type, uint64 and signed/unsigned mixes are exact, a decimal literal such as `% 2.5` is exact against integers and its zero divisor is NULL); the native bytecode has no modulo and declines it (`Unsupported`), the GPU runs it through the Arrow Project. `tests/test_sql_modulo.py` (162 DuckDB-checked, cpu + gpu, result kinds compared). Left alone: COALESCE / IS NULL over expressions is F-100. Found on the way, not fixed: `int_col + 1` (an integer literal in + - *) returns double where DuckDB returns an integer (`_float_expr` promotes the literal), so `(a + 1) % 0` is NaN, not NULL, and past 2^53 it rounds; needs its own ticket. A decimal literal (a point, no exponent; `1e3` is DOUBLE) is exact against integers as ((x % k) * s % k) / s in NumPy (about 20 ms per 1M rows on the loaded machine); it raises past k * s > int64 (a literal with ~19 digits). Still a Python loop: modulo of uint64 mixed with signed values (exact, slow on millions of rows).

## F-095 SQL over tables with uppercase or quoted column names [done]
Found by the F-087 developer (2026-10-16); untested suspicion, important for real files (DataLens)
- [x] the SQL front end lowercased identifiers; a table with `CustomerID` or `"Order Date"` columns failed to resolve (`KeyError 'Field "customerid"'`). One step now maps every written column to the real one on every SQL path (SELECT, `*`, `t.*`, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, ORDER BY incl. hidden sort keys, JOIN ON / USING, right-key copies, own-typed keys, subqueries, CTEs, IN / EXISTS, comma joins, `scan_parquet` / `read_parquet` frames)
- [x] `SELECT *, i+1 AS x FROM u` with a column `Ab`, and `u.*` over a join, keep the source spelling
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_sql_case_names.py`, 117; 113 fail on main) over names with uppercase, spaces, a mixed-case quoted name, a case-only clash and Parquet files; column names and values compared
- Probed DuckDB (1.5.1), native and registered Arrow tables alike: the ticket's premise was half wrong. **Quoted identifiers are case-insensitive too** (`"customerid"` finds `CustomerID`); the result keeps the SOURCE spelling for a plain column and `SELECT *`, the written spelling for an alias, and an unaliased aggregate is named from the text as written (`sum(AMOUNT)`, `sum("Order Date")`). Two columns that differ only by case (Arrow tables only; a native table refuses to be created that way): either spelling finds the first one, and a repeated output name is renamed `A_1` case-insensitively (`SELECT *`, `SELECT a, "A"`)
- Built: `_SQLTranslator._resolve_names` (the single step: runs at the start of every `_build_select`, rewrites each `sge.Column` to the first case-insensitive match in its FROM scope, then SELECT aliases, then outer scopes for subqueries; the written text stays in the node's meta for `_default_agg_name`). Everything after it works on exact names: the `.lower()` calls on column names are gone (table names and aliases stay case-insensitive), `_dedupe` is case-insensitive, aliases keep their spelling (they were lowercased), USING takes each side's own spelling, a lone `*` renames a case-only repeat to `A_1`. `_source` is cached per translator so the extra scope lookup builds a FROM item once
- Not done, found on the way: window functions and `FILTER (WHERE ...)` are unsupported in the SQL front end (clear NotImplementedError, no ticket yet); a non-key column that two joined tables spell differently by case only (`Region` / `region`) is not renamed `region_1` as DuckDB does; `SELECT * ... ORDER BY 1` (a position over a lone star) raises "needs a named SELECT column" (not case related)

## F-096 SQL JOIN ON an expression [done]
Found by the F-087 developer (2026-10-16)
- [x] `JOIN u ON t.a + 1 = u.b` (and non-equi residual predicates `ON t.k = u.k AND t.x > u.y`) are unsupported; equi-join on computed keys (compute the key column first) and a residual filter after the equi-join, like DuckDB
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests, inner and left (a residual on a left join filters matches, not rows); fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `_split_join_on` sorts the AND-ed conditions of an ON by the tables they read (qualifier / name, `_on_side`, F-109): a key is `a = b` with each side wholly on one table (any expression); the rest are left-only, right-only or both-table residuals. `_join_keys`: a computed key is projected into a column on its side first (`__jk`, hidden from results through `_copies`); a right-only test filters the right side; a left-only test filters an INNER join's left side and in a LEFT join becomes one more key (`when(cond, 1, 0)` against a constant 1, so a left row failing it matches nothing); no equality = constant key (cross product), refused unless `_rows_upper` of both inputs is known and their product is at most `_CROSS_LIMIT` (4,000,000). Both-table residual: INNER = a Filter after the join; LEFT = `_left_residual`: row id on the left (`row_number`), the join, `when(res, 1, 0)`, per-id `max` and `row_number` windows, keep the satisfying rows plus one row of each id with none, right columns blanked with `when(ok, c)` (NULL). The left input is evaluated once, so the id cannot drift. Comma joins and `CROSS JOIN` with no linking equality are the same cross product filtered by WHERE. `_parse_join_on` and `_use_right_key_copies` are gone (`_expr` already resolves the renamed / copied names). TPC-H Q13 (`LEFT JOIN orders ON ... AND o_comment NOT LIKE ...`) is now equal to DuckDB and removed from `UNSUPPORTED` in `tests/test_sql_tpch.py`. `tests/test_f096_join_on_expr.py` (449, cpu + gpu): 440 of this ticket's and Q13's tests fail on main. Three old tests that asserted the 'cross product is not supported' errors now check DuckDB (`test_sql_tpch_forms.py`, `test_semi_anti_join.py`).
- Not done: a subquery or a residual in a SEMI / ANTI JOIN's ON; `ON` conditions with no column (`1 = 1`) raise; `IS NOT DISTINCT FROM` between the tables is a residual, not a key (a cross product when it is the only link); `lower()` and other string functions do not exist in the SQL front end, so a computed key is arithmetic, `%`, `substring`, `CASE`, `EXTRACT(YEAR)`; the LEFT residual with windows is on the Arrow path (no native / GPU kernel), and a LEFT join with a both-table residual materializes the full key join before filtering (a many-to-many key can blow up, as in any join-then-filter).
- Review round 1 (2026-10-16): `Expr.signature` made a window spec `(list, list, bool)` unhashable, so auto mode's `_shape_failed` crashed with `TypeError: unhashable type: 'list'` on any big window (also on main for DataFrame windows; the LEFT residual is the first SQL plan with one): lists and tuples are now hashed recursively. `_rows_upper` of a LEFT join is `l * max(r, 1)` (an empty right side still keeps every left row; the cap had let 9M pairs through). The cross-join cap compares UPPER BOUNDS (`_rows_upper`: table rows, Parquet footer via `io_parquet._metadata`, filters not counted), not actual row counts, and the message says "up to". Duplicate `_on_side` deleted. `tests/test_f096_auto_window_signature.py` (5; all fail before): hashable signature, DataFrame window and SQL LEFT residual under `device="auto"` with the small-query threshold at 0, and a 300,000-row Parquet LEFT residual / window at the default threshold.

## F-097 IS NULL skips row groups with no NULLs [done]
From the F-086 review (2026-10-16); a speedup, not a bug
- [x] `scan_parquet` pushes `is_null` / `not is_null` (`io_parquet._pushable`, ops `isnull` / `notnull`): a row group whose `null_count` is 0 is skipped for IS NULL, one whose `null_count` equals its rows for IS NOT NULL; a missing `null_count` statistic never skips. Composes with the other ANDed pushed conjuncts; an OR is not pushed at all (as before for every conjunct kind)
- [x] native validity test in the bytecode: `OP_ISNULL` (41) reads the column's Arrow validity bitmap (chunk offsets and slices through the bit base, a dictionary column through its indices); the column's values are never read, so it may hold NULLs, of any integer / float / bool / temporal / decimal / utf8 / dictionary-utf8 type. `_Extras` gives such a column dtype 7 and two words (bitmap address, bit base). Native executor only (`concat_dicts`): the fused pipeline and GPU compilers still decline it. A column both tested and read for its values declines (as before); an aggregate whose only use of a nullable column is `IS NULL` is no longer sent to Arrow's null path before the executor (`_arrow_aggregate_reason`, early call only: the late call still catches it when every pipeline declined)
- [x] tests (`tests/test_f097_is_null.py`): row groups read counted from the resolved scan, missing statistics not skipped, native path asserted (`native_filter`, `native`), answers equal DuckDB with NULLs on one-chunk, multi-chunk (odd cuts) and sliced (bit offset 3) tables, cpu and gpu; the pruning and native tests fail on main
- [ ] quiet-machine speed check (not run: shared machine): `scan_parquet` of a 50M-row file in 50k-row groups with NULLs in 2 groups, `WHERE x IS NULL`: bytes read and wall time vs main (expect ~ groups with nulls / all groups); and `SELECT g, count(*) FROM t WHERE x IS NULL GROUP BY g` on a 10M-row table with 10% NULLs in memory: native vs the Arrow path on main (`MXFRAME_NATIVE=0` approximates it), median of 5 fresh-process runs, load < 2
- Not done, for a follow-up: comparisons on nullable columns stay on the Arrow path. A comparison with a NULL operand is NULL and keeps no row, so under AND / OR it can be 0.0 (a NULL-masked compare: `cmp & valid(a) & valid(b)`, exact because filters drop NULL like false); NOT of it is the hard part (NOT NULL is NULL, not true), so it needs a three-valued AND / OR / NOT or a "known false" mask, or a decline while a NOT is above a nullable compare (what `OP_STR` does with `_neg`). Also the native filter still declines when an OUTPUT column has NULLs (no validity gather), so `SELECT * ... WHERE x IS NULL` over a nullable table is Arrow; only filters that output null-free columns, and aggregates, stay native

## F-098 OFFSET without LIMIT: no sentinel row count [done]
From the F-087 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] OFFSET without LIMIT uses `n = 2**62` (`sql_frontend._ALL_ROWS`): `explain()` shows the sentinel and an OFFSET above ~2^62 overflows `n + offset` in int64 in native code. Model "no limit" as `n=None` in `Limit` (or clamp), every consumer handles it
- [x] tests: explain shows no sentinel; a huge OFFSET returns no rows without overflow; fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `Limit.n` is `Optional[int]`, None = no limit (`LazyFrame.limit(None, offset)` too); the SQL front end writes it and clamps a LIMIT / OFFSET to 2^62 (no table has more rows; `n + offset` stays an int64). Consumers: `compile_and_run` drops the Limit and only skips the rows (the skip is clamped to the result's length; `n + offset` is clamped), the top-n/post-op paths (`_apply_post_ops_custom` takes no top-k for None, `_filter_topk` declines, `native_exec._split_post_ops` and the top-k pre-cut), `GraphCompiler._apply_post_ops`, the optimizer's Limit-of-Limit merge, auto_mode's row estimate, `plan_validation`, `explain` (`Limit(offset=7)`, no `n=`), and the streamed sinks (`streamed._sink_for`: a Limit with no n collects, as any plan with no top-k). `tests/test_f098_offset_no_limit.py` (133, cpu + gpu, native on and off, plus streamed over a file bigger than a 6M budget): 11 fail on main (offsets from 2^62 up, explain, `n is None`).
- Found: DuckDB 1.5.1 returns NO rows for `SELECT .. ORDER BY .. OFFSET 0` (no LIMIT) over a native table (STREAMING_LIMIT of 0; over a registered Arrow table, as `tests/test_sql_offset.py` uses, it is right). That case is checked against the SQL meaning (every row), not against DuckDB. DuckDB also refuses an OFFSET past int64; here it returns no rows.
- Review fix (2026-10-16): `sql_frontend._rows_upper` returns `max(inner - offset, 0)` for a Limit with no n (the F-096 cross-join cap crashed on `None + offset`); 5 join queries added on cpu / gpu / auto (148 total). Every `.n` read in the package re-grepped: the rest are Tail or guarded.

## F-099 SQL self-join: columns of the second copy [done]
Found by the F-092 developer and review (2026-10-16)
- [x] in `t x JOIN t y`, referencing a column that exists in both copies on the second side (`WHERE y.qty > 25`, `SELECT y.v`) raises "name exists in more than one joined table ... not supported yet". Joined tables with shared non-key column names are common (self-joins, fact-to-fact): carry both copies under distinct internal names (`v`, `v_right`) and resolve `x.v` / `y.v` to the right one in every clause
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: self-join with filters on either side, SELECT of both copies, GROUP BY / ORDER BY on the second copy, a three-way join with a repeated name; fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `_expr` looks a column up by its table alias in `_renamed` (the join renamed a clashing column `v` -> `v_right`, `v_right2`), the key a table matched on with a different name resolves to the left key (`z.v` in `ON z.v = y.v`). The select-list output keeps the written name (`v`, then `v_1`). `tests/test_sql_self_join.py` (81, cpu + gpu, F-092 self-join over `scan_parquet`): 73 fail on main. The two 'more than one joined table' cases of `test_sql_gaps` are removed (they work now); the guard stays for any shape not covered.
- Not done: a bare `v` (no table) in a self-join stays the left table's (DuckDB calls it ambiguous).

## F-100 SQL COALESCE and IS NULL over expressions [done]
Found by the F-094 developer (2026-10-16)
- [x] `COALESCE(expr, ...)` (and `IFNULL` / `NULLIF` if sqlglot parses them) and `expr IS NULL` over any expression (not only a column) are unsupported in the SQL front end. Map them onto the engine's null handling (`is_null` from F-086, a coalesce / fill_null expression), Arrow path; native where the bytecode can
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: COALESCE over columns / literals / expressions with NULLs in SELECT, WHERE, GROUP BY; NULLIF with zero divisors; `(a + b) IS NULL`; fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `compiler.coalesce_arrow` (new op `coalesce`, `lazy_expr.coalesce(...)`), `_supertype` is now the one place for DuckDB's common type of two branches and `case_when_arrow` calls it too (equal types stay, integers int64, float32 next to an integer or a DECIMAL literal stays float32 and only a DOUBLE widens it, other number mixes float64, text one family, a NULL argument takes the other's type); IFNULL / NVL are COALESCE in sqlglot; `NULLIF(a, b)` is `CASE WHEN a = b THEN NULL ELSE a END` (type of `a`, NaN = NaN, `a = b` translated as written so a DATE literal next to a date column works); `ABS` (`abs_arrow`: argument's own type, an integer minimum raises) and `CAST(x AS FLOAT | DOUBLE | TINYINT | SMALLINT | INT | BIGINT)` (`cast_arrow`: a float into an integer rounds half to even, out of range raises, double into float overflow raises). Evaluated on the Arrow path in `_eval_predicate` and `_eval_expr_arrow` and listed with the derived-column ops (`_needs_precompute`), so they also work inside `sum(coalesce(..))`, GROUP BY keys, HAVING, ORDER BY; the native bytecode declines them (unknown op), the GPU runs them through the same Arrow code. `_ftype` / `_is_int_expr` / `_is_date_col` know the new nodes, so `abs(g) > 1e-30`, `coalesce(g, 0) > 1e-30` and `cast(g as float) > 1e-30` on a float32 column widen like DuckDB (the F-110 float32-through-functions item) and `abs(i) + 1` stays an integer. `(a + b) IS NULL` already worked after F-086 (`_is` translates its operand with `_expr`); it is tested over arithmetic, CASE, COALESCE, NULLIF, ABS, CAST, division, comparison, AND, substring and date expressions. `tests/test_f100_sql_coalesce.py` (721 tests incl. cpu + gpu, native on and off, equal to a native DuckDB table, result types compared; 596 fail on main).
- Not done: COALESCE / NULLIF with constants only (`coalesce(null, 5)`) raises NotImplementedError like the other constants-only tests (no column to size the result); `cast` to VARCHAR / DATE / DECIMAL and `try_cast` are unsupported; `int + DECIMAL` branches give double where DuckDB gives an exact DECIMAL (as in F-105); `ORDER BY ... NULLS FIRST` is still unsupported (not this ticket).
- Review round 1 (2026-10-16): `CAST(decimal_expr AS int)` rounded half to even in double (`cast(i*0.5 as int)` for i = 1, 5 gave 0, 2; DuckDB 1, 3): the front end now sees a DECIMAL expression (`_is_decimal_expr`: a DECIMAL literal with integers through + - *) and rounds half away from zero (`cast_arrow(.., away=True)`); a float column or an exponent literal keeps half to even. CAST from a non-number (string, date, dictionary column, or an expression that evaluates to one) raises NotImplementedError (was a type error, and `min(cast(s as int))` returned 3.0). `docs/api.md` documents all of it. Tests added (72).

## F-101 Integer modulo in the native bytecode [done]
From the F-094 review (2026-10-16); speed
- [x] a `%` anywhere in WHERE moved the whole filter off the native path: `WHERE a > 500 GROUP BY k` runs native in 5 ms at 2M rows, adding `AND a % 7 = 0` took it to `cpu_aot` at 50 ms. An integer `mod` op in the bytecode (sign follows the dividend; zero divisor gives NULL, so decline when the divisor column can be 0, or test it), CPU and GPU kernels
- [x] tests: the filter stays native (path asserted), equals DuckDB with negatives and a zero divisor
- [ ] **Quiet-machine timing (not run: the machine is shared, no benchmarks).** Idle machine (load under 2, nothing on the GPU): 2M rows, `WHERE a > 500 GROUP BY k` vs the same with `AND a % 7 = 0`, native, pipeline and GPU, one fresh process each, min and median of 7; accept when the `%` query is within ~20% of the no-`%` one (it was 10x). Float64 `x % y` in Mojo lowers to an fmod call per lane (not a SIMD instruction): if the `%` query is slower than that, replace it by `x - trunc(x / k) * k` guarded by the 2^53 bound (the dividend and the constant are integers below 2^53, so check the one-off rounding of `x / k` first)
- Built (2026-10-17). New op `OP_MOD = 14` (`pipeline_program.py`, `pipeline_cpu.mojo` `_bin`/`_binop`, `pipeline_gpu.mojo` `_run`): the interpreter's registers are float64, an integer dividend is bound within 2^53 (`col_bound`, as for `+ - *`), so a float remainder is exact. **Mojo's float `%` is floored (the sign follows the divisor, Python's), fmod's follows the dividend (DuckDB)**: the kernels step the remainder back by the divisor when it is not 0 and the signs differ (found by the first test run: `-7 % 3` gave 2). `_Builder._mod` takes `int % <nonzero integer constant>` only; a column divisor, a zero divisor (NULL), a float or decimal operand (`Expr("mod", a, b, True)`) raise `Unsupported` and Arrow answers as in F-094: choosing "decline when the divisor can be 0" over producing NULL through the validity path (OP_ISNULL only tests a column's own bitmap and the bytecode has no per-row NULL result, so a NULL divisor result would need a new validity register in every kernel). Result bound `("mod", x, k)` = min(|x|, |k| - 1) so a following `+ - *` stays exact; `native_exec._bounds` gives `a % k` the interval [max(lo, -(|k|-1)), min(hi, |k|-1)] (a negative dividend gives negative keys), which sizes `GROUP BY a % 10` as a direct-addressed key (19 groups); `_int_valued` knows `mod`, so `sum(a % z)` over integers still takes the exact Arrow sum when the compiler declines. Rebuilt `scripts/build_pipeline.sh cpu` and `gpu` (hashes recorded, `record_build_hash.py --check` passes).
- Tests: `tests/test_f101_int_modulo.py` (144; 84 fail on main, the other 60 are the DuckDB-equality of shapes that stay on Arrow, and `auto`): DuckDB-checked aggregates / keys / filters (negative dividends and divisors, int32 / int64 / int16 / uint8 / uint32, `edge` values to +-(2^53 - 1), two derived keys, nested `(a % 7) * 2 + ...`), path asserted (`native` / `fused_pipeline_cpu` / `fused_pipeline_gpu`, `native_filter`), sums of ~2^60 under a `%` filter in every kernel (global, 7, 1000, 500k and 200k groups; native, pipeline, GPU, reproducible), the shapes that must stay on Arrow and still equal DuckDB (`% 0`, a column divisor with zeros and negatives, NULLs in the dividend, INT64 MIN / MAX and values past 2^53, float and decimal divisors), the extremes of int64 through the Arrow path. `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1` and `--sql`: output identical before and after (no TPC-H query uses `%`).
- Found, not fixed (both on main, Arrow path): `select count(a % z) ...` counts the rows whose remainder is NULL (COUNT of an expression ignores NULLs from a zero divisor: 10 for 10 rows, DuckDB 6); `GROUP BY a % 0` (an all-NULL key) returns no rows (`invalid value encountered in cast` in the key encoder) where DuckDB returns one NULL group. Narrow integer columns (int16 / uint8 / uint32) are not the native executor's types: their `%` queries run on the older fused pipeline, still on the bytecode.

## F-102 SELECT * over a join keeps both key columns, each with its own type [done]
Found by the F-090 review (2026-10-16); a missing column on main
- [x] `SELECT * FROM t JOIN v ON t.i = v.x` (and the comma form `FROM t, v WHERE t.i = v.x`) leaves out `x` entirely, even when both keys are the same type; DuckDB keeps both columns. The comma form also reads `v.x` as the left key, so its type is the left side's (values equal, -0.0 shows as 0). Keep the right key as its own output column (USING / NATURAL keep one, like DuckDB)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: SELECT * and SELECT v.x over JOIN ON, comma join, USING; same-type and int-vs-float keys; LEFT JOIN with unmatched rows (right key NULL, F-086); fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): when the query has a `*` or `t.*`, a `JOIN ... ON` (inner or left) carries its right keys as copies (`_copy_right_keys(all_keys)`, the F-086 machinery; no star, no copy, so plans of other queries are unchanged); `_star_items` lists the result columns in DuckDB's order (left table, then the right table's own order, key in place); USING / NATURAL (now parsed) keep one key; a repeated name becomes `k_1`. The comma join copies a right key when the query has a star or the key types differ (leftover of F-090: `fl.x` was the left key's int). `tests/test_sql_select_star_join.py` (63, cpu + gpu): 48 fail on main.
- Probed DuckDB: a native table and a `con.register` view keep a repeated output name at the top level (`k`, `k`); `duckdb.sql(q)` over Python Arrow variables (replacement scan) and a subquery give `k_1`. mx keeps `k_1` (F-088's convention, safer for an Arrow result). `test_sql_is_null::test_left_join_copy_never_in_a_result` expected the missing right key; now both keys. Review round 1: a lone `*` with ORDER BY on a right copy or renamed column raised KeyError (the star rename ran before the sort); it renames after the sort now. The "more than one joined table" guard (`_copy_dropped`, `_same_keys`) is deleted: no shape reached it.

## F-103 SQL integer column plus integer literal stays integer [done]
Found by the F-094 developer (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer past 2^53
- [x] `int_col + 1` (and `-`, `*`) returns double in the SQL front end where DuckDB returns an integer: `_float_expr` promotes the literal. Values past 2^53 round (`i + 1` on 2^60+1 is wrong), `(i32 + 1) % 0` gives NaN instead of NULL, and integer-typed results come back as floats. Keep integer arithmetic integer (checked overflow, F-061) unless an operand is float / decimal / division; DuckDB's result-type rules decide (probe them: int32 + int32 widens?)
- [x] F-094 cases for the tests: `(a - 1) % 10` with `a` past 2^53 raises ArrowInvalid (`a - 1` is computed as a float), and `m % 0` over `(SELECT i32 + 1 AS m ...)` gives NaN instead of NULL
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests with exact ints past 2^53 and type asserts, in SELECT / WHERE / GROUP BY / subqueries / aggregates (`sum(i + 1)`), native and Arrow paths; fails before
- Built: `_arith_operand` / `_is_int_expr` in `sql_frontend.py`. `_float_expr` promoted every literal because the MAX graph (`compiler._visit_expr`) cannot add an int64 constant to a float32 column. Now + - * keeps integers when both operands are certainly integers (integer literal, integer column of a known table, nested + - * / negation of those); anything else (float, aggregate, unresolved name, `/`) promotes exactly as before. A negative literal folds to `lit(-n)` (it was `0 - 1`, a float32 scalar that broke `i + -1`). `tests/test_sql_int_literal_arith.py` (74, native and Arrow, 58 fail on main). Exactness and checked overflow come from the existing F-037 bounds / F-061 kernels: native compiles small ints and declines past 2^53. Result width: Arrow widens `int32 + literal` to int64 where DuckDB keeps INTEGER (integer either way).
- Review round 1: merged main (F-094 `%`); `_is_int_expr` also takes `%` of integers, COUNT and integer MIN / MAX, so a big literal next to them stays exact; negation folds any constant (`-(-1)`, `- -1`). SUM is not treated as integer: the engine still returns it as a double (F-060), so `sum(s) + 9007199254740993` rounds until F-060 lands. Tests for `(i - 1) % 10`, `m % 0` over a subquery, and small-int queries staying native.
- Found, not fixed: global / grouped `max(i)` / `min(i)` on int64 past 2^53 returns double (or raises "not in range" for `min(i + 1)`); `int64 + float_col` past 2^53 raises ArrowInvalid instead of rounding to double like DuckDB; `%`, `//` unsupported (F-094); `-s` on int32 gives int64; `sum(int32)` is double (F-060).

## F-104 Chained LEFT JOIN on the previous right table's key [done]
Found by the F-086 review (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer on main
- [x] `t LEFT JOIN u ON t.k = u.k LEFT JOIN x ON u.k = x.k`: for a `t.k` with no match in `u`, `u.k` is NULL so `x` does not match (z = NULL, as DuckDB). `_copy_right_keys` also scans later joins' ON clauses for `u.k`, and `_use_right_key_copies` rewrites those ON references to the NULL-on-unmatched copy before `_parse_join_on` (which now swaps `x.k = u.k` written either way round, and registers a copy as the left key's name). After an INNER JOIN on a copy, `x.k` resolves to the copy. A right column the join renamed (`x.k` -> `k_right`, when it is not a key) is named by its output name in a later ON
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_sql_chained_left_join.py`, cpu and gpu): two and three chained LEFT JOINs on the previous right key (either operand order), mixed INNER / LEFT, a previous right table's non-key column (`u.v`), USING forms, aggregate over the chain, unmatched rows at each step; 29 of 45 failed before
- Review round 2: a DECIMAL literal (point, no exponent) against an integer column or expression is compared exactly in `_compare` with `Decimal` (`< 2.5` is `< 3`, `> 2.5` is `> 2`, `= 2.5` never holds, a bound past int64 is a constant; BETWEEN and IN too); only a DOUBLE (`np.float64`) literal casts the integer column to double (`_align_literals`, `_double_vs_int`). The native decline for big float constants is gone; `_threshold` instead treats an integer column's float bound of exactly +-2^63 as a value, not as out of range. Tests: `test_decimal_literals_exact`
- Not changed: a bare `k` in a later ON stays the left table's (DuckDB calls it ambiguous)

## F-105 SQL CASE: string results, and NULL when no ELSE [done]
Seen in the F-086 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] a CASE whose branches are strings raises "Failed to parse string 'hit' as double": the result type is forced to float. Infer the CASE result type from its branches like DuckDB (common supertype), strings and dates included
- [x] a CASE with no ELSE gives 0 instead of NULL: `count(case when p then 1 end)` counts every row (silent wrong answer, found by the F-106 developer); a missing ELSE is NULL
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: string / date / int / mixed-number CASE results, with ELSE and without (NULL), in SELECT / GROUP BY / ORDER BY; fails before
- Built: `compiler.case_when_arrow` / `case_literal` (Arrow evaluation in `_eval_predicate` and `_eval_expr_arrow`; the GPU precomputes a CASE through the same code). Result type = DuckDB's common supertype: equal types stay, integers int64, `float32` next to an integer or a DECIMAL literal stays float32 and only a DOUBLE widens it (F-106's rule), other number mixes float64, `string` / `large_string` / dictionary one text family, NULL branch takes the other's type, all NULL int32. `when()` without `otherwise` and SQL without ELSE are NULL (the DataFrame default changed from 0; CHANGELOG and docs/api.md say so). `_translate_case`: `CASE x WHEN v` (it ignored `x`), `THEN NULL / TRUE / DATE '..'`. `_arrow_aggregate_reason` counts a CASE with a NULL branch as "has nulls" (`_can_be_null`), so the fused pipeline no longer drops the NULL group of a CASE key; the bytecode declines a NULL literal and `sum(case .. else 0 end)` stays native (Q8, Q12, Q14 unchanged vs main). `tests/test_f105_sql_case.py` (326, cpu / gpu, native on / off; 195 of the first 258 failed on main).
- Review round 1: float32 branches keep float32 (`case when p then r else 0.1 end` is 0.10000000149011612), large_string and dictionary strings; tests for both.
- Follow-ups, not fixed: `max(case when .. then big end)` past 2^53 raises (float64 in the NULL-aware aggregate); `CASE x WHEN NULL` is unsupported; a timestamp branch next to a date branch should promote to timestamp (now raises); int + DECIMAL branches give double, DuckDB gives an exact DECIMAL; MIN / MAX of a string or date CASE raises "could not convert 'hit' to double" (moved to F-108)

## F-106 SQL float literals: scientific notation and float32 columns [done]
Found by the F-082 developer (2026-10-16)
- [x] a scientific literal without a dot (`x > 1e-310`, `x < 2E5`) raised ValueError: `sql_frontend` did `lit(int(v)) if "." not in str(v)`. `_number` now parses by SQL type: digits only (`1_000` too) is an int, a point and / or exponent a float, an integer past int64 (DuckDB HUGEINT) a float; `_is_double_literal` marks the exponent forms (DOUBLE in DuckDB; `.5` and `5.` stay DECIMAL, F-094's modulo flag unchanged). A double literal is `lit(np.float64(v))`, so a bare `select 1E-5` is float64, not float32
- [x] a float32 column against a DOUBLE literal: DuckDB widens the column (`x between -1e-30 and 1e-30` drops float32(1e-30)) but casts a DECIMAL or INTEGER literal to float32 (`x = 0.1` and `x = 16777217` match; F-094 / Q6 rely on it). So only exponent literals widen: `_widened` multiplies the float32 column by `np.float64(1.0)` in a comparison, BETWEEN, IN, `+ - * /`. The bytecode (pipeline_program, cpu and gpu) already computes in double, so no kernel change; the Arrow path (`_arith_operands`) widens a float32 array next to an np.float64 literal. Parquet pushdown never pushes float columns, so nothing to change there
- [x] also fixed on the way: `f64_col > 9223372036854775807` raised ArrowInvalid (Arrow refuses the implicit int to double cast past 2^53; `_align_literals` converts), and `f32_col = 16777217` raised on the Arrow path (the literal now converts through float)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_f106_float_literals.py`, cpu and gpu, native on and off, 352 cases): every literal form in WHERE / SELECT / arithmetic / IN, huge integers, float32 columns against 1e-30, 0.1, 16777217 forms, CASE, parquet scan; 244 of 352 failed before
- Review round 1: the minus sign is folded into the literal before the int64 range check (`_number(text, neg)`), so `-9223372036854775808` is an int64 (native declines a float constant past 2^53 against an int column, `pipeline_program._try_cmpc`); an integer literal past int64 against an integer column is a constant (`_compare`, also BETWEEN and IN; NULL stays NULL); the Arrow path compares an integer column with a float constant in double like DuckDB instead of raising (`_align_literals`); widening is decided by the expression type (`_ftype`: DOUBLE literal or float64 column, through + - * / and negation), so `g > 1e-30 * 1` and `-g < -1e-30` widen. Tests add int64 rows at 2^53+1, INT64_MAX, INT64_MIN
- Not changed: a bare `select 0.1` still returns float32 (DuckDB: DECIMAL); `i + 99999999999999999999` is a float (DuckDB: HUGEINT, exact)

## F-110 SQL float32 arithmetic keeps a FLOAT result, and float32 through functions [done]
Found in the F-106 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] float32 arithmetic runs in double while DuckDB keeps a FLOAT result, so `g * 0.5 > 0.05`, `g + 0.1 > 0.2` and `g * 2 = 0.2` on a float32 column differ from DuckDB on boundary rows (wrong on main too; repro `/tmp/rv106_f110.py`). Round each float32 intermediate to float32 (or compare at float32) on every path
- [x] a float32 column through a function (`abs(g)`, `coalesce(g, 0)`, `cast(g as float)`) raises "Unsupported SQL expression type"; once supported, `_ftype` must give them the argument's type so `abs(g) > 1e-30` widens
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests; fails before
- Done in F-106 (round 2): a DECIMAL literal against an integer column or expression is compared exactly (`i < 9223372036854775807.0` keeps INT64_MAX-1)
- Built (2026-10-17): DuckDB probed first: FLOAT `+ - * /` with FLOAT / INT / DECIMAL literal is FLOAT (operand cast to float32, result rounded per operation), with a DOUBLE (exponent literal, float64 column) DOUBLE; `sqrt ln power exp sin` of a FLOAT are DOUBLE; `abs coalesce CASE floor round cast(as float)` stay FLOAT; SUM / AVG DOUBLE, MIN / MAX FLOAT. New `Expr.f32()` (op `f32`): the front end (`_float_result`, `_as_f32` in `_widened`) rounds every FLOAT arithmetic result to float32 and casts the other operand first (a DECIMAL / INT literal becomes `np.float32`, an integer expression `.f32()`; `g + int64_col` no longer raises); IN lists against a FLOAT are cast to float32 too. Arrow paths: `cast_arrow(.., "float32")`. Bytecode: `OP_F32 = 34` (`float64(float32(a))`) in `pipeline_program.py`, `pipeline_cpu.mojo` (also the executor) and `pipeline_gpu.mojo`; libmxpipeline_cpu / libmxexec_cpu / libmxpipeline_gpu rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh cpu` and `gpu`, hashes recorded. Float modulo stays on Arrow (not rounded). `abs / coalesce / cast` of a float32 column and `_ftype` were already done in F-100.
- Tests: `tests/test_f110_float32_arith.py` (325 cases, cpu + gpu, native on and off, WHERE / SELECT value and type / aggregates / GROUP BY / HAVING, against a native DuckDB table): 120 fail on main, all pass. `pixi run test -k tpch`: 335 passed. `native_coverage.py --sf 1`: 22/22 no Arrow row work, same declines as before; `--sql`: unchanged except Q21 raising a correlated-EXISTS NotImplementedError (not arithmetic; not checked against main). Full suite: 22984 passed, 274 skipped, 5 xfailed (/tmp/f110_full.log).
- Not done: `count(*) FILTER (WHERE ..)` is unsupported in the SQL front end (tests use CASE); float32 `%` is not rounded.


- Review (2026-10-17): PASS. Trade-off recorded: SQL rounds float32 arithmetic like DuckDB, the DataFrame API still computes float32 in double, so SQL and DataFrame Q1 differ by ~1e-8 and SQL is now further from the exact DECIMAL answer (Q1 1.6e-9 -> 1.2e-8, Q3 2e-8 -> 5.4e-8; still 18x under the 1e-6 tolerance; equal to DuckDB on the same float32 Parquet)
- [ ] quiet-machine check: SQL Q1 / Q3 on cpu against main (each float32 op adds an OP_F32 pass; Q1 charge goes from 4 to ~8 ops); expected a few percent on CPU, ~free on GPU
- [ ] follow-ups: `test_aggregate` asserts result types (MIN / MAX FLOAT, SUM / AVG DOUBLE) via `typeof`; `count(*) FILTER (WHERE ...)` unsupported in SQL; float32 `%` (probe suggests it already matches DuckDB; pin with one test)
## F-107 Test process hangs on a futex while other suites share the GPUs [done]
Seen four times on 2026-10-16 (F-070 developer, F-094 review, a main-repo run, F-103 developer): a full-suite process idles at ~0% CPU for 30+ minutes, 145 threads in `futex_do_wait`, holding the GPU device handles, while 3-4 other suites use the same GPUs. Killing and rerunning passes. Could be load, could be a lock-order deadlock between our GPU warm-up / residency upload threads (F-057, F-063) and a query, or a CUDA context lock across processes
- [ ] (no dump was ever captured) capture a stack: the test runner now dumps all threads after 15 minutes stuck (`faulthandler_timeout=900`); keep suite output in a file (lessons). ptrace is blocked here, so the dump is the only way
- [x] from the dump, find the waiting lock(s); if it is ours, fix the lock order and add a stress test (concurrent queries + background upload + warm-up); if it is the driver under cross-process contention, document it and make the GPU tests serialize on a file lock

Finding (no dump was captured: the stack "try_split -> thread start" is the F-103 developer's report of what they saw; the cause was found by reading the lock sites and reproduced): `coproc_fused._evict`, the `weakref.finalize` callback on a cached input's owner, took the non-reentrant `coproc_fused._lock`. A finalizer runs on whichever thread drops the last reference or runs the cyclic GC, and a GC can start at any allocation, including inside `with _lock:` (`_start_thread` holds it across `Thread.start()`, which allocates; so does every other locked block). The thread then waits for itself: main thread idle, all other threads parked in `futex_do_wait`, last stack in `try_split` -> thread start. Load only changes when the GC fires. Not the CUDA driver, not cross-process.
Fix: `_evict` never blocks; it queues the key and evicts at once when the lock is free, else `_drain_evictions()` (blocking, called before every cache lookup: `_acquire`, `_start_upload`, `resident_for`, `clear_residency`) does it, so a dead owner's entry is still never found. Same pattern removed in `aot_kernels._get_cuda_driver` and `gpu_stream._driver` (DeviceArray `__del__` calls them while the init holds their lock): lock-free fast path once built.
Tests: tests/test_f107_lock_reentry.py (3 deterministic, child processes with timeouts; two hung before the fix) and tests/test_f107_stress.py (a child process: 4 threads of concurrent `device="auto"` computes on fresh 400k-row tables, each queueing a resident upload, with the GPU warm-up running and the cyclic GC firing at nearly every allocation; a watchdog dumps all stacks with faulthandler and exits 97 when no compute finishes for 60 s). Short by default (48 computes, 2 s); `MXFRAME_F107_STRESS=1`: 6 threads x 1000 (6000 computes, ~75 s). Before the fix the long run stalled within the same time with the watchdog dump in `coproc_fused.resident_for -> deferred_background.flush -> request_upload -> _start_upload`; after the fix it passes.
Full suite on this branch while other suites shared the GPUs (load 13-19): 10348 passed, 198 skipped, 4 xfailed in 15:51, no hang and no faulthandler dump (log kept at /tmp/f107_full.log).
Review round 1: also drain in `begin_prepare` and `resident_count`; comment at `_dead_keys` on why a queued key cannot hit a new owner's entry.

## F-108 SQL TIMESTAMP literals and temporal expressions in aggregates [done]
Found by the F-093 developer (2026-10-16)
- [x] `TIMESTAMP '2024-01-01 10:00:00'` (and `CAST('...' AS TIMESTAMP)`) is unsupported in the SQL front end (`Unsupported SQL expression type: Cast ... TIMESTAMPNTZ`); map to a timestamp literal compared in the column's unit and zone like DuckDB
- [x] MIN / MAX of a computed date expression (`MIN(d + INTERVAL 1 DAY)`) keeps the date type (F-093 covered plain columns only); the same for a CASE with date results, and MIN / MAX of a CASE with string results (found in F-105: `_aggregate_with_nulls` evaluates the argument then casts it to float64, and `np_arrow.group_aggregate` has no string min / max; plain `min(s)` is answered elsewhere)
- [x] `HAVING min(ts) > DATE '2024-01-01'` raises ArrowNotImplementedError (timestamp vs date32 compare); DuckDB accepts it (date promoted to timestamp)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: timestamp literals in WHERE / HAVING / BETWEEN against s / ms / us / ns columns, with and without time zone; MIN / MAX of date expressions; fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `sql_frontend._timestamp_of`: `TIMESTAMP '..'`, `CAST('..' AS TIMESTAMP)` and `TIMESTAMP_S / _MS / _NS '..'` are a `np.datetime64` literal of that type's unit (TIMESTAMP is microseconds, finer digits are cut like DuckDB; parsed by hand, so `2999-01-01` does not wrap in ns; a bad or out-of-range text raises ValueError, a zone offset or TIMESTAMPTZ NotImplementedError). Comparison alignment in `compiler._align_temporal` (WHERE, BETWEEN, HAVING, IN via `np_arrow.is_in`): a DATE next to a timestamp becomes a timestamp of that column's unit and zone, a naive literal next to a zoned column is an instant in UTC (DuckDB's session zone is assumed UTC), two zones are one instant, units compare in the finer one (Arrow does that; ns values past 2^53 stay int64, tested to the single nanosecond). `_is_date_col` also recognises timestamp columns and `x + INTERVAL`, so `DATE '..'` next to them is a date (promoted), not the TPC-H day number: `HAVING min(ts) > DATE '2024-01-01'` works. `_supertype` handles temporal branches: a date next to a timestamp is that timestamp's type, two timestamps take the finer unit, a zone and no zone raise (DuckDB: "Cannot mix"), so CASE and COALESCE of timestamp and date work. `custom_ops._computed_extremes` (before `_string_minmax`): a MIN / MAX whose argument is a computed value made of text, date / timestamp or 64-bit integer columns and literals (CASE, COALESCE, NULLIF, `d + INTERVAL`) is evaluated once by Arrow into a column of the aggregate's input, and the aggregate then takes the paths that already answer a plain column: F-072's rank-based string min / max, F-093's temporal min / max, F-090's exact int64 min / max (`max(case .. then big end)` past 2^53 no longer raises). Numeric arguments are not touched. Also needed by the ticket's own example: `date_col +/- INTERVAL n DAY | HOUR | MINUTE | SECOND` on a column (new op `add_us`, result TIMESTAMP in microseconds in the argument's zone, like DuckDB; MONTH / YEAR on a column raise NotImplementedError). `tests/test_f108_sql_timestamps.py` (1549 tests, cpu + gpu, native on and off; 1456 of 1497 fail on the F-100 commit; a Parquet scan, a join and a subquery included). Review catch before commit: `date '1998-12-01' - interval '90' day` next to an int32 day-number column (TPC-H Parquet) must stay a day number: the SQL native-coverage run raised on 10 queries until `_is_date_col` asked about the column inside the interval (regression test added).
- Not done / notes: a zoned ns / s / ms column keeps its own unit (DuckDB has one zoned type, microseconds; its sub-microsecond digits are lost there, ours stay); a timestamp literal compared with a zoned column assumes UTC; a string next to a timestamp (`ts > '2024-01-02'`) is still unsupported; `d + 1` / `d - d` (date arithmetic with numbers) is not supported; `select date '2024-01-01'` in a SELECT list returns a day number (float) instead of a date (pre-existing, `_date_lit`).
- Review round 1 (2026-10-16): `_computed_extremes` looks inside `abs`, `cast` and `+ - * %` (`max(abs(big))` past 2^53 no longer raises) and takes any integer column, and runs the rewritten plan through `compile_and_run`, so `max(coalesce(small, 1))` / `max(case .. then id end)` are integers again (they were doubles: 5.0 for 5; plain `max(id)` was already an integer). DST: `tests/test_f108_sql_timestamps.py` pins `zm +/- interval` across 2024-03-10 and 2024-11-03 against DuckDB (UTC session: an interval is a fixed length, a day is 24 hours). `docs/api.md` documents TIMESTAMP literals, INTERVAL on a column (TIMESTAMP result; MONTH / YEAR raise) and the UTC assumption.

## F-109 SQL JOIN ON ignores the table qualifier [done]
Found by the F-104 review (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer on main
- [ ] `_parse_join_on` matches columns by name only. `t JOIN x ON x.v = t.k`, where `k` and `v` are in both tables, joins `t.v = x.k`: mx returns ids 1, 2, 5 and DuckDB returns 2, 5. Decide each side of an equality from its qualifier (`t.` / alias in the tables joined so far, or the table being joined), falling back to the column name only for an unqualified column; an unqualified name found in both sides raises (DuckDB: ambiguous)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests: qualified ON in either operand order with the same column names in both tables, two and three tables, LEFT / INNER, a USING form; fails before
- Built (2026-10-16): `_parse_join_on(on, alias, right_schema)` decides each side of an equality with `_on_side` (qualifier: the table being joined is R, a table in `self._joined` is L; a bare name must be in exactly one side, in both raises 'ambiguous', in neither 'unknown column'), either operand order, and names the left column through `_expr` (the same resolution F-095 / F-099 / F-104 use: renamed, copied, aliased). `_use_right_key_copies` is deleted: it rewrote qualified ON columns to bare names, which lost the side (`y.v = x.v` after `t JOIN x ON x.v = t.k` became ambiguous), and `_expr` does the same lookup. `tests/test_f109_join_on_qualifier.py` (81, cpu + gpu): 44 fail on main.

## F-111 SQL ambiguous bare column in a join silently picks the left one [done]
From the F-095 review (2026-10-16); a silent wrong answer
- [x] a bare column name present in two joined tables raises `ValueError: ambiguous column reference: region (tables t, u): qualify it with its table` (DuckDB: "Ambiguous reference to column name"); case-insensitive (`Region` vs `region`); SELECT, WHERE, GROUP BY, HAVING, ORDER BY, ON, comma joins, self-joins, subqueries (their own scope and the outer ones); qualified names stay fine
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_f111_ambiguous_column.py`, 64 incl. gpu; 24 fail on main): each error case first checks DuckDB raises, each good case equals DuckDB
- Probed DuckDB (1.5.1): the exceptions are a USING / NATURAL column (one merged value; it also wins over a later table's same-named column, `t JOIN u USING (k) JOIN x ON x.q = 1` then `k`), ORDER BY (a name that is a SELECT output wins: `SELECT t.v ... ORDER BY v` is fine, `SELECT v AS vv ... ORDER BY v` is ambiguous), the inner scope first for subqueries (the outer tables only count when the inner one lacks the name), a JOIN's ON sees only the tables up to itself, a SEMI / ANTI table is seen by its own ON only. The SELECT alias does not rescue WHERE / GROUP BY / HAVING (`SELECT t.v AS k ... WHERE k > 0` is ambiguous when both tables have `k`). A USING key that two unmerged left tables have (`t JOIN u ON .. JOIN x USING (k)`) is ambiguous too.
- Built: `_SQLTranslator._resolve_names` / `_resolve_col` (the one resolution step) count the tables of a scope that have the name (`_check_unambiguous`), with the merged USING / NATURAL names per join. `test_sql_subqueries` had `SELECT k FROM t, u JOIN un ...` (k really is ambiguous): now `SELECT t.k`.

## F-112 SQL correlated subqueries: inner SELECT alias in WHERE, scalar in the SELECT list [done]
From the F-095 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] a correlated EXISTS / NOT EXISTS / IN whose inner WHERE uses an inner SELECT alias (`WHERE EXISTS (SELECT u.b AS bb FROM u WHERE bb > 5 AND u.k = t.k)`) works: the alias is replaced by its expression in the inner WHERE (`_unalias_where`; a column of the subquery's FROM with the alias's name wins, as in DuckDB; case-insensitive)
- [x] a correlated scalar subquery in the SELECT list is decorrelated (`_select_subqueries`, after the FROM / WHERE plan): the per-key aggregate is LEFT-joined on the key, and the item is rewritten over the aggregate columns, each COUNT as `coalesce(.., 0)`: no partner or a NULL outer key gives NULL (0 for COUNT, 1 for `count(*) + 1`, the default for `coalesce(sum(b), 0)`). Any expression over aggregates works (`max(b) - min(b)`, CASE), several subqueries per query, with `*` / `t.*`, WHERE, ORDER BY an output name, LIMIT, DISTINCT, derived tables inside the subquery. An unaliased item is named from the subquery text (DuckDB's text differs in case and parentheses)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests (`tests/test_f112_correlated_subqueries.py`, 74, cpu + gpu; 70 fail on main) with mixed-case names, NULLs in the outer key, inner key and aggregated column
- Still refused, with a clear NotImplementedError: a correlated scalar subquery in the SELECT list of a query that has GROUP BY or aggregates, in ORDER BY, a non-equality correlation (`u.b > t.a`) or an expression on the outer side (`u.k = t.a / 10`), a grouped / LIMIT inner query. Seen, not in this ticket: a constant WHERE condition (`WHERE 1 = 1`, also from an alias to a constant) raises `ArrowTypeError: Filter should be array-like` on the Arrow filter path.

Declines so nothing ignores the NULL: the file-scan pushdown (`io_parquet`, a NULL list is not pushed), the dictionary shortcut (`arrow_dict`), the bytecode (`_isin` already raised Unsupported for a non-number), and `_can_be_null` (an `isin` with a NULL yields NULL, so a group-by over such a WHERE takes the null-aware path instead of crashing on a nullable mask). `tests/test_f114_sql_in_null.py` (155, cpu + gpu, native on and off): all 163 fail on main.

## F-115 SQL `select date '..'` returns a float day number, not a date [done]
Found in the F-108 review (2026-10-16); also on main
- [x] `select id, date '2024-01-01' as c from t` returns the day number as a float (19723.0), DuckDB a DATE. The front end turns every `DATE '..'` into a day number (`_date_lit` false) because the TPC-H Parquet files store dates as int32 day numbers; it is a date only next to a date32 / timestamp column. A DATE constant in the SELECT list (also `coalesce(x, DATE '..')` with no date operand, `DATE '..' + INTERVAL ..` as an output) should come out as date32. Decide by context: a comparison with an integer day column keeps the day number, everything that is output is a date
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests for SELECT, CASE and subquery outputs; TPC-H checks: all 22 SQL queries (`tests/test_sql_tpch*.py`, `scripts/native_coverage.py --sql`) unchanged in result and path
- Built (2026-10-16): a SELECT item goes through `_value` (the CASE / COALESCE path of F-108), so a DATE constant that is an output is a date32; comparisons never reach it and keep the day number against an int32 column. `DATE +/- INTERVAL` as an output is a TIMESTAMP (us), as DuckDB (the ticket said date32; probed). Root cause behind derived tables: a zero-row Project of a string / date literal had type null (`_eval_expr_arrow` `lit`), so `FROM (select date '..' as c ...) WHERE c > DATE '..'` took the date for a day number. `_no_constants` counts date constants, so `coalesce(NULL, DATE '..')` is refused like `coalesce(NULL, 5)` instead of crashing. `tests/test_f115_sql_date_output.py` (99, cpu + gpu, native on and off; the int32 day column is a DATE in the DuckDB reference, which refuses INTEGER vs DATE). `native_coverage.py --sf 1` and `--sql` output identical to before; `pixi run test -k tpch` 314 passed.
- Not done, found on the way (also on main): a constant next to an aggregate (`select g, 1 as c, count(*) ... group by g`) raises "must appear in the GROUP BY"; `(select date '..')` as a scalar subquery; `min(date '..')`; `extract(year from date '..')` on a bare literal.
- Review fix (2026-10-16): a temporal value meeting an int32 day column is read as days (`compiler._align_temporal`: int32 -> date32), and `date - int32/date` is the day count (int64); derived-table shapes added to the test (139 total).
