## F-122 SQL constant WHERE conditions [done]
Found by the F-111 / F-112 review (2026-10-16); on main too
- [x] `WHERE 1 = 1` / `WHERE 1 = 0` raise `ArrowTypeError: Filter should be array-like`, `WHERE TRUE` raises "Unsupported SQL expression type: Boolean" (`WHERE 1 = 1 AND a > 1` works). Fold a constant predicate: true keeps every row, false / NULL keeps none (with the right schema)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests incl. constant conditions in HAVING, ON, CASE, and under NOT; fails before
- Note (2026-10-17): decided at translation, in the SQL tree (`_fold_conditions` / `_fold_cond` / `_const_truth`, three-valued): a TRUE WHERE / HAVING is dropped, FALSE or NULL becomes a Filter that is false on every row (`_never`; keeps the columns and types; an aggregate over it still returns its one row), a decided CASE branch is dropped or ends the CASE, a FALSE / NULL conjunct in an ON filters the right side (INNER empty, LEFT keeps the left rows with NULLs), `x OR TRUE` is TRUE, `x AND FALSE` FALSE, NOT keeps NULL. Left: `WHERE (select 1) = 1` (scalar subquery of a constant), a constant condition in the SELECT list (`select 1 = 1 as b`), a typed NULL from a CASE with no branch left (comes out null-typed). Tests: tests/test_f122_constant_conditions.py (680 across cpu/gpu x native on/off). Review fixes: exponent literals fold as DOUBLE (`0.1e0 + 0.2e0 = 0.3e0` is false), a constant predicate Arrow evaluates to one boolean is broadcast (`compiler._eval_predicate`: `1 IN (1, 2)`), `'true'` / `'false'` fold, a NULL beside a real condition under NOT is a boolean NULL, a FALSE ON conjunct keeps the keys beside it and an empty right side is not counted against the cross-join cap.

## F-123 SQL WHERE using its own SELECT alias [done]
Found by the F-111 / F-112 review (2026-10-16); on main too
- [x] `SELECT v AS kk FROM t WHERE kk > 0` raises KeyError; DuckDB accepts a SELECT alias in WHERE when no FROM column has that name (the FROM column wins). F-112 did this inside correlated subqueries only; do it in every WHERE (and GROUP BY / HAVING, per DuckDB's rules)
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests incl. an alias shadowing a column, an alias of an aggregate (not allowed in WHERE), case-insensitive names; fails before
- Note (2026-10-17): `_inline_aliases` (one function, WHERE + ORDER BY expressions + F-112 subqueries). Probed DuckDB: FROM column wins in WHERE; HAVING takes the alias even over a FROM column (already so); GROUP BY takes it only when no FROM column has the name (already so); alias in a JOIN ON is an error in DuckDB (still is). Alias of an aggregate or window: ValueError "not allowed in WHERE". Not done: a SELECT alias used by a later item of the same SELECT list (`select x as kk, kk + 1 as j`, DuckDB accepts). Tests: tests/test_f123_where_select_alias.py (200, 144 fail on main).

## F-124 SQL subquery correlated two levels out [done]
Found by the F-111 / F-112 review (2026-10-16)
- [x] `EXISTS (... AND EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM w WHERE w.k = t.k))` raises "unknown column t.k in a subquery": the column exists, two scopes out. Support it (decorrelate through both levels) or raise "correlation two levels out is not supported"
- [x] tests; also the F-112 review should-fixes: tests for the ORDER BY / non-equality / LIMIT refusals and repeated outer keys, `_unalias_where` written once, and the ORDER BY output-name exception matching case-insensitively where DuckDB raises
- Built (2026-10-17): **both**, by shape. A nested EXISTS / NOT EXISTS that reads the outer query's columns and none of the middle subquery's is a condition on the outer row alone: `EXISTS (u: A(u) AND E(t))` is `EXISTS (u: A(u)) AND E(t)`, so `_correlation(sel, what, lifted)` hands it back and `_exists_form` decorrelates it like an EXISTS of the outer query, at any depth (the three-deep case recurses through the same function); the inner-only part keeps its own keys, and an inner query with no outer key (`EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM u WHERE u.b > 5 AND EXISTS (... t.k))`) still counts rows. Everything else two levels out raises `NotImplementedError: <what>: correlation two levels out is not supported (t.a is a column of the outer query used inside a nested subquery[, which also reads the subquery's own columns]; only a nested EXISTS / NOT EXISTS that needs the outer row alone inside an EXISTS is)`: the nested query also reads the middle one (`w.k = u.k AND w.m = t.a`, it needs a join of u and w), a NOT EXISTS above it (the lift is only valid under a positive EXISTS), a nested IN, and the IN and scalar-subquery forms. `_unalias_where` is already one function (`_inline_aliases`, F-123).
- Probed DuckDB for the should-fixes (all in `tests/test_f124_two_level_correlation.py`): repeated outer keys (`u.k = t.K AND u.j = t.K`, `u.k = t.K AND u.k = t.K`, two columns against one outer column) equal DuckDB in EXISTS, NOT EXISTS, IN, comparison-with-subquery and the SELECT list; they already worked, now pinned. ORDER BY an output name matches without regard to case (`ORDER BY M`, `mM DESC`) as before. `ORDER BY m + 1` (any case) over a subquery alias raises in DuckDB too (BinderException "Alias referenced in a SELECT clause - but the expression has a subquery"); we raise NotImplementedError; `ORDER BY (SELECT ...)` DuckDB answers and we refuse with "supported only as a comparison that is a top-level AND condition of WHERE". Refusals pinned by message: ORDER BY on a subquery, non-equality (`u.b > t.a`) and outer-expression (`u.k = t.K / 1`) correlation in EXISTS / IN / scalar / SELECT list, LIMIT and GROUP BY in the inner query.
- Tests: `tests/test_f124_two_level_correlation.py` (85, cpu + gpu; 35 fail on main: the lifted shapes and the two-levels-out message). Full suite on the F-127 + F-124 branch, output in `/tmp/full_f127_f124.txt`: 22,364 passed, 274 skipped, 5 xfailed, 23 minutes at load 13-17 (shared machine). `tests/test_f112_correlated_subqueries.py` unchanged and green.

## F-060b Exact integer arithmetic without the min/max pre-pass [done]
Split from F-060 (2026-10-17)
- [x] replace F-037's min/max pre-pass for `+ - *` with a runtime flag in the bytecode: int64 results in the kernels, overflow and an int64 past 2^53 meeting a float op set a flag, the exact path answers
- [x] tests: exact results past 2^53 with no pre-pass (`_minmax` / `col_bound` / the int64 range check counted: zero), overflow raises; from the F-060 review: the `pipeline` / `pipeline-repro` kernel tests assert `fused_pipeline_cpu`, the GPU tests assert the mode (0-4) the kernel library picks
- Built (2026-10-17), the F-060 / F-060c pattern (int64 BITS in a float64 register word). New ops `OP_IADD = 15`, `OP_ISUB = 16`, `OP_IMUL = 17` (int64 over two "int" registers), `OP_F2I = 18` (a float register of whole numbers within +-2^53 to int64 bits), `OP_I2F = 19` (int64 bits to a double). `OP_LOADI` (F-060c) now feeds arithmetic too. **The flag** is the F-060 one (`Prog.ovf`, `ovf_a` on the GPU): an IADD / ISUB / IMUL result outside int64 sets it (IMUL: the double product below 2^62 in magnitude proves it fits, any other lane is checked exactly, by dividing back; the GPU first tests both factors inside +-2^31), and so does `I2F` of a value outside +-2^53. The kernel returns `E_OVERFLOW (-21)`, Python declines (`SumOverflow`), Arrow's checked kernels answer or raise "Overflow in multiplication of INT64" like DuckDB. Lanes past the block's row count are never looked at. **Compiler** (`pipeline_program._Builder`): a value has a representation, `rep` "f" (float64 register), "i" (int register) or "c" (a bare integer column, loaded on demand: `as_reg` = LOAD_COL as a double, `int_reg` = LOADI as int64). An integer `+ - *` whose bound, from the column TYPES only (`type_bound`, int64 = 2^63), exceeds 2^53 is an int op on `int_reg` operands; one the types prove stays the float op it was (so `int32 + int32`, `year(d) * 100`, literal factors, every TPC-H program are unchanged). Every float consumer (compare, `/`, float `+ - *`, `%`, `year`, IN, BETWEEN, a derived group key) reads an int value through `as_reg` = I2F. The aggregates read int registers: `OP_AGG_ISUM` now takes int64 bits (a float source gets an F2I), `min` / `max` of an int64 expression is `imin` / `imax` (a derived group key, `__key*`, stays a float register: the executor reads it as one). **Deleted:** `_Builder.col_bound(tight)`, the data pass behind it, `col_bounds`, `within_exact`. `year_days`' range pass is another thing and stays. **`int_regs=False`** (was `int_extremes`): no `OP_LOADI` (the fused join's gathered partner columns): a column is loaded as a double (range-checked, as before) and converted with F2I, the arithmetic is int64 all the same. **`types_only=True`**: integer arithmetic the types cannot prove raises `Inexact` and the program is `None`: `int_sum_inexact`, the late-stage question (the compiled graph has no int64), which now asks the types only. **Mojo:** `pipeline_cpu.mojo` (`_int_bin`, `_f2i`, `_i2f`, `_run_ops`; the ISUM sources in `_accumulate`, `_accumulate_shared`, the staged path read bits), `executor_cpu.mojo` (the three programs that ran with a dummy flag cell, the mx_filter family, the top-k filter and the fused join's fact pre-filter, now check theirs and return `E_OVERFLOW`; `hashagg_cpu.mojo` needed no change: it runs `_process_range`), `pipeline_gpu.mojo` (`_run` takes `ovf`; `_imul_ovf`; the ISUM reads in modes 0, 3, 1, 2, 4). Rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh cpu` and `gpu`, hashes recorded, `record_build_hash.py --check` passes. F-082's `par` / `par_n` / `sync_par` wrappers untouched; nothing in the float rounding of the bytecode (F-110) was touched. **Python elsewhere:** `native_exec` gives `DT_I64X` to a LOADI-only int64 column in the filter, top-k and pre-filter programs too (as `try_execute` did); `np_arrow.checked_arith` and `compiler._double_vs_int` convert an integer array beside a DOUBLE array with a rounding cast, like DuckDB (Arrow refused an int64 past 2^53 with "Integer value ... not in range": a bug on main on the exact path the flag now sends queries to, F-103 had listed it).
- Tests: `tests/test_f060b_int_arith.py` (167): sums / max of int64 products and sums on every kernel shape (global, 7, 1,000, 1M and 200k groups; direct and hashed float keys; native, native-repro, pipeline, pipeline-repro, GPU, GPU-repro) with the path asserted and the GPU mode asserted, the pre-pass counters at zero; the digits a double loses (odd products near 2^55); overflow of `* + -` at both ends, the largest product that fits, rows a WHERE removed that would overflow; int64 expressions meeting floats (within 2^53: on the bytecode; past it: declines, equals DuckDB); SQL; Parquet with 240 row groups and three streamed budgets, a streamed overflow; a CPU+GPU split at four fractions; a join; the native row filter, the top-k filter and the fused join's fact filter, exact and flagged. **On main** (scratch copy, the new tests added): 99 of 167 fail (the counters, the path / mode asserts, `sum(a * b)` going to Arrow, the ArrowInvalid on int64 beside a double); the rest are controls (overflow raising, equality with DuckDB). Changed, intent kept: `test_int_arith_2_53.py` (9 tests: the compiler no longer declines, it emits IADD / ISUB / IMUL, and declines only with `int_regs=False`; no query reads the data), `test_f060_int_sum_kernels.py` (the F-060 review item above). The F-037 / F-061 / F-060 / F-060c / F-101 / F-103 files pass unchanged.
- `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1` and `--sql`: output identical to main (no TPC-H query has int64 arithmetic).
- Full suite once on the build before merging main, output in `/tmp/full_f060b.txt`: 22,809 passed, 288 skipped, 5 xfailed, 3 failed (20 minutes at load 6-8): `test_f057_coproc.py` (two: it spied the deleted `col_bound`; a `sum(int64 past 2^53)` is now accepted by the split check, since it reads int64) and `test_fused_join_agg.py::test_integer_sums_over_fact_and_partner_columns` (the fused join declined an int product: fixed by giving the join int64 ops through F2I, `types_only` for the late stage); those files rerun green. Then merged main (F-110 OP_F32 = 34, F-074): no op number collides (mine are 15-19), libraries rebuilt, `record_build_hash.py --check` passes, `test_f110_float32_arith.py` and the integer / kernel / split / join files rerun: 1,151 passed, 43 skipped; coverage vs the merged main still identical.
- [ ] **Quiet-machine check (not run: the machine is shared, no benchmarks).** Removing the pre-pass should be a speedup; the int ops should cost nothing the pass did not. Idle machine (load under 2, nothing on the GPU), `main` vs this branch, one fresh process per query, min and median of 7: (1) `pixi run python scripts/bench_int_sums.py --sf 1 --label before|after` (also `--device gpu`): Q1, Q6, Q18 (F-060's) and the new A1-A3, int64 * int32 arithmetic that read `price`'s min / max first on main. Expect A1-A3 faster (one pass over 6M rows less) and Q1 / Q6 (which have `price * (100 - disc)`) not slower; accept each within 5% of main or faster. (2) `pixi run python scripts/bench_user_felt.py --mode engine --sf 1 --queries 1-22 --engines mx-cpu,mx-gpu --out /tmp/f060b_{before,after}`: no TPC-H program changes, so every query must be within noise; the coverage run above already shows identical paths. If A1 is slower on the CPU, `_int_bin`'s IMUL (an AVX2 int64 multiply is three 32-bit ones plus the double product used as the overflow filter) is the suspect.
- Follow-ups found, not done: int64 `< <= = ...` on two int registers is a float compare through I2F, so `a * b > c` with both past 2^53 declines to Arrow (correct, slower): an int64 compare op would keep it on the kernels; `%` of an int register and a derived group key from one go through I2F the same way; a fused join + aggregate has no `OP_LOADI` (its gathered partner columns would need it in the front stage), so an int64 partner column past 2^53 still declines there; `uint64` columns are still unsupported by the bytecode.

## F-060c Integer MIN / MAX return int64 on every path [done]
Split from F-060 (2026-10-17)
- [x] integer `min` / `max` past 2^53 still come back as float64 on the native paths (F-090 routes values past 2^53 to the exact Arrow route and casts the type back, so values are exact; the kernels themselves compute in float). Compute integer MIN / MAX as int64 in the kernels (CPU and GPU), so no route-and-cast is needed
- [x] tests: exact 2^60-scale values, grouped and global, native / pipeline / GPU paths asserted; fails before
- Built (2026-10-17), the F-060 pattern (the float64 slot holds int64 bits, no reader treats it as a float). Two new ops: `OP_LOADI = 3` (a bare integer column's values widened to int64 and stored as that int64's BITS in a register; nothing but an extreme slot reads that register) and `OP_AGG_IMIN = 54` / `OP_AGG_IMAX = 55` (slot identity INT64_MAX / INT64_MIN as bits: `_fill_identity`, and `PipelineProgram.identity` for the GPU). `_Builder` emits them for `min` / `max` of a bare integer column of any width (`int_extremes=True`); output ops `imin` / `imax` (executor `O_IMIN = 7`, `O_IMAX = 8`), the int64 result is cast to the column's own type by `_exact_counts`. Sites (every one that touched `OP_AGG_ISUM` or `OP_AGG_MIN`): `pipeline_cpu.mojo` `_run_ops` (LOADI, dense and gathered), `_accumulate` (global SIMD int64 lanes; grouped), `_flush_slot` and `_accumulate_shared` (cached and direct; atomic `Atomic[int64].min / max`), `_init_values`, `_init_partials`, the output identities, the legacy per-worker merge, the table-of-sub-totals merge, the staged path's bins; `hashagg_cpu.mojo` (`_flush` lanes, worker identities, the new-entry identity, the partition merge); `executor_cpu.mojo` (`O_IMIN` / `O_IMAX` words, `_out_value`, HAVING / ORDER BY decline past 2^53 as for `O_ISUM`: `_int_out`); `pipeline_gpu.mojo` (`_load_i`, mode 0, 3, 1 (shared-table atomics, flushed with a global atomic that skips the identity), 2 (global atomics: llvm `atomicrmw` min = 8, max = 7 on the untyped slot), 4 (ordered), the warp reduce and the block merge (`_combine`), `_ident`). F-082's `par` / `par_n` / `sync_par` wrappers are untouched. Python: `pipeline_program` (slot kinds, `exact_cols`, `merge_partials`, `finalize`), `native_exec` (`_OPS`, `_INT_OUT`, `range_partials` for the CPU share of a split), `pipeline_exec`. **The 2^53 guard**: a column that only `OP_LOADI` reads is exact past 2^53, so `_int64_past_2_53` (pipeline path) skips it and the executor gets dtype `DT_I64X = 8` for it (`_i64_cols_exact` checks dtype 1 only; `_itemsize(8) = 8`); a column also read as a number (a filter on it, a `sum`) is an ordinary float column and still declines past 2^53. **F-090's route**: the early pass (`pc.min_max` over the column of every int64 min / max query) is gone; Arrow still takes an int64 extreme in integers whenever it answers for another reason (NULLs, a date / timestamp extreme next to it, `exact_sum`), and the `late` call (every pipeline declined, the next stop is the float64 compiled graph) still routes values past 2^53 to Arrow, so that code is not dead, but it no longer runs for a query the kernels answer. A fused join + aggregate (`try_execute_join`) compiles with `int_extremes=False` (its partner columns are gathered copies): float slots, and it declines past 2^53 as before (the joined table is then aggregated by the single-table path: tested). An int64 *expression* (`max(a + 1)`) still goes through the float registers (exact: its inputs are bound within 2^53).
- Rebuilt with `scripts/build_pipeline.sh cpu` and `gpu`, hashes recorded, `record_build_hash.py --check` passes.
- Tests: `tests/test_f060c_int_minmax.py` (149, 14 skipped for hashed groups on the GPU; 64 fail on main: the path is `arrow_null_semantics` for any column with a value past 2^53, and the early `min_max` pass is there; the rest pass on main because F-090 made the values right): every width (int64 / int32 / int16 / uint8 / uint32; values at INT64 MIN / MAX, 2^53 + 1, a group holding only the identity), global and grouped, against DuckDB's values AND types, with the path asserted on native / pipeline / GPU; with a filter and sums / counts in the same query; NULLs; empty input; every kernel shape (global, 7, 1,000, 500,000 and 200,000 groups; reproducible; hashed float keys; the GPU's modes by group count) with 2^54..2^62 values; SQL (ORDER BY / HAVING over an extreme past 2^53 declines to Arrow and still orders exactly); a Parquet scan with 30 row groups; streamed at three budgets; a CPU+GPU split at four fractions; over a join. Changed: `test_f057_coproc.py` (the "int64 past 2^53" shape now filters on the column too, so it is still read as a float; a new shape pins that `max(i)` alone is accepted by the split check). Review catch in the full-suite run: a date `min` next to an int64 `max` past 2^53 (`test_f093_temporal_minmax.py`) raised "not in range" because the Arrow path relied on F-090's pass to set `exact_sum`; Arrow now takes a 64-bit integer extreme in integers whenever it answers (NULLs or a temporal extreme).
- Full suite once on the F-101 + F-060c build, output in `/tmp/full_f101_f060c.txt`: 17,332 passed, 4 failed (the two above, fixed; those files rerun green), 27 minutes at load 8-9 on the shared machine. `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1` and `--sql`: output identical to main (no TPC-H query takes an integer min / max of a column).
- [ ] **Quiet-machine check (not run: the machine is shared, no benchmarks).** Integer min / max now run in int64 lanes (AVX2 has no native int64 min / max: a compare and a blend) instead of float64 `min` / `max`, so speed may change. On an idle machine (load under 2, nothing on the GPU), in the style of `scripts/bench_int_sums.py`, one fresh process each, min and median of 7, `main` vs this branch (and `--device gpu`): `max` / `min` of an int32 and of an int64 column, global and grouped (4, 1,000 and 1.5M groups), SF1 `lineitem` 6M rows. Accept when each is within 5% of main; an int64 column with a value past 2^53 has no "before" on the kernels (it was Arrow plus a `min_max` pass): compare it with main's `arrow_null_semantics` time. If the global lanes are slower than float64's, the int64 compare + blend is the suspect.
- Follow-ups found, not done: an int64 extreme of an integer *expression* past 2^53 (the registers would have to carry int64 bits for the whole expression: F-060b's interpreter work); a fused join + aggregate with an int64 column past 2^53 still declines (its gathered partner columns would need `OP_LOADI` in the front stage).

## F-125 Modulo by zero: NULL rows in count and GROUP BY [done]
Found by the F-101 developer (2026-10-17); silent wrong answers on main (Arrow path)
- [x] `count(a % z)` counts rows whose remainder is NULL (z = 0): 10 where DuckDB gives 6. `count(expr)` must skip NULL results of the expression, not only NULL inputs
- [x] `GROUP BY a % 0` returns no rows where DuckDB returns one NULL group: NULL group keys from an expression form a group
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests for count / sum / avg / min over expressions that produce NULL (modulo by zero, NULLIF, CASE without ELSE), grouped and global, and GROUP BY on such expressions; fails before
- Built (2026-10-17): root cause was `custom_ops._can_be_null`, which knew CASE-without-ELSE and `IN (.., NULL)` but not `mod`: a plan whose only NULL source is a zero divisor went to the fused / native / group-id paths, which assume a computed value or key is never NULL (`present_mask[composite]` IndexError on a NULL key, an empty result for `GROUP BY a % 0`, and `count(a % z)` counting every row). `mod` by anything but a non-zero literal now counts as nullable, so `_arrow_aggregate_reason` sends the plan to Arrow's hash aggregate, which skips NULL results and keeps a NULL group; the same predicate keeps the F-101 `OP_MOD` kernel from being used on a nullable result. NULLIF and CASE without ELSE were already covered. `x / 0` is not NULL in DuckDB (count 7 of 7), not touched. `tests/test_f125_null_expressions.py` (514: 11 expressions x count / sum / avg / min / max, global, grouped, filtered; GROUP BY keys; with and without NULL inputs; 60 and 20,000 rows; cpu + gpu; native on and off); 54 of them fail on main (24 on gpu)

## F-126 AVG of large integers, and F-101 / F-060c follow-ups [done]
Found by the F-101 / F-060c review (2026-10-17)
- [x] `avg(int64)` never raises on an integer overflow and returns DuckDB's exact double, grouped and global, on every path (native, fused pipeline CPU, GPU, the Arrow NULL-aware path, streamed, CPU+GPU split, over a join, SQL); `sum` over the same data still raises
- [x] `INT32_MIN % -1` (and int8 / int16 / int64 minimum) raises "Overflow in division of -2147483648 / -1" on every path, like DuckDB
- [x] `test_f101_int_modulo.py::test_the_sign_follows_the_dividend_in_the_kernels` asserts the path that answered; the `custom_ops._exact_counts` docstring is current
- Built (2026-10-17). **Probe of DuckDB 1.5.1:** `avg(BIGINT)` is its HUGEINT total taken as an x87 `long double`, divided by the count there and rounded to a double. It is NOT the correctly rounded total / n (a case where they differ: ten values near 2^62, group 575 of the test: DuckDB 4.6116867269789e+18, exact quotient 4.611686726978899e+18; 19 of 19 differing random cases agree with the long-double formula and with nothing else) and not `float(total) / n` (which was also what the kernels did for any total between 2^53 and 2^63: 1 ulp off in about 1 table in 5; `native`, `fused_pipeline_*` and the split are fixed too). `np_arrow._ld_div` is the one definition; it is `np.longdouble`, so it follows the platform's x87 (x86-64 Linux here). **Overflow:** the kernels already decline on `E_OVERFLOW` (and on a column with a value past 2^53, which they never load), so the Arrow path answers: `np_arrow._int_group_mean` / `int_mean` add in 21-bit limbs, wider than int64, and divide with `_ld_div`; `sum` still goes through `_int_group_sum` and raises. **Kernel means:** `pipeline_program.finalize` (`imean`) divides the exact int64 total with `int_mean_of_totals`; the native executor divided inside Mojo (a double conversion first), so `native_exec._exact_imeans` asks it for the integer total (`isum`) plus a count and `_finish_imeans` divides in Python; an `ORDER BY` / `HAVING` over an integer mean declines (the executor would sort by the total). No .mojo change. **Streamed:** a batch or the merge that overflows raises like any sum; `streamed.try_stream` catches the OverflowError when the plan has a `mean` and runs the whole query again with each integer mean held as two int64 sums (`sum(x % 2^32)` and `sum(((x - x % 2^32) / 2^32).cast(int64))`, `_widen_mean`), put together as `hi * 2^32 + lo` in `_wide_mean` (good for about 2^31 rows of extreme values; a real `sum` that overflows raises again in the second run); the integer mean's `format` no longer casts its total to a double (it raised "not in range" past 2^53 even without an overflow). **Modulo:** the kernels do not compile `% -1` (`pipeline_program._mod` raises Unsupported: the dividend can be the type's minimum, and the registers are doubles), and `np_arrow.modulo` raises when a valid dividend equals the minimum of the RESULT type (int32 column % int64 -1 is 0 in DuckDB) with a divisor of -1 (it only checked int64 before, so int8 / int16 / int32 returned 0 on Arrow too). An empty array against a scalar divisor no longer broadcasts to one row (found by the `% -1` filter on no rows).
- Tests: `tests/test_f126_int_avg.py` (115: values past 2^53 whose total passes int64, a column whose values each fit a double but whose total does not, both signs, INT64 MIN / MAX, a total between 2^53 and 2^63; global and grouped; native / pipeline / GPU with the answering path asserted; sum still raises; avg beside min / max / count / float avg; NULLs, an all-NULL group, empty input; 60 tables of the one-ulp case; ORDER BY / HAVING over the mean; SQL; streamed at three budgets and with spilled group state; a CPU+GPU split at four fractions; over a join), against DuckDB bit for bit; 90 fail on main (main's engine in a scratch copy with the new tests; the rest are controls). `tests/test_f101_int_modulo.py`: the sign test now runs the divisors on native / pipeline / GPU with the path asserted (`% -1` from Arrow, the rest on the bytecode), and 48 new cases of the type minimum % -1 (four widths, global / grouped / filter / key, three modes) plus one for the cases that must not raise; 51 fail on main.
- Review round 1 fixes: `np_arrow._X87` (`np.finfo(np.longdouble).nmant >= 63`) guards the long-double imitation; without it `_ld_div` is Python's `total / n` and `int_mean_of_totals` divides in float64 within 2^53 and per element in Python past it (the hi/lo split cancels for negatives on a 64-bit longdouble); `int_mean_of_halves` also takes the Python route when |lo| > 2^61; the streamed rerun calls `ParquetOut.reset()` so rows a sink already received are not written twice (without it the sink test writes 6,753 rows for 6,000 groups). Tests: no-x87 (flag monkeypatched) and sink + spill + overflow, 115 in the file; `-k "f060 or f101 or f056 or f125 or np_arrow"`: 1,440 passed (`/tmp/f126_final_b.txt`).
- Full suite once, output in `/tmp/full_f126.txt` (before the review fixes): 19,627 passed, 274 skipped, 5 xfailed, 0 failed (22 minutes at load 14 on the shared machine). No benchmarks run (shared machine); the kernels' hot loops are untouched, the native executor now returns a count next to an integer mean's total.
- Follow-ups found, not done: a streamed mean past about 2^31 extreme rows still overflows in the merge; the Arrow `%` of two columns where one is a literal -1 typed by DuckDB's literal rules is approximated by "literals do not count in the result type".

## F-127 SQL arithmetic gaps: NULL literals, integer division, math functions [done]
Found by the F-125 review (2026-10-17): raise rather than answer (missing features, not wrong answers)
- [x] `a + NULL`, `a * NULL`, `a % NULL` ("Unsupported ... Null"): NULL is a typed NULL in arithmetic
- [x] `a // b` integer division ("Unsupported ... IntDiv"), with DuckDB's zero-divisor rule
- [x] grouped `a % 2.5` raises "Unsupported expression op: 'mod'" (the global form works): **not reproducible on main** (see the note)
- [x] `power`, `sqrt`, `ln`, `try_cast` if cheap
- [x] small cleanups from the F-125 review: `_can_be_null` docstring lists every NULL source; one helper for `year_days` shared by `compiler.py` and `custom_ops.py`
- [x] DuckDB-checked tests for each; fails before
- Probed DuckDB 1.5.1 first (all in `tests/test_f127_sql_arithmetic.py`): `a + NULL` is NULL of a's type (`int32 + NULL` int32, `/` and `power` DOUBLE); `//` truncates toward zero in the operands' type (`int32 // 2` int32, `uint64 // 2` uint64, `-9 // 4 = -2`), a float or decimal operand makes it a plain division (`7 // 2.5` = 2.8, `f // 2.5` double), a zero divisor is NULL for integers AND floats (`f // 0.0`), `INT64_MIN // -1` raises "Overflow in division"; `power(x, y)` / `x ** y` is a DOUBLE whatever the arguments (negative base to a fraction NaN, `0 ** -1` inf, overflow inf, never an error); **`sqrt` of a negative and `ln` of zero or a negative RAISE** ("Out of Range Error: cannot take square root of a negative number", "... logarithm of zero / of a negative number"), they are not NULL; `try_cast` of a double to an integer tests the UNROUNDED value against [MIN, MAX] (`try_cast(-2147483648.4 AS integer)` is NULL), NaN is NULL, a double too big for FLOAT is NULL (inf stays inf).
- Built (2026-10-17). New Expr ops `nullify` (typed NULL), `idiv`, `pow`, `sqrt`, `ln` in one table `np_arrow.SQL_MATH` (name -> function of the evaluated arguments), run by both Arrow evaluators (`compiler._eval_predicate_node`, `custom_ops._eval_expr_arrow`); the bytecode, kernels and executor never see them (no .mojo touched; they decline an op they do not know). `TRY_CAST` is the existing cast with a flag (`Expr.cast(..., try_=True)`, `cast_arrow(try_=)`); `sql_frontend` maps `sge.IntDiv`, `sge.Pow`, `sge.Sqrt`, `sge.Ln`, a Null operand of `+ - * / % // power` (`_arith_with_null`), `sge.TryCast`; `_is_int_expr` lets a NULL take the other operand's type (so `(a + NULL) + 1` stays an integer expression).
- **Where these route.** An aggregate or WHERE that contains one of the SQL_MATH ops goes to Arrow's hash aggregate (`_arrow_aggregate_reason`: "an expression only Arrow evaluates"): the legacy GPU path rounds every derived column to float32 (`sum(a // 2.5)` was 45.5999999 on the GPU), and Arrow filters FIRST, so `sqrt` / `ln` only see the rows the WHERE kept (DuckDB: `WHERE f > 0 AND ln(f) > 1` does not raise on `f <= 0`). A `sqrt` / `ln` predicate runs after the other filters of the same WHERE (`custom_ops._raising_last`) and, inside one AND / OR, only on the rows its left side leaves open (`compiler._eval_predicate_node`). Tests cover the guarded forms (projection, global, grouped, HAVING, AND, cpu + gpu, native on and off) and the unguarded ones raising `ValueError` with DuckDB's text.
- **`_can_be_null`** (`custom_ops`) now says in its docstring every NULL source and registers the new ones: `nullify`, `//` by anything but a non-zero literal (same rule as `%`, F-125), `TRY_CAST`. `sqrt` / `ln` are not sources (they raise); `x / 0` is inf / NaN. `tests/test_f127_sql_arithmetic.py::test_every_null_source_is_registered_in_can_be_null` pins each, and every NULL expression is counted with `count / sum / avg / min / max`, global, grouped, filtered, as a GROUP BY key (the F-125 class) on cpu + gpu. `compiler.year_days_arrow` is the one helper both evaluators call.
- **The grouped `a % 2.5` report: not reproduced.** Tried on main: SQL and DataFrame API, `sum / avg / min / max / count` of `a % 2.5`, `f % 2.5`, `i32 % 2.5`, `f32 % 2.5`, `a % 2.0`, `(a + 1) % 2.5` grouped, global, filtered, as a key, under HAVING / ORDER BY / a join / a derived table / a CTE, 40 and 30,000 rows, with and without NULLs, native on and off, cpu, gpu and auto: all answer and equal DuckDB. The message comes from the MAX-graph compiler (`compiler._visit_expr`), which `mod` could reach only if a plan got past the native, fused and Arrow paths. Structural fix so it cannot: `mod` joins the ops `custom_ops` pre-computes as an Arrow column before the graph (`_ARROW_ONLY_OPS`, one tuple for `mod` and the new ops, in `_needs_precompute`, `_extract_derived_cols` and the min / max kind check). Tests: `test_a_grouped_decimal_modulo_equals_duckdb` and four grouped `% 2.5` queries in the DuckDB matrix. If the reviewer has the failing query, it is worth adding.
- Tests: `tests/test_f127_sql_arithmetic.py` (2,828: 40 expressions x global / grouped / projection, filtered and key forms, cpu + gpu, native on and off, 60 and 20,000 rows, with and without NULLs; result types compared on projections; hand-checked integer division, try_cast, power edge values, overflow; sqrt / ln raising and guarded). On main (its sources, the new test file): 2,314 fail, 81 pass (the 81 are `% 2.5` shapes and plain values that already worked). `-k "sql or tpch"` 8,205 passed, 117 skipped, 3 xfailed.
- Review fix (2026-10-17): a sqrt / ln guard written AFTER the risky call no longer raises: `compiler.guarded_logic` flattens an AND / OR chain, runs the terms without sqrt / ln first, then each risky term only on the rows still open (`eval_on_rows`: `pc.filter`, compute, scatter back); `CASE WHEN f > 0 THEN ln(f) END` computes a risky branch only on the rows that take it (`case_when_guarded`, both evaluators). Tests added to the guarded list (guard-last, three-term AND, OR, count / GROUP BY, CASE in SELECT / sum / WHERE). Not matched: `sqrt(f) > 2 OR f < 0` raises in DuckDB (OR is not short-circuit there) and answers here.
- `scripts/native_coverage.py --sf 1 --sql`: all 22 lines identical to main (Q21 still raises, F-112). Output in `/tmp/f127_cov_main.txt` and `/tmp/f127_cov_new.txt`.
- Not done: `exp`, `log`, `log10`, `log2`, `ceil`, `floor`, `round` (not asked); `try_cast` of a string or a constant-only expression; `a + 1` still gives int64 for an int32 column where DuckDB keeps int32 (found while writing the type checks; on main).

## F-128 auto prices the CPU as the native executor [done]
Needs: none · Found by the F-058 scoreboard run (2026-10-17, quiet machine)
- [x] the cause, measured: with no calibration file auto priced the CPU from the older single-threaded kernels (join 11 ms per M rows; the native executor does 0.9-5.2), so SF10 Q5, Q8, Q9, Q18 and Q21 went to the GPU and ran 5-9x slower: auto 11.7 s against cpu 2.8 s over the 22 queries (`docs/bench/f058-engine-sf10.md`)
- [x] `native_global`, `native_filter`, `native_group` (by group count) and `native_join` (by build-side rows) prices, measured end to end through `compute(device="cpu")` on 2M / 8M / 32M rows on the reference box, are the defaults and calibration probes; `estimate_plan` prices every CPU line with them (the GPU side is unchanged)
- [x] auto's decision no longer imports `pyarrow.compute` for a string group key (~25 ms on the first query): string samples are read from the Arrow buffers with NumPy (one window gather per row, a 64-bit hash per string, a small 8,192-row sample first for keys with few values); same estimates as before on 16 column shapes (`tests/test_auto_key_card.py`)
- [x] a CPU query no longer queues the GPU warm-up again once it has started (each queued start cost a launcher thread per query)
- [x] COUNT(DISTINCT b) per a under auto runs as device="cpu" does, with codes where the source keeps them (it decoded the counted column; J4 at SF10: GPU 6.7 s, CPU on codes 0.66 s), unless `prefer="gpu"` or a GPU placement (`tests/test_count_distinct_codes.py`)
- [x] measured after the fix, engine-only first runs (`docs/bench/f058-auto-*.md`; cpu and auto in the same run): SF10 auto 2.96 s / cpu 2.80 s / DuckDB 4.96 s (was 11.7 s); SF10 shuffled auto 4.54 / cpu 4.83 / DuckDB 5.51; SF1 0.61 / 0.52 / 0.61; SF1 shuffled 0.69 / 0.60 / 0.61. Every auto query ran on the CPU. These runs were before the string-sampling speed-up and the warm-up change: warm, in process, auto is now within 0-2 ms of cpu on SF10 Q1, Q11, Q22 and 4 ms on Q16 (three computes; was 36 ms)
- [x] full suite 23,235 passed
- [ ] open: when does the GPU win now? On host data never in these runs (mx-gpu 0/22 at SF1 and SF10); resident data (F-057 split) is the case left to measure
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK, fixed: (1) a measured `native_*` fit now replaces every default of its name (`from_dict`, `_store_fits`; per-size defaults won in `_predict`, so a calibrated box priced joins from the reference box); the join probe measures three sizes per build size (`NATIVE_JOIN_BUILDS`, a grid now takes precedence over `bucketed`); (2) the string sampler reads every chunk with one window width (equal strings in chunks of different widths hashed apart: 7 distinct for 4); (3) `explain(device="auto")` and `compute` share the COUNT(DISTINCT) decision (`_auto_may_count_codes`, `_auto_codes_decision`); (4) that decision applies only when a dictionary column reaches the count (plain columns go to the cost model); (5) tests: mixed widths across chunks, `prefer="gpu"` decodes, plain columns are priced, explain says what compute does, measured fits override defaults, the join grid. Noted, not fixed: `string_view` keys still go to `pc.count_distinct`, which raises for them (predates this ticket)
- Review round 2: F-129 PASS (old and new `hash_keys` bit-identical on 20+ types and shapes, no writes to shared buffers); F-128 NEEDS WORK, fixed: (1) the codes decision reads the counted pair's own types from the sources (`_auto_codes_decision`): a dictionary in memory, a string in an unread Parquet file (the reader keeps it as a dictionary); an int-only Parquet pair and a dictionary column used only in a filter go to the cost model; explain = compute on memory and Parquet, coded and not (tests); (2) the `native_group` probe has its own grid up to 1M groups (`NATIVE_GROUP_CARDS`), so a measured fit keeps the cache cliff; (3) the join probe's smallest point is twice the build size (`min(card, n // 2)` kept the label's size), comment says the probe's side ratios
- Review round 3: NEEDS WORK, fixed by restructuring (Subtract Before You Add): one `_auto_codes_decision`, taken on the plan before it is resolved by compute and explain alike, and compute resolves with codes only when it says so (they agree by construction; the GPU never gets coded columns). It asks the Parquet reader which columns it keeps as dictionaries (`dictionary_columns`, metadata only: a dictionary over 64 KB, a file written without dictionaries and `dictionary=False` are not coded), reads a Scan's types, and returns None when a join or anything but filters and selects sits between the pair and its source (a shared column name across a join can no longer mislead it). Calibration: a measured per-size fit replaces only the default of the same key, so a size a slow box's time budget skips keeps the reference price and the 1M-group cliff stays (`_merge_measured`); a plain measured line still replaces every per-size default. Tests for each case
- Review round 4: NEEDS WORK (`_pair_is_coded` predicted codes for shapes the reader decodes: a filter above the select, a column-to-column predicate), fixed by subtracting the prediction: under auto every COUNT(DISTINCT) per key runs on the native CPU count, as device="cpu" does, with codes wherever the reader keeps them; the reason no longer claims codes ("the native cpu count (on dictionary codes where the source keeps them)"), explain and compute decide from the shape alone, the GPU never gets coded columns. Tests assert what the native count received (codes or plain) on 9 shapes (memory, Parquet small / large dictionary, plain file, dictionary=False, filter above the select, column-to-column filter), and the result against DuckDB
- Review round 5: NEEDS WORK (the reason promised the native count on shapes it declines, e.g. nulls; the shared-name join test could pass with no native count), fixed: the reason says only what was decided ("runs as device=\"cpu\" does (the gpu path decodes the columns; SF10 6.7 s against 0.66 s)"); tests: the join test asserts the native count ran on the left int column, a nulls shape takes the same path as device="cpu". Reviewer on check 5: no query in the F-058 tables where mx-gpu beats mx-cpu on host data, so forcing the CPU for this shape is safe; data already on the GPU is unmeasured
- Review round 6: code and tests PASS; the site and plan sentences that still said "the native count" now say "as device=\"cpu\" does", the nulls test asserts the native count declined. Done

A guard in `tests/conftest.py` (`_no_env_leaks`) now fails any test that leaves an `MXFRAME_*` variable changed and restores it; two full runs with it were clean, so the cause is not found yet (if the guard never fires, look at threads or `/proc/meminfo` reads under memory pressure). Earlier note: `test_native_lookup.py::test_a_selected_partner_read_from_parquet_keeps_its_strings_encoded` streamed into a Grace join (depth 6, hot key) on KB-sized files: the host budget must have been tiny for that query. No test leaves `MXFRAME_HOST_MEM_BUDGET` set; the default budget is 70% of MemAvailable. If a busy machine can make a small in-memory query stream and fail on a hot key, the default needs a floor

## F-130 Small-query regressions since 0.6.0 [done]
Needs: none · Found by the F-058 random suite (2026-10-17): SF1 MXFrame / DuckDB 0.83x (0.6.0) -> 0.60x, a median 5 ms per query slower; DuckDB's own times unchanged
- [x] A/B against the 0.6.0 release-run code (worktree at 37265ea, same data, fresh process per run) and `git bisect` on random SF1 seeds: (1) seed 1 (MAX of a float expression over a join) 26 -> 48 ms from F-108 `92e77c1`: `_computed_extremes` materialized the join below the aggregate before checking whether the MIN / MAX argument is text, temporal or a 64-bit int, then left a float expression alone; (2) seed 2 5.8 -> 7.9 ms from F-056 `4cbab57`: every first `compute()` imported `streamed` and with it `spill` (shutil, tempfile, bz2, lzma), even for in-memory data
- [x] fixes: the computed-extremes rewrite decides from `column_types` first and materializes only when a MIN / MAX needs it or a column is computed below the aggregate (`tests/test_f108_sql_timestamps.py`: no join materialized for a float MAX over a join; fails before); `streamed` is imported only for a plan with a Parquet source or while a stream runs (`tests/test_first_query_imports.py`)
- [x] auto on a fresh process paid ~3 ms to load the calibration and price a small query: below `COLD_GPU_SOURCE_ROWS` (25M source rows; 10 ms per M rows is twice the dearest native price, and the GPU's start-up alone is >= 250 ms) with the GPU not started, auto picks the CPU unpriced and queues the warm-up off the clock (`start_gpu_warmup_if_any`), so later queries can still split (test: no calibration import during the query, warm-up queued once)
- [x] measured, fresh process, median of 3: seed 1 cpu 48 -> 25-27 ms, auto 25-27; seed 2 cpu 7.9 -> 5.5, auto 5.7 (0.6.0: 26 and 5.8)
- [x] full suite 23,258 passed
- Review: PASS (no must-fix). Should-fixes taken: (1) the 25M-row bound and its comment were wrong (1M-group native price is 9.5 ms per M rows, and costs add over operators): the cold shortcut now bounds the query's CPU cost with every operator at the dearest default native rate of its kind (`COLD_NATIVE_MS_PER_MROW`) over all source rows, against `COLD_GPU_START_MS` (240 ms, the lowest start-up measured here: 245.6); a test checks the rates bound the default fits, another that a dear plan is priced; (2) on a machine with no GPU every query queued a warm-up thread: the first one remembers "no gpu" (`_no_gpu`) and no more are queued (test); (3) the redundant try/except is gone; plus a regression test for MIN / MAX over a join with clashing column names (int64 past 2^53)
- [x] the site's auto explainer prices the CPU with the native fits (per-size interpolation ported from `_predict`), has the cold-GPU rule, and its simulated session queues the warm-up after a cold-rule query; the global shape's GPU side includes the host filter like `estimate_plan`

## F-131 M0: Mojo package spike [done]
Needs: none
- [x] `scripts/build_mojo_pkg.sh` builds `build/mxframe.mojoc` (Mojo 1.1 packages are `.mojoc`) from `mojo/mxframe/` plus the kernels copied from `kernels_aot/` as `mxframe/kernels/` with package-relative imports. Measured why: a `.mojoc` built with `-I kernels_aot` left the consumer with "unable to locate module 'pipeline_cpu'", and a shared library that re-imports `@export` functions exported 0 symbols, so `kernels_aot/` stays the one source and the Python `.so` builds are untouched
- [x] a Mojo binary built against the package alone runs the native CPU kernel: exact SUM / COUNT under a filter over 2M int64 values past 2^53 (cancelling, so a float64 sum would drop the +1s), and `E_OVERFLOW` (-21) when the total passes int64; no Python: `ldd` shows the Mojo runtime, libc, libm, libstdc++, libgcc_s (and libzstd / libsnappy, which every test links)
- [x] Parquet codecs: zstd and snappy from the pixi env (pyarrow's), called by `external_call`, 1 MiB round trips (zstd 5 KB, snappy 60 KB compressed); linked with `-lzstd -lsnappy`; to vendor or link statically for iOS later (agreed: small C libraries, replaceable)
- [x] `pixi run mojo-test` builds and runs `mojo/tests/test_*.mojo` and fails any test binary that links libpython; `mojo/README.md` says how to build, test and link

Open for M6 / M7: build the kernel test on a Mac (the GPU modules import `max.gpu` at the top; a CPU-only package may have to leave them out), and static codecs for iOS
- Lesson for M1: Mojo frees a value at its last use, so an address taken from a `List` dangled before the kernel ran (the program's magic check failed). The Table and Query API own their buffers; no raw addresses cross the API

## F-140 Mojo API: a query over a Parquet file bigger than RAM (streamed row groups) [done]
Needs: F-135, F-137 · Spec §2, §5 · `mojo/mxframe/parquet.mojo`, `query.mojo` · Owner asked 2026-10-17 ("step 1": the cheap half of a disk tier; spilling joins / big group-bys is a later ticket)
- [x] `Query(scan_parquet(path))` runs `where` / `group_by` / `agg` one row group at a time: only the columns the plan reads are decoded, each row group becomes a Table, the CPU kernel runs on it, partials merge (sums and counts add with the int64 overflow check, min / max combine, mean = merged sum / merged count). Peak memory is one row group's columns plus the result
- [x] TEXT: `scan_parquet` finds each TEXT column's file-wide dictionary when it opens (page headers walked; dictionary pages decoded, dictionary-indexed data pages not even decompressed; a PLAIN text page, as after pyarrow's dictionary fallback, is decoded for its words), in the order `read_parquet` adds them, so a word keeps its code. While streaming, each row group's chunk-local dictionary is mapped onto it (one lookup per distinct word). `scan.word_count(c)` / `code_of` / `text_of` give `groups=` and filter literals before the query
- [x] equal to `read_parquet` + the same query on CPU, every slot / count / sum / min / max / `present` and the slot layout (each group is lowered with a non-null count per aggregate so partials share one layout; the merged result is re-laid out as a whole-file run, using the null counts seen). Tests: `mojo/tests/test_scan.mojo` (our writer, zstd / snappy / none, 8 row groups, an all-null column in a group, a late word, overflow), `tests/test_mojo_parquet.py` (pyarrow: zstd / snappy / none, dictionary on / off, page v1 / v2, small pages, empty row groups at the start, middle and end, dictionary fallback to PLAIN; DuckDB-written files; every query also checked against DuckDB SQL; a file with no rows, truncated files, 60 random byte flips)
- [x] memory proof (`scripts/bench_scan_memory.py`, 60M rows x 5 columns, 60 row groups, 1,624 MB file in /tmp, query by_region over region / revenue / cost): streamed peak RSS 72 MB (anon 58) vs 4,462 MB through `read_parquet`; under a 600 MB cgroup cap (`systemd-run MemoryMax`) the streamed run finishes with the same answer and the `read_parquet` run is killed at 603 MB. One decoded row group of the three columns is 46 MB. Informal: the stream took 6.9 s (single-threaded decode, 8.7M rows/s) against the kernel's 98 ms on in-memory data
- [x] AUTO / CPU: always the CPU; `QueryResult.device` is CPU, `compute(GPU)` and `on_device` raise "a Parquet scan runs on the CPU, one row group at a time". The F-138b builds still work: `test_switches` (no GPU, serial, no codecs; a zstd file cannot be opened, an uncompressed one streams) and `scripts/check_mac_link.sh` pass, with no new libc call (the scan uses `open`, `lseek`, `mmap`, `munmap`, `close`)
- Review round 1 fixes: the scan keeps the file open and checks its size before each row group; dictionaries are built lazily per column (reviewer's 5M-row unique-id file, `sum(int)`: 943 MB / 2.5 s -> 39 MB / 0.25 s); a row group's text pages are decoded straight into file-wide codes (no chunk-local dictionary, no remap pass)
- Design choices: API `scan_parquet(path) -> ParquetScan` + `Query(scan)` (a Query constructor beside `Query(table)` / `Query(frame)`); file access is a read-only `mmap` of just one column chunk's bytes (page-aligned for 4 KB and 16 KB pages, `total_compressed_size` bounds it, `munmap` straight after decoding), never the whole file and no `pread`; the page walker is the one `read_parquet` uses (`_read_chunk`), given a base shifted to the chunk
- Limits / open after this ticket: row groups decode one at a time on one thread (decode overlapped with the kernel, or column chunks decoded in parallel, would speed it up); a sum whose partial totals pass int64 raises as in memory but the partials differ (per row group), so a borderline input can raise on one path only; a high-cardinality text column the query DOES read keeps all its distinct words in memory (a TEXT column's file-wide dictionary is built on first use, so an unused one costs nothing); a truncation while one column chunk is being decoded is a bus error (a change between row groups raises "the file changed since it was opened"); a file with a wrong `total_compressed_size` raises "(corrupt file)"; no Frame overlay on a scan; the GPU per row group, joins / group-bys whose state outgrows RAM (spill, as `spill.py` / `grace.py`), CSV streaming
- Review round 2: PASS (7 adversarial files x 4 shapes x 8 filter words equal to read_parquet + Query; 450 more byte flips, never a signal; hc.parquet `sum(v)` 37 MB / 0.26 s, was 943 MB / 2.5 s). Wording fixed: only a truncated or resized file is refused; a same-size rewrite in place is not detected (size only; mtime would need `fstat`). For F-141: build every dictionary a query needs before any parallel decode (`ensure_words` mutates the shared scan info)

## F-141 Mojo API: Parquet decode at memory speed (bulk, then parallel pages) [done]
Needs: F-140 · `mojo/mxframe/parquet.mojo` · Engine-first (owner 2026-10-17: algorithms and speed over usability). F-140's streamed query spent ~98% of its time decoding (2.34 s for 20M rows, the kernel ~40 ms)
- [x] bulk decode: a row group's columns are sized once (uninitialised, every row is written) and written in place, no per-value `push` / `append_null`: PLAIN int64 / int32 by SIMD copy / widening loop, FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY decimals by one byte swap each, definition levels unpacked straight into the page's validity bits (a bit-packed run of 1-bit levels IS a bitmap run; a repeated 1 sets a range) and merged into the column bitmap, values spread over the null rows backwards in place, dictionary indices unpacked and gathered into codes in one loop (TEXT: file-wide codes), an uncompressed page is read in place (no copy), the file is memory-mapped (no read of the whole file into a buffer), TEXT words are found by a byte-keyed index (`_WordIndex`, a String is made only for a word seen for the first time). The old per-value `_plain` / `_read_chunk` / `_remap_words` are gone
- [x] parallel pages: the page headers of every column of a row group are walked serially (dictionary pages decoded there, so every dictionary the query reads exists before the parallel section; `ensure_words` runs before it, never inside), each page knows its first row, the pages of ALL columns of the group decode in parallel (`par_n`) straight into their slices; PLAIN text pages that must ADD words to a table (`read_parquet`) run serially in file order; page errors are recorded per page and the first in page order is raised; validity and null counts are merged in page order after the join; row groups merge in order (F-140). `read_parquet` and the scan use the same decoder (`_decode_group`)
- [x] measured, informal, 24-core machine with load average 4.5-5 (so noisy; `scripts/bench_parquet_decode.py`, snappy, 1M-row groups, files in /tmp): 20M-row numeric file (carrier, day, revenue decimal, cost; 346 MB): streamed query 2.79 s -> 0.75 s on one thread (serial build), 2.69 s -> 0.24 s on all cores; `read_parquet` 3.30 s -> 0.45 s one thread, 3.78 s -> 0.26 s all cores (in-memory kernel on the same query 0.18 s / 0.04 s). 5M-row text-heavy file (a 200k-word column that falls back to PLAIN pages): `read_parquet` 1.22 s -> 0.81 s one thread, 1.58 s -> 0.62 s all cores; streamed query on its two cheap columns 0.31 s -> 0.06 s / 0.02 s. Every checksum equals the pre-change answer. Uncompressed files: numeric `read_parquet` 0.46 s one thread (PLAIN no-null int64 columns alone decode at 2.5 GB/s, 0.12 s for 20M x 2 columns)
- Target (replaced after review): "within 1.5x of DuckDB on the streamed numeric query" (was "within 2x of the in-memory kernel", which snappy decompression and first-touch page faults make unreachable). Today 0.19 s against DuckDB's 0.10 s. Reviewer's numbers: numeric `read_parquet` 0.18 s vs pyarrow 0.25 s; text-heavy `read_parquet` 0.48 s vs pyarrow 0.12 s. Next: F-142 (two-phase parallel text decode), F-143 (decode row group N+1 while the kernel runs on N)
- Review fixes (round 1 and 2): before a column is sized, the rows a row group claims are checked: the pages add up to the chunk's num_values (the header walk), the chunk's num_values equals the row group's num_rows ("column `x` has N values in a row group of M rows (corrupt file)"), and rows x 8 bytes x the columns read must fit the machine's physical memory (`sysconf`; "row group claims N rows: X GB for 1 column, more than this machine's memory (Y GB); a corrupt file, or too big to read whole (stream it with scan_parquet)"), which `read_parquet` also checks for the whole file. The earlier per-page "2^20 values per byte" rule is gone (it refused real files and trusted the header's sizes). Also: a chunk with a second dictionary page is refused; the "dout of range" typo is fixed and tests match whole messages; the unused `_Ctx.rows` is gone; errors of serial (word-adding) and parallel pages are all recorded and the first in page order (columns in file order, pages in order within a column) is raised, header-walk errors first. Tests: both bomb2 files (4,096 empty pages of 2^23 rows; a snappy chunk of 64 pages claiming 2^31-1 rows and bytes), a row group whose pages disagree with its row count, two dictionary pages, an index past the dictionary, error order on a TEXT column under `read_parquet` (checked to fail with the serial pages raising immediately, as in round 1), and every cell of 32 pyarrow files (pages of 1 / 3 / 7 / 13 rows with nulls on the page edges, v1 and v2, zstd and none, a dictionary that overflows mid-column) through both `read_parquet` and the scan
- Limits: a corrupt file can still reserve up to the machine's memory (or up to a lower address-space cap's worth, which aborts: `ulimit -v` below the RAM is not consulted); that memory is uninitialised and untouched until the pages decode, and a corrupt page raises when it does. `read_parquet` maps the whole file, so a file truncated while it is read is a bus error (the scan maps one chunk at a time and checks the size before each row group). A file with more rows than fit in memory is refused by `read_parquet` with a pointer to the scan. `sysconf` is a new libc call in the package: the omxplorer app's tbd and `ALLOWED` need `_sysconf` (F-138c)
- Tests: all existing oracles green (pyarrow / DuckDB files, 22 corrupt-file cases, random byte flips, `test_scan`, `test_switches`, `check_mac_link.sh`); new: the serial build gives identical answers on a file of many small pages with nulls, streamed and whole-file
- Review rounds 2-4: the size guard became one check before sizing (each chunk's count equals its row group's, rows x 8 x columns read within physical memory via `sysconf`, negative or wrapping footer counts refused); round 4: PASS (ten bomb files refused readably under a 32 GB cap, 168-file cell probe 0 mismatches, 1,000 byte flips never a signal). Limit kept: an RLIMIT_AS below physical memory can still abort. The app (omxplorer) needs `_sysconf` in its Mac tbd / ALLOWED when it re-vendors past 8c74693

## F-142 Mojo API: text Parquet pages decoded in parallel (two-phase word codes) [done]
Needs: F-141 · `mojo/mxframe/parquet.mojo` · Text reads gain nothing from F-141's parallel pages (0.49 s to 0.48 s, 5M rows) and are 4x behind pyarrow (0.12 s), because PLAIN text pages that add words run serially
- [x] phase 1 in parallel: every PLAIN text page that may add words looks its values up in the frozen `_WordIndex` and records the words it misses (address, length, hash; one entry per distinct word of the page, a page-local table) and writes a placeholder `-1 - miss number`; phase 2 serial: the missed words get codes in file order (page order, first seen first, so the codes equal one serial pass's); phase 3 parallel: the placeholders are replaced by the codes. From 50,000 misses of a column in a row group the cross-page dedupe also runs in parallel (64 hash partitions, each task finds the first page/miss of each of its words), so the serial phase only names first occurrences, in order, and copies codes to the repeats. read_parquet and the dictionary build of the scan (`ensure_words`) use it; scan row groups already looked words up in the finished dictionary in parallel. The decompressed page is kept until its misses are read
- [x] every cell, words AND codes, equal on pyarrow files (`test_text_words_and_codes_through_the_two_phase_decode`: 240k rows in two row groups, small pages, unique ids with nulls (67k misses a group: the parallel dedupe runs), 60k words repeated across pages and groups with nulls, dictionary fallback mid-column, an 8-word dictionary column; v1 and v2; none and snappy): the words equal pyarrow's values, the codes equal a first-seen numbering made in Python, through `read_parquet` and the scan, and the serial build (`-D MXFRAME_SERIAL`) prints the same bytes. `pq_cells` has a `codes` mode for it. The F-141 oracles, `test_scan`, `test_switches`, the Mac link check stay green
- [x] measured (5M rows: `region` 8 words, `name` 200k words that fall back to PLAIN pages, `cost`; 1M-row groups, snappy, 39 MB; `scripts/bench_text_decode.py`, best of 7, load average 2.6-3.2): `read_parquet` 1.29 s -> 0.16 s on all cores (the F-141 number in this run; 0.48 s in the review), 0.72 s on one thread (unchanged: the same hashing and lookups, one core). pyarrow on the same file: 0.085 s all cores / 0.25 s one thread when it keeps plain strings, 0.236 s / 0.365 s when it dictionary-encodes `region` and `name` as ours do (`read_dictionary`). So the target "pyarrow's all-core time" is met against the like-for-like pyarrow read (0.16 s vs 0.236 s), NOT against its plain-string read (0.085 s, which builds no dictionary); one thread we are 2x behind its dictionary read
- Where the 0.16 s goes (all cores): the first row group's serial phase (~70 ms: naming 200k new words, a String and two hash inserts each), then ~23 ms a row group (header walk 5 ms, then the pages: a row group has only ~7 big text pages, so the slowest page, ~150k lookups, sets the time). Next steps: split big PLAIN text pages into value slices (a serial pass over the lengths, then parallel lookups), decode dictionary pages of different columns in parallel, a cheaper `Column` word insert (the `codes` Dict is hashed a second time), F-143 overlap. A 32-bit-tag slot layout for the word index was tried and was slower (kept the plain one)
- Review: PASS (16 adversarial pyarrow files: last-byte / NUL / empty / combining vs accented / CJK / emoji / 5 KB words, late first appearance, the 50k dedupe path: codes equal a Python first-seen numbering, the pre-F-142 build, the serial build and 5 parallel runs; 1,500 byte flips, never a signal). Fair bars: plain strings (pyarrow 0.086 s) is a different output; ours matches pyarrow `read_dictionary` + `unify_dictionaries()` (0.5-0.6 s, we are 3x ahead); for an engine, DuckDB hashing every word (`count(DISTINCT name)` by region, 0.163 s whole query) vs our 0.166 s decode. Follow-ups (with F-143): the `nowhere` buffer safe by construction; drop the `parallel` list

## F-143 Mojo API: a streamed query decodes the next row group while the kernel runs [done]
Needs: F-141 · `parquet.mojo`, `query.mojo` · Bar: within 1.5x of DuckDB on the streamed numeric query (0.19 s vs 0.10 s at 20M rows, F-141 review)
- [x] row group N+1 decodes while the kernel aggregates N: `_compute_scan` runs the two as the two tasks of one `par_n` (the decode's page jobs and the kernel's threads share the pool), and every dictionary the plan needs is built before the loop (the scan info is not mutated beside the kernel). Two row groups in memory (peak RSS 111 MB in the review against 80 MB for the one-group stream; 126 MB by `ru_maxrss` here, which also counts the mapped file pages). Partials are merged in row-group order; a decode error of group N+1 is held until group N has been merged, so an overflow while merging N is reported before it, and an error in N's kernel is raised before N+1's decode error (tests for both). The serial build (`par_n` loops its tasks) runs kernel then decode in turn
- [x] a pool of value buffers (decode into the buffers of a finished group) was built and measured against fresh allocation (best / median of 9 runs, 20M rows: pooled 0.159 / 0.172 s and 0.164 / 0.173 s, fresh 0.170 / 0.187 s and 0.159 / 0.172 s; peak RSS 127 vs 125-127 MB): no gain, so it and its `read_group_reusing` API were removed
- [x] measured against DuckDB on the F-141 bench files (20M rows, snappy, 346 MB; best of 5-7; load average 1.8-3.6): streamed numeric query (group by carrier: sum cost, sum revenue, count, max day) on ONE thread: ours 0.69 s, DuckDB 0.62 s (1.1x, bar met); on ALL cores: ours 0.15 s, DuckDB 0.07 s (2.1x, bar NOT met; it was 0.19-0.24 s before this ticket, the same query run in turn measured 0.185 s). Results equal (the answer checksum is the one from before the change); all oracles, `test_scan`, `test_switches`, the Mac link check green
- Where the all-core time goes (the review's analysis; the earlier "two page rounds" explanation was wrong): the single-page `carrier` column (1M rows in one dictionary-index page) is decoded by one worker; the ~1 MB dictionary pages of `revenue` and `cost` are decoded serially inside the header walk; and snappy is ~12 of the 18 single-thread ms. Next: F-145 (parallel dictionary-page jobs as a first round, big pages split by row range). Fewer kernel threads beside the decode was slower, MAP_POPULATE gave nothing
- Review round 2: PASS (an overflow merging group N beats N+1's decode error, tested; pool removed; 325 streamed runs equal the pre-F-143 answers; 1,500 byte flips). Bar not met on all cores (~2x DuckDB): F-145 next

## F-144 Mojo API: one word index per text column (a byte arena, no String per word) [todo]
Needs: F-142 · `table.mojo`, `parquet.mojo`, `csv.mojo`, `frame.mojo` · Naming new words is ~70 of F-142's 166 ms: each costs a String plus a second hash into `Column.codes`
- [ ] a TEXT column keeps its words in one byte arena with offsets and one hash index (the `_WordIndex` shape); the String-keyed `codes` Dict goes; `text_of` / `code_of` / `add_word` / the CSV reader / Frame session words / the Parquet writer use it; codes and words unchanged everywhere (every existing oracle)
- [ ] then: big PLAIN text pages split into value slices so the one-page tail of a row group shrinks
