## F-145 Mojo API: every core busy inside a row group (parallel dictionary pages, big pages split by row range) [done]
Needs: F-143 · `mojo/mxframe/parquet.mojo` · F-143 review: decode is the critical path and ~3-4 ms per group does not parallelise: a 1M-row single-page dictionary-index column decodes on one worker, ~1 MB dictionary pages decode serially inside the header walk, snappy is ~12 of 18 single-thread ms. Bar: within 1.5x of DuckDB on the streamed numeric query (0.149-0.164 s vs 0.072-0.086 s at 20M rows)
- [x] dictionary pages are decoded as parallel jobs (the scan's are lookups only; `read_parquet`'s TEXT dictionaries still add words in file order, in the header walk). A first design ran three rounds (dictionaries, pages, slices) and was SLOWER than before (0.181 s vs 0.162 s, A/B of 11 interleaved runs, load ~4): every round costs a launch and a join. The final design has two: round A = the dictionary pages, the pages that need no dictionary (PLAIN, text that adds words) and the preparation of every big page; round B = the small dictionary-indexed pages and one job per slice. A job writes its dictionary into its own slot, moved into the chunk's context after the join: with the dictionary assigned into the `_Ctx` itself by a job, other jobs of the same round that held that context lost the write (all dictionaries read empty after the round)
- [x] pages with at least 262,144 rows are decoded in slices of at least 65,536 values by row range: the serial part is `_prep_page` (decompress, definition levels into the page's bits, and the cut points: a dictionary-index page by one pass over its run headers, cutting at run boundaries or at groups of 8 values inside a bit-packed run and entering it mid-way with the groups still to read; a PLAIN INT32 / INT64 / FIXED page by byte offset), then `_decode_slice` per slice, which writes its values and spreads them over its rows (a slice holds the rows from its first value's row to the next slice's first value's row, so nulls split with it). No slicing in the serial build (nothing to run them on)
- [x] exact: new test `test_big_single_page_columns_slice_by_row_range_and_decode_the_same` (700k-row single-page columns: dictionary-index with and without nulls, PLAIN int64 / int32 with nulls, decimals of width 8 and 5, dictionary text, all-null; v1 and v2, none and snappy) compares every cell with pyarrow through `read_parquet` and the scan, in the parallel and the serial build (a deliberately wrong row cut made it hang, so the slices are exercised); all F-141..F-143 oracles, `test_scan`, `test_switches`, the Mac link check green; the answer checksums of the bench files are unchanged
- [x] measured against DuckDB (20M-row numeric file, snappy, 346 MB, streamed query group by carrier: sum cost, sum revenue, count, max day; load average 2.2-3.7): ONE thread: ours 0.72 s, DuckDB 0.62 s (1.17x, bar met); ALL cores: ours 0.166 s, DuckDB 0.070 s (2.4x, bar NOT met). A/B against the F-143 code, 11 interleaved runs at load ~3: 0.162 s best / 0.169 median before, 0.150 / 0.162 after (about 7% faster). `read_parquet` numeric 0.20 s -> 0.15 s (all cores); the text-heavy `read_parquet` is about 7% slower (0.208 s vs 0.194 s best, the extra bookkeeping) and still 0.16-0.25 s depending on load
- Why the bar is still out of reach with this design: one thread costs 34 core-ms a row group, 12 cores should take ~3 ms and a group takes ~6-7 ms; every group still pays two parallel rounds plus the kernel's launch and joins, and tail imbalance in each (the work is ~100-1000 µs jobs on 12 workers). DuckDB gives each thread whole row groups, so it has no join inside a group. The next step is that model (F-145's open item): several row groups decoded concurrently, one worker (or a few) per group, which trades the two-group memory bound for speed, a product call

- Review: PASS (cut-point probe: RLE / bit-packed mixes, 17-bit indices, 97% null pages, null edges every 8 rows, constant columns; every cell equal to pyarrow, parallel x3, serial, pre-F-145; 300 streamed runs; 2,000 byte flips). Kept: numeric read_parquet 0.222 -> 0.146 s. Follow-ups (with F-149): read-only `p` / `ctx` in slice and dictionary jobs (then re-test the lesson's stale-write explanation), `_Page`'s 33 positional fields grouped, skip round B when nothing needs it (text read back to one round). The gap to DuckDB is idle tails inside each barrier, not join cost; next is F-149 (morsels)

## F-146 Mojo API: the GPU for a streamed Parquet file [done]
Needs: F-145 · `query.mojo`, `gpu.mojo`, `parquet.mojo` · omxplorer (goal 20) opens files bigger than memory streamed, CPU only (`docs/plans/needs-mxframe.md` in the app)
- [x] `Query(scan).compute(GPU | AUTO)`: the group-at-a-time loop of F-143 with the kernel task replaced by "copy the decoded row group to the device, run the program in keyed mode, copy the partial back": row group N+1 decodes on the CPU (all cores, F-145's parallel pages) while group N is on the device; the partial has the CPU loop's layout and is merged in row-group order, so the answer equals the CPU scan's number for number (`test_gpu_scan.mojo`, and the pyarrow files of `tests/test_mojo_parquet.py` against the CPU scan, read_parquet and DuckDB). F-149's morsels stay CPU: 32k-row blocks are too small for a launch and a copy
- [x] the device side of a scan (`_ScanDevice` in `gpu.mojo`): one `DeviceContext`, ONE set of column buffers on the device that every row group is copied into in turn (grown when a group is bigger, never freed between groups), and TWO sets of pinned host buffers: row group N+1 is decoded straight into the set the device is not reading (`ParquetScan.read_group_into`: the page jobs write to the caller's addresses and the table keeps only validity and null counts), then copied from pinned memory at the bus's speed (13 GB/s against 4 GB/s from pageable memory) with no host copy; a group the GPU declines is copied into its table for the CPU. `_ScanContext` owns all of it and its destructor frees it on every way out of the scan (it synchronises, frees the buffers, then the context): the first version freed the context only on success, so a scan that raised kept it. Test: `test_gpu_scans_that_raise_give_their_device_context_back` (1 and 1,000 raising scans in one process, the process's GPU memory read from nvidia-smi: 512 MiB either way, the CUDA context itself; without the destructor 1,500 scans grew it by 234 MiB). The free-memory reading of the process cannot see this leak, because the CUDA context is shared inside it
- [x] device memory: one row group of the columns the plan reads (rows x columns x 8 bytes, 32 MB for 1M rows x 4 columns) plus validity bitmaps and the query's buffers (a few MB), never the file; host side: two pinned sets of the same size (64 MB). A group the device cannot hold: GPU raises, AUTO runs that group on the CPU and says so in `fallback_reason`
- [x] AUTO per file: the GPU when a GPU is there, the file has at least 5M rows and the plan runs more than `AUTO_GPU_OPS_PER_CORE_COLUMN` (3) kernel instructions a row per core per column read (`Query.auto_gpu_ops(n)`; 0: every file to the GPU); otherwise the CPU with the reason in `fallback_reason`. The constant was first 8 per core (columns ignored), then 5, and is 3 now that the copy is cheaper; the six cases below (light 28 instructions / 4 columns and heavy 90 / 3, on 12, 4 and 2 cores) all go to the faster side or to a tie: 12 cores CPU/CPU (28 <= 144, 90 <= 108), 4 cores CPU (tie)/GPU (28 <= 48, 90 > 36), 2 cores GPU/GPU (28 > 24, 90 > 18). `QueryResult.device` is GPU only if every row group ran there. Holistic aggregates: GPU raises, AUTO runs the CPU loop and says why. A Frame over a scan works on the GPU (the frame's mask slice is copied with each group, changed / appended rows added on the CPU after). No-GPU builds unchanged
- [x] measured, 20M-row numeric file (snappy, 346 MB, 20 row groups of 1M rows), GPU 0 (RTX 3090, shared), load average 4-7, warm context (the first GPU run of a process also pays ~0.2 s of CUDA start-up); times of `scan numeric` (light) / `scan heavy`: 12 cores: GPU 0.141 / 0.17-0.19 s (was 0.198 / 0.26 s before the persistent and pinned buffers, -29%), CPU morsels 0.107-0.113 / 0.153-0.161 s, CPU group loop 0.17 s, DuckDB 0.074-0.082 s for the light query. Pinned to 4 cores (`taskset`): GPU 0.23-0.26 / 0.19-0.20 s, CPU 0.23-0.24 / 0.35-0.40 s. Pinned to 2 cores: GPU 0.285 / 0.225-0.245 s, CPU 0.35-0.36 / 0.59-0.60 s. All checksums equal. The file is decoded on the CPU either way, so with many cores the CPU scan (decode and kernel at once on every core) still wins; the GPU wins where the cores are few or the plan heavy
- Left: several groups in flight, batching morsel blocks into GPU-sized pinned buffers (its own ticket), an app-owned `DeviceContext` for scans (`Query.on_device` is still refused for a scan), a GPU-side Parquet decoder
- Review round 2: PASS (512 MiB held after 1, 20 or 1,000 scans, failing or not: the context itself; pinned double buffers race-free; GPU = CPU = AUTO on 5 layouts x 10 queries, 12 repeats; no-GPU build links no CUDA). AUTO picked the faster side or a tie in all six cases: 12 cores CPU (light 0.109 vs 0.132 s), 4 cores heavy GPU (0.178 vs 0.366 s), 2 cores GPU (0.212 / 0.220 vs 0.344 / 0.597 s). The leak test skips when nvidia-smi cannot see the process

## F-147 Mojo API: editing a streamed file (a Frame overlay on a scan) [done]
Needs: F-136, F-140 · `frame.mojo`, `query.mojo` (marked `---- F-147` blocks), `parquet.mojo` (`ParquetWriter`), tests `mojo/tests/test_scan_frame.mojo`, `tests/test_mojo_parquet.py` (probe `mojo/tools/pq_edit_probe.mojo`) · API: `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md` §3 · omxplorer's streamed sheet is read-only today
- [x] `Frame(scan)`: sparse edits, appends, removes, merges and formulas over a ParquetScan with the same API as `Frame(shared)`; `get` reads the row group holding the row (a small bounded cache: decoded column chunks, 128 MiB by default, `set_cache_limit`); `Query(frame_over_scan)` streams with the overlay applied (mask + delta as F-136, per row group or morsel block; holistic aggregates too); `permutation` streams the column (O(rows) keys); `bake()` is refused
- [x] equal to `read_parquet` + `Frame` + the same edits on files that fit (random edits, undo, both scan loops); memory still bounded for files that do not (12M-row file, 4 MiB cache: save peak 84 MB on a 332 MB file)
- Decision: `bake()` over a scan is refused with a message naming `save(path)`; `save(path, metadata, compression)` streams the edited file out a row group at a time (`ParquetWriter`, which `write_parquet` now uses too), atomic by rename, so the app can save over the file it has open. Over a table, `save` writes `bake()`.
- Follow-ups noticed: every saved row group's dictionary page repeats all of a TEXT column's words (as `write_parquet` already did), which costs a lot for a high-cardinality text column; `permutation` over a scan is about 50 bytes a row.
- Review: PASS (edits at group / morsel-block edges, remove + undo, late-word merges, formulas over edits, holistic aggregates, two saves over the open file: equal in all three scan modes; saved files read the same in pyarrow and DuckDB; 60,000-read cache fuzz). Follow-ups in progress: O(1) cache lookup (2M rows in 1,000-row groups: a get walk 2.06 s vs 21 ms over a table), keep foreign key-value metadata and refuse a save that would narrow a column (TIMESTAMP -> DATE) unless asked, regression tests for groups not a multiple of 8 rows and for editing after a save over the open file, not thread-safe noted. Fixed by the main session: `pytest -n` raced on build/mxframe.mojoc (every fixture rebuilt it with `rm -rf build/pkg`): the package build is now locked, staged privately, published by rename and skipped when unchanged (161 passed with -n 6, was 167 errors)
- Follow-ups review: PASS (cache walk 2.06 s -> 0.11 s and formula sort 8.6 s -> 0.75 s on 2,000 row groups; foreign metadata kept over two saves; appended-row edits saved). Save policy (main session, on the reviewer's advice): only a loss of values is refused (TIMESTAMP held as its day: `scan.narrowing(c)`); a type change that keeps every value (DECIMAL(p, s) -> (18, s), ENUM / JSON -> text, narrower or unsigned ints -> int64: `scan.type_change(c)`) is written without asking, since pyarrow / pandas write decimals at the data's precision and refusing them would refuse most foreign decimal files

## F-148 Mojo API: count distinct, percentiles and top-k as aggregates [done]
Needs: F-133 · `mojo/mxframe/holistic.mojo` (new), `query.mojo` (hook, windows, scan, results), `frame.mojo` (`text_ranks`, session words in the overlay), tests `mojo/tests/test_holistic.mojo`, `test_holistic_query.mojo`, `tests/test_mojo_holistic.py` (probe `mojo/tools/holistic_probe.mojo`) · API: `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md` §5 · omxplorer computes these in app code over group-by results today
- [x] `count_distinct(e)`, `percentile(e, p)` / `median(e)`, `top_k(e, k)` per group, over tables, frames and scans (streamed: exact for distinct and top-k by merging per-row-group state; percentiles exact, memory stated); CPU first, GPU where it pays: `r.values(i)` (distinct count, percentile value), `r.present(i, g)`, `r.top(i, g)` (values, largest first); percentile = DuckDB `quantile_disc` (index `max(ceil(p * n) - 1, 0)`); `median` = DuckDB `median` (coordinator's decision after review: mean of the two middle values, exact at the column's scale + 1, text stays the lower middle word; `percentile_cont` is a follow-up); `top_k` = the k largest values with repeats (`ORDER BY e DESC LIMIT k`, DuckDB `max(e, k)`); text orders alphabetically as `permutation` does; nulls ignored. GPU: left out (CPU only; AUTO says why in `fallback_reason`, `compute(GPU)` raises): the kernel's per-group accumulators cannot hold them and moving pairs to the device costs more than the CPU pass
- [x] equal to DuckDB on random data with nulls, many groups (up to 100,000), ties, values near 2^62, decimals, text: 19 cases in `tests/test_mojo_holistic.py` (6 data sets x table / scan / frame-after-300-edits, shapes all / filtered / top_k alone / global), plus Mojo tests against a plain-loop oracle (table in one and in several windows, frame with 500 random edits and a merge then undone, scan of 8 row groups, an all-null row group, words that appear late)
- Algorithms: the kernel's block hook writes each value source's (group, value) pairs, packed, in windows of up to 256 MB; count distinct = hash set of (group, value) in 4 x cores partitions (scatter, then a task per partition, no locks), tables sized from a HyperLogLog sketch, prefetched probes, the first window counted in tables that live one task; percentile = parallel counting sort by group then quickselect (small groups) or parallel byte-wise radix select (groups of 262,144+ values); top-k = bounded min-heaps, fed straight from the hook into per-worker heaps when it is the only aggregate on its values and groups are few (no pairs written)
- Informal timings (20M rows, 12-core Ryzen, load ~3; DuckDB 24 threads in brackets): 8 groups, ~19M distinct: distinct 148 ms [258], median 120 [322], top-10 19 [14]; 1,000 groups: 93 [132], 94 [248], 21 [17]; 1M groups: 372 [617], 398 [2,208], 449 [2,295]; a plain sum + count is 17 / 18 / 108 ms. Scan of 20 row groups: distinct 490 ms and 756 MB peak, median 187 ms and 598 MB, top-k 105 ms and 77 MB (plain sum 107 ms, 77 MB)
- Overlap with F-145: none in `parquet.mojo` (untouched); the scan path is `_compute_scan` in `query.mojo` (its inline partial-merge loop became `_merge_partial`, shared with the windows of a table)
- Limits: percentiles hold every non-null value (about 27 bytes a row at the peak); distinct state about 35 bytes per distinct pair; GPU not used; a top-k of many groups x large k is capped (groups * k <= 268M); `top_k` returns values, not row numbers
- Follow-ups: per-worker hash sets from the hook for distinct with few groups (would skip the pair write); a multi-group radix select for percentiles with a few groups (would skip the counting sort); a GPU radix select for percentiles if a table already lives on the device

- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK (`r.sums` wrong across windows; a huge `top_k` k overflowed the size guards), fixed; median now interpolates as DuckDB's `median` (exact, at the column's scale + 1; owner-side call by the main session, easy to reverse); text order stays case-insensitive (documented against DuckDB's byte order). Round 2: one line, fixed by the main session as the reviewer prescribed: the negative median bound was `Int64.MIN // 5` (floors one step too far) and wrapped to +9.2e18; now `-(Int64.MAX // 5)`, tested red then green on both sides of the limit; the decimal median is compared exactly (DuckDB floors to scale 2)

## F-149 Mojo API: morsel-driven streamed scans (row ranges of row groups, 32k-row blocks) [done]
Needs: F-145 · `parquet.mojo`, `query.mojo` · F-145 review: per-core cost is near DuckDB's (0.72 vs 0.62 s on one thread) but scaling is 4.7x on 12 workers against DuckDB's 8x+, lost to idle tails at the barriers inside each row group. Bar: within 1.5x of DuckDB on the streamed numeric query (today 0.150 vs 0.066-0.073 s)
- [x] part 1: a worker owns a row group and reads it with a `GroupReader` block by block (`SCAN_BLOCK_ROWS` = 32,768 rows, a multiple of 8 for the frame mask): headers walked and dictionaries decoded when the group is opened (`open_plan` -> `GroupPlan`), then each block is decoded into the worker's block table, page after page (a page is mapped, decompressed, its levels unpacked when its first block is wanted, and let go of with its last), the kernel runs on the block on one thread and the block's partial is folded into the morsel's; partials are merged in order. Pages are cut at the block grid with exact entry points (`_plan_dict_cuts`, `_dict_runs(.., skip)`); dictionary-indexed and PLAIN fixed-width pages are cut, a page of another encoding (text, booleans, delta) is decoded whole once and copied block by block
- [x] part 2 (review round 1): the row groups are cut into row-range morsels of whole blocks that share the group's `GroupPlan` (dictionaries decoded once, read only; each morsel has its own copies of the page descriptors, a page spanning a range boundary is decompressed by both ranges), partials merged in (group, range) order. With at least as many groups as cores each group is ONE morsel opened by the worker that runs it (a separate opening round cost more than the second round it removed: row ranges of 20 groups on 12 cores measured 0.117-0.138 s against 0.111-0.120 s for a morsel per group, A/B interleaved); with fewer groups they are opened in parallel (waves of two per core) and cut into ~3 morsels per core. `_lower` per block costs 8 us against 726 us for the kernel on the block (1%), so it is not cached per worker
- [x] options, not environment: `Query.morsels(MORSELS_AUTO | MORSELS_ON | MORSELS_OFF)` and `Query.block_rows(n)`; AUTO uses morsels on more than one core when the file has at least 8 blocks of rows. The library reads no environment variable; only the test tools (`pq_scan_probe`, `pq_decode_bench`) read `MXFRAME_SCAN_MORSELS` / `MXFRAME_MORSEL_ROWS` and pass them as options. The unreadable-page fallback (`MORSEL_UNSUPPORTED`) was dead and is gone. A plan with holistic aggregates (F-148) keeps the group-at-a-time loop; F-147's frame mask is sliced per morsel (`_range_mask`) and applied per block, tested by `test_scan_frame` in both modes
- [x] errors in order: a kernel error inside a morsel no longer stops its decoding (the remaining blocks are decoded, not aggregated), so a decode error later in the same range is raised, as the group path raises it; per row group a decode error comes before a kernel error, an earlier group's error before a later group's, an overflow while merging a group before the next group's error (`test_a_decode_error_later_in_a_morsel_beats_an_earlier_blocks_kernel_error`, with a corrupt bit width after an overflowing block)
- [x] memory, sampled from /proc (RssAnon + RssFile/Shmem), 12 workers: 20-group 346 MB file: morsels 168 MB peak (125 anonymous + 44 mapped file pages), against 126 MB (102 + 24) on the group-at-a-time path, so morsels use MORE memory there, not less; the 4-group file (5M-row groups): morsels 159 MB, the group path 457 MB. The process floor is 39 MB; ~8 MB a worker (two dictionaries of ~1 MB, the decompressed pages, the block buffers). A reused decompression buffer per worker was not tried
- [x] exact and measured: all 165 tests of `tests/test_mojo_parquet.py` + `tests/test_mojo_holistic.py` pass in the default mode and with every scan forced onto morsels with 2,000-row blocks; `mojo-test` (incl. `test_scan` under forced morsels, `test_scan_frame`, the holistic suites) and `check_mac_link.sh` green; new tests: morsels vs `read_parquet` and DuckDB on pyarrow files with blocks of 1,000 / 3,001 rows, pages cut inside long runs and over nulls, 30 / 3 / 1 row groups. Timings (20M-row numeric file, snappy, streamed query; load average 6.5-7, DuckDB measured in the same minute): 20 row groups ALL cores: ours 0.116 s best / 0.126 median, DuckDB 0.082 / 0.090 (1.41x / 1.40x, bar MET at the median; group path 0.182 / 0.190); 4 row groups: ours 0.110 / 0.127, DuckDB 0.169 / 0.171 (0.65x / 0.74x); one thread: ours 0.67-0.68 s, DuckDB 0.63-0.68 s
- Left: decompressing into one reused buffer per worker (anonymous memory); per-worker holistic state; the 20-group file is still two rounds on 12 cores, and finer ranges did not beat it here
- Review round 2: PASS (144 range-boundary cases cell for cell; Frame over a scan equal to Frame over read_parquet with groups starting mid-byte and edits at every block edge; 570 query runs equal the pre-F-149 build; error order identical in every mode; 2,500 byte flips). At load 9-10: 20 groups 1.31x DuckDB (0.129 vs 0.098 s), 4 groups 0.81x (faster; peak 143 MB vs 448 MB on the group path). Follow-ups (with F-146): a fixed pyarrow-written test of 3 x 13,337-row groups with edits at block edges under MORSELS_ON (this 12-core box never takes the row-range path in test_scan_frame); `GroupReader` / `open_plan` underscored or holding the plan by reference. Bench note: pyarrow caps row groups at ~1M rows unless `row_group_size` is passed

## F-133 M2: Query on a Mojo plan IR [done]
Needs: F-132 · Spec §5 · `mojo/mxframe/expr.mojo`, `query.mojo`, test `mojo/tests/test_query.mojo`
- [x] plan nodes in Mojo (Scan, Filter, Aggregate built; Project / Sort / Limit / Join kinds reserved), built by `Query(table).where().group_by(key, groups=).agg(...)`; expressions are flat post-order trees (`col`, `lit`, `+ - * // %`, unary `-`, `least` / `greatest`, comparisons, `between`, `& | ~`)
- [x] lowered to one program for the CPU pipeline kernel, called directly (no Python): every value an exact int64 register, integer constants as int64 bits in the constant pool (exact past 2^53), filters ANDed into the predicate, the dense group key computed per block (register 0, the kernel's `keyed` path); a key outside 0..groups-1 or null is left out (the stand-in's rule); sum, count(), count(e), min_of, max_of, mean; nulls: a filter reading a null cell is false, an aggregate skips rows whose value reads a null (value x validity, from the kernel's `ISNULL` on the Arrow bitmap); a register allocator keeps final values and frees temporaries per expression (48 registers)
- [x] kernel: new exact integer opcodes 42-46 in `pipeline_cpu.mojo` (`IDIV` floor, `IMOD`, `IMIN`, `IMAX`, `ICMP`), x // 0 = x % 0 = 0 as the app's formulas, INT64_MIN // -1 flags overflow; the Python compiler never emits them (full Python suite 23,263 passed after the rebuild)
- [x] equal to an independent plain-loop oracle on 300k-row random tables, with and without nulls: the app's five shapes (chart key, pivot `region * 7 + carrier`, bin `least(day // 7, 51)`, filtered report tile and chart), mean and a formula value, an overflow and an unknown column raise readable errors (DuckDB is not reachable from Mojo; F-139's Python bridge can add it)
- Review: NEEDS WORK, fixed: (1) `group_by().where().agg()` dropped the grouping: `agg` attaches to the query's one aggregate wherever filters are, a second `group_by` raises; (2) expressions are typed: a number where a comparison is due (or the reverse) raises, and + - least greatest and comparisons refuse columns of different decimal scales (decimal-aware formulas are F-136; values stay the stored integers); (3) tests for min / max / mean over nullable columns, an all-null group (`present(i, g)`; min / max now carry a non-null count), `//` and `%` with negative and zero divisors checked row by row against Mojo's Int64, INT64_MIN // -1 raising and % -1 = 0, `|` `~` `!=`, unary minus, least / greatest, filtered pivot and bin; (5) the key's range is checked per selected row in the kernel's block hook (out of range -> -1, which every accumulator skips), so a key formula is never evaluated on a row the filters left out; (6) the `|` null rule is documented and tested (SQL would keep a row whose other side holds); (8) opcodes 42-46 reserved in `pipeline_program.py`
- Review round 2: PASS. Small follow-ups taken: `present` for a sum over only nulls (every aggregate over a nullable value carries its non-null count), "group_by must come before agg", per-group counts checked after `where`, keys past int32 / negative / INT64_MIN tested. Known trade-off: a row whose key is out of range still has its value formula evaluated (the range is checked per block after the body), so a value that overflows only there raises; the filters' rows are never evaluated
- Limit: the kernel cannot tell a running total that passes int64 from a final one, so a sum whose partial totals pass int64 raises even if the final total would fit (Python's path has an exact fallback; the Mojo API does not yet)

## F-134 M3: CSV reader [done]
Needs: F-132 · Spec §2 · `mojo/mxframe/csv.mojo`, tests `mojo/tests/test_csv.mojo` (run by `pixi run mojo-test`) and `tests/test_mojo_csv.py` (pyarrow oracle through `mojo/tools/csv_dump.mojo`)
- [x] type inference (INT, DECIMAL scale 2 / 4, DATE, BOOL, TEXT), quoting, embedded newlines, BOM, CRLF, TSV (by `.tsv` / `.tab` or `CsvOptions(delimiter=)`); decimals parsed from the digits (exact, no float; extra digits round half away from zero); empty cell = null; after the `infer_rows` window a value that does not fit becomes a null plus a failed row ("row 41,207: `12.x` isn't a number")
- [x] import report per column: `empty`, `distinct` (text), `sample` (first 8 raw values), `failed` (row, text, reason; at most 1000, `failed_count` is the true number); also `skipped` (columns past `max_columns`) and `ragged` (rows with more cells than the header: the extra cells are dropped and the row is reported)
- [x] multi-threaded, memory-mapped (no copy of the file): a 4-state quote pre-scan (each chunk run from every start state, then composed) gives every chunk its exact starting quote state, so chunks start on true row boundaries even inside quoted multi-line fields (no fallback needed); no String per cell (numbers parsed from bytes, text through a byte-keyed dictionary). Equal to the single-threaded parse on a 300k-row generated file and on files cut into 7-byte chunks; equal to pyarrow on awkward files (the empty text cell is a null here, "" in pyarrow)
- Measured, informally (24-core machine, not a quiet-machine benchmark): a generated 1M-row, 6-column, 38 MB file reads in about 0.065 s on all cores (about 15M rows/s) and 0.23 s on one thread (about 4M rows/s); the spec's target was 0.5 s
- Review: NEEDS WORK, fixed: `max_columns` no longer reports every row as ragged (compared with the full header count); a quote not at the start of a field is a literal character (as pyarrow), with the parallel pre-scan running the same 4-state machine from every start state and composing, so chunk starts stay exact; an unclosed quote is reported in `ragged` ("has an unmatched quote: the rest of the file was read as one cell"); timestamps with a zone offset convert to UTC then floor to the day; a scale-2 DECIMAL widens to scale 4 when later values have 3-4 places (earlier values x100, overflow = failed cell) and values with more than 4 places are counted in `rounded`; window values that overflow int64 at the chosen scale make the column TEXT. Tests added in `test_csv.mojo` and the pyarrow oracle.
- Review round 2: PASS (9,600 random one-thread vs 3-thread comparisons, 0 mismatches). Nits taken: failed cells found while widening a column are kept in row order; a widened INT64_MIN prints with one minus sign. Left: a leap second (`23:59:60Z`) floors into the next day; the 0.065 s / 0.23 s timings predate the 4-state pre-scan (an informal re-run on a loaded machine was 10-30% slower on all cores), re-time on a quiet machine before quoting
- For the switch-over (M7): the stand-in imports true/false as INT 0/1 (here BOOL; a 0/1 column stays INT). It reads empty cells as 0 or "" (here null, a quoted "" too). Its scale covers the whole column (now the same, via the widening). Its timestamps are UTC (now the same).
- Limits: a file truncated while mapped can SIGBUS; the delimiter is chosen by extension only; leading zeros are lost; no exponent numbers (`1e5` is TEXT), no thousands separators, a lone `\r` is not a line end, UTF-8 only (not validated); the chunks' columns are held next to the final columns while stitching (about 2x the table's size for a moment), so it is parallel but not constant-memory streaming; a high-cardinality text column is stitched serially (one String per distinct word per chunk)

## F-135 M4: Parquet read and write [done]
Needs: F-131, F-132 · Spec §2 · `mojo/mxframe/thrift.mojo`, `parquet.mojo`, tools `mojo/tools/pq_write_sample.mojo`, `pq_dump.mojo`, test `tests/test_mojo_parquet.py`
- [x] Thrift compact protocol codec (writer and a reader that skips unknown fields), the Parquet footer and page headers
- [x] write: INT -> INT64, DECIMAL -> INT64 DECIMAL(18, scale), DATE -> INT32 DATE, BOOL -> BOOLEAN, TEXT -> BYTE_ARRAY STRING with a dictionary page and RLE_DICTIONARY indices (the table's codes, no remap); a column with nulls is OPTIONAL and its definition levels are the validity bitmap's bits; zstd (default), snappy, none; row groups of a multiple of 8 rows; the "omx" key-value metadata. pyarrow and DuckDB read every value, type, null and the metadata back exactly (3 codecs x 0 / 1 / 1,000 / 5,000 rows, 1 and several row groups)
- [x] read: data pages v1 and v2, PLAIN, PLAIN_DICTIONARY / RLE_DICTIONARY, DELTA_BINARY_PACKED (INT32 wraps modulo 2^32), RLE booleans; zstd, snappy, none; INT32 / INT64 / BOOLEAN / BYTE_ARRAY, DECIMAL on INT32 / INT64 / FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY (precision <= 18), DATE, TIMESTAMP ms / us / ns -> its day (floored, the CSV rule); OPTIONAL and REQUIRED; a dictionary page's words become table codes once per page. Equal to pyarrow's reading on files pyarrow wrote with 8 option sets (codecs, dictionary on / off, page v1 / v2, delta ints, small row groups and pages) and on a DuckDB file; our own files round-trip with their metadata
- [x] unsupported input raises a readable error naming the column: DOUBLE / FLOAT / INT96 ("MX frame has no kind for it yet"), nested columns, DECIMAL past 18 digits, other encodings, corrupt offsets
- Review: NEEDS WORK, fixed: unsigned columns (INTEGER logical type and UINT_8..64 / INT_8..64: UINT_32 zero-extended, UINT_64 past int64 refused naming the column); corrupt files raise "(corrupt file)" and never crash (page sizes and UNCOMPRESSED csize == usize, thrift lengths / skips / list sizes / nesting depth, FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY and bit-pack byte counts checked before multiplying, RLE / bit-packed / DELTA runs cut at the values still needed, v1 and v2 level lengths, bit widths over 64, physical type names); writer refuses DECIMAL past 18 digits and zero-column tables, pages past int32, writes `path.tmp` then renames; BINARY, TIME, DECIMAL on BYTE_ARRAY and invalid UTF-8 raise readably; v1 BIT_PACKED definition levels read; `total_byte_size` uncompressed, `created_by` "MX frame version 0.6.0"; timestamps keep their UTC day (documented). 65 tests in tests/test_mojo_parquet.py (22 corrupt-file cases, 150 random byte flips, every shape the reviewer listed). Known limit: a page may claim up to 2^31-1 values, so an RLE-bombed file can still ask for a large level list.
- [ ] open: the whole file is read into memory (no streaming or mmap); gzip / brotli / lz4 not linked; floats have no MX frame kind (spec §1 lists none); per-value appends (`push`) are not the fastest path for 100M rows
- Review round 2: PASS (840 random byte flips over 7 files, never a signal). Nits taken: metadata strings (column names, the "omx" value) pass the same UTF-8 check as text values; the temp file carries the process id and is removed when a write fails. Left: a page may claim up to 2^31-1 values, so a crafted RLE file can still ask for a ~16 GB level list (memory, not a read past the buffer)

## F-136 M5: edits, formulas, sort [done]
Needs: F-133 · Spec §3, §4, §6 · `mojo/mxframe/frame.mojo`, `query.mojo` (Query over a Frame), test `mojo/tests/test_frame.mojo`
- [x] `Frame(shared)` overlay with no copy of the shared data: sparse cell edits (`set`, `set_null`, `set_text` parsed by kind with the CSV parsers, values checked: TEXT codes, BOOL 0 / 1), appended rows (`append(values)`, typed text), tombstones (`remove`, `alive`), session words after the shared dictionary, `merge(c, codes, into)` (one step deep, the stand-in's REPOINT bookkeeping), one undo log (a merge undoes as one step), `edits` counter, `changed_rows`, `bake()` (a plain Table: edits, merges, words applied, removed rows gone)
- [x] formulas `f.formula("margin = revenue - cost")` with the app's grammar and decimal rules (+ - % min max align scales, * adds scales then floors back to 2 places, / pre-scales to 2 places, // whole), formulas over formulas, cycles and errors raise readably; `formula_text`, `depends`, `dependants`, `get`, `program` (formulas inlined, merges applied)
- [x] queries over a Frame (`Query(f)`): the query copies the frame's overlay when built (formulas, merges, a bitmap of changed shared rows, the changed / appended rows' values: no pointer to the frame); formulas are inlined into the kernel program; a merged text column is remapped in exact integer arithmetic (`c + int(c == a) * (t - a)`, new internal node `N_BOOL2INT`), so keys, pivots and filters all see merges; the kernel skips the changed shared rows (the bitmap through `OP_ISNULL`) and a CPU pass adds the changed and appended rows with the kernel's rules; every aggregate carries a non-null count on a frame
- [x] `permutation(c, descending)`: stable, nulls last, text alphabetical ignoring ASCII case (byte order breaks ties; owner confirmed 2026-10-18, everywhere including SQL: "apple" before "Zebra") over shared and session words with merges applied, formulas evaluated, removed rows left out
- [x] tests: 400 random edits (sets, nulls, new and old words, appends, removes, a merge) on 50k rows: three queries over the frame (grouped by text with sum / count / count(e) / min / max, a filtered pivot with mean, a filtered global) equal the same queries over `bake()`, and differ from the shared table; undoing everything returns the shared results exactly; formula values for each decimal rule; formula errors and a cycle; sort order with a new word, a null, ties, a removed row; edit value checks
- [ ] open: sort is a merge sort over per-row values (O(n log n), not the stand-in's radix: 100M rows will be slow); `get` / `evaluate` on a formula expands it per call; formulas are not checked for decimal overflow beyond the int64 checks; the frame mask is rebuilt per query (O(rows / 8))
- Review: NEEDS WORK, fixed: aligned-scale formulas are queryable (an internal N_SCALE_TO node carries the target scale); `_compile_expr` frees operand registers and writes into the first operand's (dest == source, safe on CPU and GPU), so merges of any size compile; a refused redefinition rolls back; dependants are re-parsed in order on redefinition; `parse` refuses the reserved null value; text sort ignores case; tests for queries over formulas and merges vs `bake()`, edited-only groups, append-only frames, `present()` after edits
- Review round 2: PASS (a 5,000-row probe with edits, nulls, appends and removes: kernel = row-by-row `get` for seven nested formulas; 19 aliases compile)

## F-137 M6: GPU [done]
Needs: F-133, F-136 · Spec §7 · `mojo/mxframe/gpu.mojo` (`DeviceTable`, `gpu_available`), `query.mojo` (`Query.on_device`, `compute(GPU / AUTO)`), kernel `kernels_aot/pipeline_gpu.mojo`, test `mojo/tests/test_gpu.mojo` (skips cleanly with no GPU)
- [x] device copy uploaded once per process and shared, int64 values (nothing is narrowed: values past int32 and past 2^53 tested), validity bitmaps beside the columns, `release()` frees it for every handle copy (tested through the device's free memory); `DeviceTable(table)` makes its own context on device 0, `DeviceTable(table, ctx)` uses the app's `DeviceContext`: buffers are allocated on it and the query kernels are enqueued on the same context (`run_pipeline_gpu(ctx, ...)`), so the app's own buffers and kernels share the device (test: the app's buffer is intact beside the table and queries on it are right)
- [x] GPU interpreter: opcodes 41 ISNULL (device bitmap through the same 3-entry column table), 42 IDIV, 43 IMOD, 44 IMIN, 45 IMAX, 46 ICMP with the CPU's semantics (floor, Mojo `%`, x // 0 = x % 0 = 0, x % -1 = 0, INT64_MIN // -1 sets the overflow flag); keyed mode (`gids_a == -1`): the group id is register 0 of the body, outside 0 .. groups-1 leaves the row out, no host round trip; every op reads its operands before writing, so a destination equal to a source is safe. Programs with many registers (a pivot with 10 aggregates needs 23) run in 128-thread blocks when 256 do not fit shared memory (`narrow_ok`, asked only by the Mojo API). Python path unchanged: its GPU tests (165 passed, 4 skipped) and `tests/test_kernel_hashes.py` pass with the rebuilt `libmxpipeline_gpu.so`
- [x] `Query.compute(device=GPU)` equals the CPU path exactly (slots, counts, sums, `present`): chart / pivot / bin / filtered tile / filtered chart, pivot, bin, global, 2000 groups, with and without nulls; min / max / mean / count(e); negative // and %; keys past int32; 2.5M rows through the global, shared-table and atomic modes; overflow raises the same message; INT64_MIN // -1 raises; a frame with 300 random edits, a merge and appended / removed rows, with no re-upload (the frame's changed-rows mask, rows / 8 bytes, goes up per query; the CPU adds the delta as before)
- [x] `AUTO`: the GPU when a live DeviceTable is attached and the table has at least `AUTO_GPU_MIN_ROWS` (1,000,000) rows, else the CPU; a released table or a program the kernel cannot take falls back to the CPU on AUTO and raises on GPU. `QueryResult.device` says where it ran
- How a query reaches the DeviceTable: `Query(...).on_device(dt)` (refused when `dt` was made from another table). Chosen over a process-wide registry because Mojo has no safe mutable globals and the app owns the one DeviceTable next to its SharedTable
- Informal timing (shared machine, load ~7, RTX 3090 GPU 0, 100M-row INT column, device copy already uploaded; upload 0.4 s): `sum` CPU 30 ms vs GPU 2.2 ms; grouped by 8 keys (sum + count) CPU 57-62 ms vs GPU 5.7 ms; results equal. The spec's stand-in figure was 19 ms at 100M
- Review round 1 fixes: scratch sized to the mode (only the small-group modes use it; 3M groups on AUTO run on the GPU), any device failure on AUTO falls back to the CPU (GPU raises), a 0-row DeviceTable no longer copies from a null pointer and `compute(GPU)` on 0 rows runs on the GPU, narrow-launch (30 aggregates) and cannot-take (44 aggregates) tests, no-re-upload proven by `uploads()` and unchanged buffer addresses, `DeviceTable` holds its table's handle so `mirrors` cannot match a reused address (it keeps the host columns alive while it lives), `release()` documented as not for use while a query runs
- [ ] open: the register file lives in shared memory, so a program needing more than about 45 registers at 128 threads (or many groups with many aggregates) returns "needs more GPU shared memory" (AUTO falls back to the CPU); the whole table must fit on the device (no chunking yet); a sum whose partial totals pass int64 raises on the GPU as on the CPU, but the order of partials differs, so such borderline inputs may raise on one and not the other; the changed-rows mask is uploaded per query (rows / 8 bytes, not cached per edit count)
- Review round 2: PASS (3M groups on AUTO runs on the GPU and equals the CPU; a 0-row table works; 40 / 44 aggregates fall back on AUTO). Follow-ups moved to F-138b: the fallback test fills GPU 0 (cap it or make it opt-in); AUTO's catch-all fallback could hide a kernel bug (narrow it to allocation failures or record why it fell back); `uploads()` adds nothing over the address check

## Ready for later: the queue (owner, 2026-10-20: stop at a good point, keep every ticket ready)
Resume in this order when the owner says go. Each ticket below has its full entry further down.
1. **Finish the app's showcase needs:** F-175 and F-176 are done (2026-10-20); next F-176 follow-ups (formula columns in range / block reads, cached frame patches), F-177 stddev / quantile bins / ISO week, F-179 undo for formulas, F-180 compressed Parquet everywhere, F-178 streamed CSV.
2. **M8 to its gate:** F-158 joins over streamed files (WIP on branch `worktree-agent-a27f985ecf9ff3560` at 4a9c93f; first bisect a Mojo compiler crash), F-159 CTEs / correlated subqueries / UNION ALL, F-160 named views + result cache, F-161 join speed (star-aware planner, filters inside joins, Python on the shared kernel), F-162 gate: TPC-H 22 through `mx` equal to DuckDB.
3. **Scoreboard and gaps:** F-166 quiet-machine scoreboard vs DuckDB / Polars (needs the owner's go), F-167 sort / large outputs / parallel text building / spill, F-168 window functions.
4. **Release:** F-165 rename to omxcore + 0.7.0 (PyPI needs the owner's yes), F-169 MCP server for `mx`.
5. **Agent plugins (M10):** F-170 vector column + top-k kernel, F-171 vector search, F-172 agent search, F-173 UDFs + classification API, F-174 agent memory.
6. **Later:** F-163 joins bigger than RAM, F-164 Arrow output from `mx`, F-139 Arrow C Data Interface, M11 Linux / Omarchy fast path, M9 Delta-Lake persistence (parked).
App requests are GitHub issues on this repo labelled `omxplorer` (#6-#18), each mapped to a ticket above; apps take versions from `STABLE`.

## Core vision (owner, 2026-10-19): the engine is the kernel; apps and Python are plugins
MX frame's Mojo engine is the nucleus (a Linux kernel / SQLite for many apps: data apps like omxplorer, simulations, rendering): it must work and beat DuckDB and Polars on CPU and GPU, and get faster every release. Python is a plugin (the hook into its ecosystem); omxplorer is one app among many. Rules: every ticket states its core value (speed, correctness, generality) first; an app's request becomes a generic core primitive plus thin glue outside the core (a plugin module or the app's repo); no app concept, file format or workflow in the core; the core API stays stable and app-agnostic. Re-cut of M8 below: F-153..F-159, F-161, F-162 are core (SQL engine, joins, planner, speed, the DuckDB gate); F-160's Explore steps move to a plugin layer over two generic core primitives (named views, a versioned result cache); after M8 the next core work is the scoreboard: the Mojo engine against DuckDB / Polars on CPU and GPU (benchmarks wait for the owner's go on a quiet machine).

## M8: SQL and joins in the Mojo API (brief `docs/briefs/mojo-api.md` M8)
Design (2026-10-18, Plan agent, read-only survey of query.mojo, the kernels the package carries, sql_frontend.py, join_engine.py, grace.py / spill.py, docs/design/partitioned-hash-join.md and omxplorer's needs-mxframe.md):
- SQL text and the Query builder both lower to one logical plan (the PlanNode list, extended: source, right, how, keys, names, sort, limit, offset, distinct; groups=0 = unknown) → rule rewrites (folding, pushdown, pruning, OR common factor, IN / EXISTS → semi / anti, CTEs, decorrelation) → a physical planner behind a `Planner` trait that reads only `Stats` and records every decision with its reason; `PlannerOptions` forces each choice, so tests reach every path and assert which ran → an executor of Sources (Table, Frame mask + delta, ParquetScan morsels) × front operators (fused join probe) × Sinks (dense / hashed / holistic aggregate, rows, top-k, join build, spill)
- every SQL result is an owned `Table` (`SqlResult`): HAVING, CTEs, Explore step caches and a workbook's second table compose from it; the builder keeps its documented rules (`QueryResult` unchanged, gains `to_table()`)
- SQL gets its own lowering mode: three-valued logic, a NULL group, only non-empty groups, unknown key ranges, SUM / MIN / MAX / AVG of nothing is NULL
- one hash-join kernel shared with the Python engine (`kernels_aot/hashjoin_cpu.mojo`, closing F-046a slice 1); fused FK→PK probes in `pipeline_fused`'s front stage (no joined rows written), materialized pairs otherwise
- risks: the SQL parser / binder is the largest new code (a DuckDB subset; every unsupported form raises by name); 48 registers / 64 constants per program (IN lists through OP_IN_SET; past the registers a readable error, never a wrong answer); m:n expansion inside the fused stage; package compile time (split modules)
Order: F-150 → F-151 → F-152 → F-153 → F-154 → F-155 (F-156 / F-157 in parallel after F-150) → F-158 → F-159 → F-160 → F-161 → F-162; F-163 / F-164 wait for the owner.
Owner decisions (2026-10-18): (1) text order is case-insensitive EVERYWHERE, SQL included (ORDER BY, MIN / MAX, percentiles; byte order breaks ties), as the builder and Frame.permutation already do; the DuckDB oracle compares under NOCASE / lower(); this also settles the F-136 "pending owner review" sort call; (2) FLOAT64 is a column kind in M8 (F-157, no longer an owner decision); (3) Grace spill (F-163) deferred: an oversized build side raises a readable budget error before allocating; (4) defaults accepted: SQL NULL rules while the builder keeps its own; SUM past int64 raises (no int128 yet); one join kernel shared with the Python engine; `mx` Arrow output later (F-164). Oracle: DuckDB on data it generates (DECIMAL / DATE TPC-H via `dbgen`, random tables with nulls, text, mixed-scale decimals), each SQL string through `mx` (from F-151) and DuckDB; every test asserts the path that ran. Sizes: S ≤ 2 days, M 3-5, L 1-2 weeks.

## F-150 M8: SQL aggregate queries over one table (SELECT … WHERE … GROUP BY … HAVING … ORDER BY … LIMIT), results as a Table, EXPLAIN [done]
Needs: F-133, F-148 · Size: L · new `mojo/mxframe/sql.mojo` (lexer, parser, AST), `bind.mojo` (Catalog, binder, typed expressions), `plan.mojo`, `planner.mojo` (`Planner` trait, `RulePlanner`, `Stats`, `PlannerOptions`), `query.mojo` (SQL lowering mode) · omxplorer Query mode (SQL cells, EXPLAIN, HAVING)
- [x] `sql(catalog, text) -> SqlResult` (`table`, `explain`, `ms`, `device`, `fallback_reason`, `notes`) over one table / Frame (overlay snapshot, version = `edits`) / ParquetScan registered by name; DuckDB dialect subset: SELECT list with aliases, WHERE (= != < <= > >= BETWEEN, AND / OR / NOT, IN lists, IS [NOT] NULL, text and DATE literals, decimal literals at the column's scale), GROUP BY columns / expressions / ordinals / aliases, HAVING, ORDER BY (keys, aggregates, aliases, ordinals; ASC / DESC; NULLS FIRST / LAST, NULLS LAST by default), LIMIT / OFFSET; SUM, COUNT(*), COUNT(x), COUNT(DISTINCT x), MIN, MAX, AVG, MEDIAN, QUANTILE_DISC; + - * // % on INT / DECIMAL (scales aligned as DuckDB). Anything else raises naming the construct and its line / column
- [x] SQL semantics, distinct from the builder's (unchanged): three-valued logic in WHERE / HAVING; NULL is a group; only groups with rows; SUM / MIN / MAX / AVG of no non-null value is NULL; a global aggregate over no rows returns one row; a word absent from the dictionary matches nothing; a sum past int64 raises (documented difference from DuckDB's HUGEINT)
- [x] group strategy chosen by the planner and shown in EXPLAIN: dense (word counts, BOOL, or a min / max pass cached per table version; mixed-radix composite with a NULL slot per nullable key) up to a bound, else hashed (`hashagg_cpu.hash_aggregate`; holistic over hashed keys in two passes); `PlannerOptions` forces either, tests run both
- [x] AVG and `/` results are FLOAT (result-only kind until F-157), AVG correctly rounded from the exact sum and count; HAVING and post-aggregate expressions run as a Query over the result table (closed by F-152: they run as a row query over the groups on the executor, `post.mojo`; this was done except the last clause: they run in the column evaluator `sqleval.mojo`, not as a `Query`, because the builder has no row output until F-152; F-152 moves them onto its executor)
- [x] `EXPLAIN <query>`: logical plan after rewrites and the physical plan with each decision, its reason and its numbers
- [x] equal to DuckDB on generated tables with nulls, mixed-scale decimals, dates, 8 / 10k / 1M groups, through a table, a Frame after 300 edits and a 13-row-group scan; ORDER BY without ties compared in order, else as sets; error messages for 20 unsupported or misspelled queries tested whole

Built (2026-10-18, branch worktree-agent-a27f985ecf9ff3560): `sql.mojo` (lexer, parser), `bind.mojo` (Catalog, typed tree, binder), `planner.mojo` (`Planner` trait, `RulePlanner`, `Stats`, `PlannerOptions`, interval arithmetic for expression keys), `sqlprog.mojo` (SQL lowering mode: Kleene logic as 1.0 / 0.0 / 0.5 registers, NULL flags, truncating `//` and `%`, NULL slot in dense keys, key words for hashed), `sqleval.mojo` (HAVING / ORDER BY / select expressions over the groups; AVG as DuckDB's long-double division, reproduced), `sqlexec.mojo` (chunks: table, frame = mask + changed-rows table, scan = row groups; partial results; hashed merge; two-pass holistic over hashed groups; top-N heap / radix ORDER BY; result Table). Not `plan.mojo`: the logical plan is rendered in `sqlexec.mojo` (there are no rewrite rules yet beyond constant folding at bind). `query.mojo`: only `Program.set_bytes / set_meta` (the IN sets), additive.
- Differences from the ticket text: HAVING and post-aggregate expressions run in a small column-at-a-time evaluator over the groups (`sqleval.mojo`), not as a `Query` (the builder has no row output until F-152); `FLOAT` is `ColumnKind(5)` in table.mojo (`write_parquet` refuses it); a frame's formula columns are columns of its source, with the builder's rules (floor `//`, `x // 0` is 0); an IN list of numbers holds at most 40 (text lists any size); GPU is not used (`device` is always CPU).
- Also done, because the SQL path needed them: Parquet footer statistics (`ParquetScan.column_range`, `null_count`: INT32 / INT64 / short decimal / DATE) so a scan can use dense groups and skip NULL machinery; `kernels_aot/hashagg_cpu.mojo` starts the pool through `_fpenv.start_pool()` (it called the AsyncRT symbol directly, which the Apple link refuses; `libmxexec_cpu.so` rebuilt); `scripts/check_mac_link.sh` allows `_memset_pattern16` (libSystem; the app's `libSystem.tbd` and `ALLOWED` need it, F-138c) and the link probe runs SQL.
- Tests: `tests/test_mojo_sql.py` (443 tests: ~45 queries x table / frame (300 edits + a merge) / 13-row-group scan x 8 / 10k / ~1M groups against DuckDB with the planner's notes asserted; forced dense / hashed in all four sources; 37 error messages whole; EXPLAIN; empty, NULL-group, overflow, text-order cases; 240 seeded random queries; a scan's statistics plan like the table), `mojo/tests/test_sql.mojo`, `mojo/tests/test_sql_parse.mojo`. Informal timings: `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md` section 8.
- Known limits for later: a scan with very many groups merges one partial per row group (F-158); a program is limited to 48 registers and 64 constants (said in words); AVG whose exact sum passes int64 raises like SUM; decimal `*` past int64 raises where DuckDB widens.
- Review fixes (2026-10-18): a Parquet footer's min / max and null counts are verified while the row groups are read (a null count that differs from the decoded group's; an extra group catches rows outside a stated range) and a query that finds a lie runs again without the statistics, which `notes` says (`statistics: ...`); expression nesting is limited to 256 levels with a positioned error (parentheses, NOT, minus, and chains of + / AND / OR count by depth); `x / 0` is IEEE like DuckDB (inf, -inf, NaN; NaN is one value that sorts last and equals itself), `//` and `%` by zero stay NULL; `median(TEXT)` is the lower middle word, `quantile_disc` takes DATE and TEXT, `median(DATE)` raises (DuckDB gives a TIMESTAMP, a kind MX frame does not have: F-157); the date error names the missing day; `test_switches` runs SQL (dense, hashed, two-pass holistic). Not matched: DuckDB converts a negative HUGEINT `SUM` of BIGINTs past 2^53 to a double a few units off the correctly rounded value (6 of 41 sums in the review's probe; `sum(v)::double` itself differs from Python's `float(sum)`, literals and HUGEINT columns do not), so `sum(d) / sum(v)` can differ by an ulp there; MX frame converts the exact int64 total.
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK (a lying Parquet footer gave silent wrong answers on scans; ~350 nested parentheses crashed; `x / 0` was NULL), fixed: footer null counts checked per row group and an overflow group catches keys outside the stated range, the query re-runs without statistics (`statistics:` note); expression depth capped at 256 with a positioned error; IEEE `/` as DuckDB. Round 2: PASS (1,104 adversarial runs equal DuckDB but three deliberate refusals; 122 lying-footer cases; 2,183 fuzz cases, no crash or hang). Open: HAVING / post-aggregate expressions run in `sqleval` (moves onto F-152's executor); a ~255-term OR chain now raises (use IN); DECIMAL / INT can differ from DuckDB by one ulp where DuckDB's `sum(v)::double` is not correctly rounded (negative sums past 2^53: theirs, not ours)

## F-152 M8: SQL row queries: projections, SELECT *, DISTINCT, ORDER BY and top-k, OFFSET (Source x Sink executor) [done]
Needs: F-150 · Size: L · `exec.mojo` (new), `query.mojo` (reorganized as Source x Sink without behaviour change), `frame.mojo` (multi-key radix)
- [ ] first step (F-150 review): the logical plan as a real IR (`plan.mojo`, the PlanNode list of the M8 design) between the binder and the planner; `sqlexec.mojo` lowers from it, `sqleval` folded into the shared expression path, so F-153's second table plugs in without growing sqlexec (NOT DONE, review round 1: plan.mojo builds the IR and renders EXPLAIN and sets two flags, but sqlexec, rows and post still execute from BoundQuery, which has one source. Making the executor walk PlanNodes, PlanNode gaining a right input, is F-153's first step, as HAVING was F-150's open line)
- [x] executor split into Sources and Sinks; every existing Mojo test and the pyarrow / DuckDB suites unchanged
- [x] materialize sink: the kernel's block hook writes selected rows and computed registers (the `_mat_hook` pattern), compacted by a prefix sum into an output Table; TEXT keeps source words (compact result dictionary)
- [x] ORDER BY on several keys (stable LSD radix; text by the decided SQL order), NULLS FIRST / LAST; ORDER BY + LIMIT k (+ OFFSET) as per-worker heaps in the hook; LIMIT alone stops a scan early; SELECT DISTINCT as a group-by on all selected columns
- [x] equal to DuckDB: `SELECT *`, expressions, WHERE + ORDER BY + LIMIT over 1M rows with ties, DISTINCT over text + int + null, OFFSET past the end, on table / frame / scan; informal timings vs DuckDB

Built (2026-10-18/19, branch worktree-agent-a27f985ecf9ff3560): `plan.mojo` (the logical plan IR: PlanNode list Scan, Filter, Aggregate, Filter (HAVING), Project, Sort, Limit, built from the BoundQuery, rewrites mark a top-k and an early stop; EXPLAIN's logical plan is rendered from it), `exec.mojo` (the Sources: table / frame mask + delta / scan row groups, the footer check, statistics and the shared finishing helpers, moved out of `sqlexec.mojo`), `rows.mojo` (the row Sinks: materialize through the kernel's block hook with a prefix-sum compaction, plain columns gathered by row number, top-k heaps per worker inside the hook with a first-key prefilter, merged across chunks), `post.mojo` (HAVING, the select list, ORDER BY and LIMIT after aggregation as a row query over the groups' table, by the same executor), `sqlprog.lower_rows` and float registers in the emitter (`/`, `+ - *` and comparisons of FLOATs with DuckDB's NaN order), `planner.plan_sinks`; `sql.mojo` parses `*`, `t.*`, `DISTINCT` (`DISTINCT ON` raises); `bind.mojo` binds row queries (`routs`, `rorder`) and rewrites `SELECT DISTINCT` as the group-by of the select list. `sqleval.mojo` lost its evaluator (it keeps `ValCol`, `float_bits`, `ld_div`).
- Differences from the ticket text: `query.mojo` is not reorganized as Source x Sink (the builder's code is untouched; the SQL path has the split in exec / sqlexec / rows) and `frame.mojo` is untouched (the multi-key stable radix is `_order_rows` over `frame._radix_order`, as F-150 built it); a plain column of the select list is gathered from the chunk by the selected rows' numbers instead of travelling through registers (so `SELECT *` over 30 columns needs none); the top-k heaps are used when `limit + offset <= 4,096` and the source has more than 8x that many rows (otherwise a full sort, said in the notes); `SELECT DISTINCT` together with aggregates or GROUP BY raises (use GROUP BY); a row query may not select a string constant yet (F-156); FLOAT columns and `/` work (F-157 merged; the float registers are F-157's, one implementation). Closes F-150's open line: HAVING and post-aggregate expressions run on the executor.
- Tests: `tests/test_mojo_rows.py` (28 queries x table / scan / frame / framescan at 20,000 and 200,000 rows and the limited ones at 1.2 million rows with ties, against DuckDB; the path that ran asserted; heaps vs full sort vs early stop; stable ties; EXPLAIN; frame formula columns; row-query errors), `mojo/tests/switches/test_switches.mojo` (row queries with no thread pool), the Mac link probe; F-150's 443 SQL tests, F-151's `mx` tests and all Mojo tests unchanged except the three error tests for the forms that now work. ## F-154 M8: LEFT, SEMI and ANTI joins; IN / EXISTS / NOT IN / NOT EXISTS subqueries (uncorrelated) [done]
Needs: F-153 · Size: M
- [x] LEFT JOIN (null build columns, fused and row output); SEMI / ANTI; IN (SELECT) / EXISTS → semi, NOT EXISTS → anti, NOT IN → null-aware anti; RIGHT / FULL / CROSS raise by name
- [x] build side = the right for left / semi / anti; presence bitmap or mark flags for semi / anti
- [x] equal to DuckDB with nulls on both sides, empty sides, NOT IN with and without NULLs, a left join counted per left row (Q13's shape)
- Built: `JK_*` join kinds through parser (`sql.mojo`: LEFT [OUTER] / SEMI / ANTI JOIN, `SubQuery`, X_SUBIN / X_EXISTS), binder (`_lift_subquery`: a WHERE subquery becomes the right table of a semi / anti join, scope rules, ON classification), plan (`Join (left|semi|anti|anti, null-aware)`), executor (`sqlexec.mojo`), `jfront.mojo` (`FM_LEFT`, presence filters), kernel (`hj_probe_pairs(outer)`, `hj_probe_select`, `hj_filter_block`, `hj_probe_left_block`, `hj_build(presence)`; C ABI `mx_hj_probe_pairs_outer`, `mx_hj_probe_select`, `mx_hj_build_presence`), `Query.join(how=left|semi|anti)`.
- Review round (F-153): `hj_probe_pairs` returns no arrays when nothing matches.
- Review round 1 (F-154): a WHERE equality between the tables of a LEFT / SEMI / ANTI join is a post-join filter, not a key (it removes NULL-extended rows); presence-only maps refuse probes that return build rows (-34); a subquery over the same table with its own alias works; kfuzz2 (outer pairs, select modes, presence maps, 184 builds, threads 0/1/3/5/7/24/64) 0 bad.
- Deviations: EXISTS needs a correlated equality (an uncorrelated EXISTS is a constant: refused, F-159); one subquery per query and none beside an explicit join (F-155); ON conditions that are not key equalities or right-only filters raise in LEFT / ANTI joins (a left-only condition is also refused in ANTI / LEFT, allowed in SEMI as a left filter); a LEFT join on hashed composite keys raises; a LEFT join with repeated build keys is materialized (the one-to-many expansion inside the fused probe is F-161); NOT IN is single-key (the null-aware rule is per key). NOT IN found one bug through the frame test: a probe value that cannot equal any build key (a TEXT word the list lacks) was treated as NULL.
- Informal timings (24 threads, lineitem 6M, orders 1.5M, customer 150k; mx ms vs DuckDB ms): lineitem LEFT JOIN orders GROUP BY 18 vs 48; lineitem IN (SELECT ..) 11 vs 15; NOT IN 12 vs 14; orders EXISTS (lineitem) 20 vs 70; orders NOT EXISTS 9 vs 35; customer NOT EXISTS (orders) 3 vs 7.5 (presence-only build: 11 ms to 2.4); customer LEFT JOIN orders (one-to-many, materialized) 34 vs 13: the gap F-161 closes.
- Reviews: round 1 NEEDS WORK (a WHERE equality after a LEFT join became a join key: unmatched rows kept), round 2 NEEDS WORK (a same-table subquery's `t.col` bound to the outer row: IN answered 138 for 10,255), both fixed at the root (non-inner keys only from ON / USING; names resolve innermost scope first); round 3 PASS (870 adversarial runs equal DuckDB across 9 configurations, 36 deliberate refusals; kernel fuzz 184 builds 0 bad). Open: an EXISTS with no correlation is refused (constant EXISTS, F-159); TEXT = TEXT filters (F-156); one-to-many LEFT is materialized (34 vs 13 ms, F-161)

## F-155 M8: multi-way joins and the cost-based planner (join order, build sides, EXPLAIN ANALYZE) [done]
Needs: F-154 · Size: L
- [x] join trees flattened to leaves + key edges + residuals (the `join_engine._flatten` model); leaf filters first, exact filtered counts; greedy order over every start leaf (port of `plan_order`); build side per join; star probes in one fused stage; cross-leaf residuals as soon as their leaves are joined; OR common factor
- [x] `PlannerOptions`: written order, forced build side, forced fused / materialized, memory budget; decisions in EXPLAIN, actual rows per operator in EXPLAIN ANALYZE
- [x] equal to DuckDB on 3-6 table joins (TPC-H Q3, Q5, Q7, Q8, Q9, Q10 shapes, dbgen SF0.1) and with every forced order; the planner's choice never slower than the written order beyond noise (informal)
- Built: parser (`a JOIN b ON .. JOIN c ON ..`, `a, b, c`, `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`), binder (join graph: `edge_a / edge_b`, `leaf_conj`, `resid` with table masks; OR common factor `factor_or`), `planner.mojo` (`JoinStats`, `JoinPlan`, `rule_join_order`, `Planner.join_order` with a default body, new `PlannerOptions`: `written_order`, `join_order`, `build_side`, `join_budget`), `sqlexec.mojo` (`_run_multiway`: leaf filters first with exact counts, `column_ndv` sampled distinct counts, steps with `_join_step`, residual filters after the step that joins their tables, the last join as the two-table `_run_join_query`; `_run_star` + `_build_cluster`: a tail of unique-key dimensions in one fused pass; `_explain_analyze`), `jfront.mojo` (`StageData`, `_jf_star`: stages probed in turn, partner rows kept by block row), kernel (`hj_probe_block(by_row)`, `MapView.store` / `load_view`; library rebuilt), `table.mojo` (`WordDict` shared by `ArcPointer`: a join's gather and a virtual schema share dictionaries instead of copying every word), `mx --order / --build / --join-budget`.
- Also in the two-table join (found on the way: Q3's lineitem filter wrote 3.2M rows in 63 ms for a 4 ms kernel): a big plain table with a filter on the probe side is counted first and, when at least half of it passes and the join is fused, read unfiltered with its filter in the pipeline (no filtered copy).
- Tests: `tests/test_mojo_multiway.py` (52: TPC-H Q3 / Q3 with JOIN syntax / Q5 / Q7 / Q8 (8 tables) / Q9 (composite key) / Q10 / Q19 (OR factor) shapes on dbgen SF 0.1 against DuckDB under the planner's order, the written order, up to 24 forced orders each (a refused order is a cross product or a memory-budget error), forced build sides, materialized, one thread, streamed scans; stars and a snowflake; EXPLAIN / EXPLAIN ANALYZE; the refusals), `mojo/tests/test_multiway.mojo` (the planner on plain statistics, a custom `Planner` choosing the order, three Frames with edits and a merge under every order and build side against their baked tables), `test_hashjoin.mojo` (by-row block probe).
- Deviations: the TPC-H shapes drop EXTRACT / CASE / LIKE (F-156); LEFT / SEMI / ANTI with a third table, `USING` with three tables, cross products and a subquery beside a join raise by name (F-159); the cost model does not know about the star, so it can pick an order slightly slower than the written one when the written order stars (Q5: 40 ms vs 35); no bushy plans beyond a star's dimension groups.
- Informal timings (SF 1, 24 threads, mx ms: planner / written order / DuckDB; machine shared, load 5-10): Q3 32 / 52 / 27, Q5 41 / 34 / 13, Q7 72 / 85 / 16, Q8 26 / 24 / 13, Q9 73 / 63 / 112, Q10 69 / 64 / 25, Q19 24 / 22 / 23; stars (lineitem with orders, part, supplier): 39 vs DuckDB 80, with nation behind supplier 37 vs 61, two dimensions 16 vs 12. The planner's choice is within noise of the written order everywhere except Q5 (+17%) and beats it on Q7 (-15%) and Q3. Q10 429 to 59 ms and Q9 to 60 ms came from sharing dictionaries; Q5 1,258 to 41 ms from distinct counts replacing the base-size heuristic. Remaining gaps vs DuckDB: filtered fact tables that pass under half of their rows are still written (Q10: 25%) and the join order's cost model does not see fused stages: F-161.
- Review: PASS (698 multi-way runs + 227 Frame reruns equal DuckDB under every permutation, forced build side, materialized, one thread and streamed; shared dictionaries proven copy-on-write, no leaks; filtered-probe fusion 624 runs, boundary exact at 50%). Next for F-161: star / bushy aware order search: the largest filtered table as the one probe, every branch a group of unique-key maps probed in one fused pass, the fact filter kept in the pipeline whatever its selectivity, cycles (Q5's c_nationkey = s_nationkey) checked in the pass (removes every intermediate table in Q5 / Q7 / Q10). Follow-ups: kernel-hash test should also hash the .so itself; document or enforce the table limit (9 work)

## F-156 M8: SQL expressions: CASE, COALESCE, CAST, text and date functions [done]
Needs: F-150 (parallel with F-153..F-155) · Size: M
- [x] CASE WHEN, COALESCE / IFNULL, NULLIF, exact CASTs, IN lists of any length (the kernel's IN set), `IS DISTINCT FROM`
- [x] text functions once per dictionary word (a TEXTMAP: code remap or new dictionary): LIKE / ILIKE, starts_with, contains, lower / upper / trim / substr / length / ||; TEXT = TEXT between columns, across a join too
- [x] dates: EXTRACT, date_trunc, date ± INTERVAL, date differences in integer civil-date arithmetic
- [x] division / modulo by zero, decimal result scales, integer `/` → FLOAT as DuckDB; past the register limit a readable error, never a wrong answer; equal to DuckDB per function × nulls × case / accents × leap years and pre-1970 dates

Built (2026-10-19): rules, the table of what is supported and the differences in `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md` ("Expressions (F-156)"). Kernel: three opcodes in `kernels_aot/pipeline_cpu.mojo` (`OP_TAB`: a table lookup by code, `OP_ISET`: an int64 hash set, `OP_FLAG`: the error flag; `libmxpipeline_cpu.so` and `libmxexec_cpu.so` rebuilt and force-added). Parser (CASE, CAST / TRY_CAST / `::`, LIKE ... ESCAPE, `||`, IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM / TRUE / FALSE, INTERVAL, EXTRACT, `SUBSTRING(s FROM a FOR b)`), binder (a marked section in `bind.mojo`: one type for the branches, constant folding of text and date functions, derived TEXT dictionaries named `src = DERIVED_BASE + node`), emitter (`sqlprog.mojo`: `prep_dict` makes each derived dictionary once, a text function is a table over the words of the dictionary it reads, CASE evaluated forward with a branch guard for error flags, civil dates in the integer opcodes, or a per-day table when the date column's range is short against its rows: guarded when the range is a footer's, `FOOTER_LIE` reruns the query without statistics), `sqlfn.mojo` (the scalar functions on values), `unicase.mojo` (DuckDB's case mapping, generated by `scripts/gen_unicase.py`). The group key planner knows which expressions can be NULL (`planner.expr_nullable`) and bounds date parts, truncated dates and dates moved by months by the column's range (dense groups); the executor reads `src` for derived TEXT keys and aggregates.
- Tests (all against DuckDB, kinds and scales too): `tests/test_mojo_exprs.py` (1,290 cases: each function over a table, a frame after 250 edits and a merge, a streamed file, a frame over it; the per-word path asserted from the notes), `test_mojo_expr_scale.py` (every day 1800-2400 and every 5th day of years 1-9999, 60,000 UTF-8 words, value by value), `test_mojo_expr_fuzz.py` (1,500 random expressions as select item, filter, key and aggregate), `test_mojo_cast_fuzz.py` (`mojo/tools/cast_probe.mojo`: each text cast against DuckDB's TRY_CAST on thousands of generated words), `test_mx_exprs.py` (through `build/mx` and `mx_oracle`), `mojo/tests/test_sql_parse.mojo`; the old "not supported yet (F-156)" tests became tests of the new syntax.
- Decisions: CAST from TEXT has three outcomes per word (reads / certainly not / a DuckDB quirk not read: an error even for TRY_CAST), so no cast ever turns a word DuckDB reads into NULL; `reverse` refuses words DuckDB reverses by grapheme clusters; `%` on a FLOAT stays refused (the kernel's remainder is not C's fmod); `//` on DECIMAL / FLOAT is DuckDB's (a division, NULL for a zero divisor); `date + INTERVAL` and `date_trunc` are DATEs (DuckDB: TIMESTAMP); `EXTRACT(epoch)` is a FLOAT as DuckDB's.
- Informal timings (5M generated rows and TPC-H SF1, under a load of 6 to 10; table in `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md`): MX frame wins `date_trunc` group (4.3 vs 20.6 ms), `date + INTERVAL` filter (3.7 vs 18.8), `datediff` (2.6 vs 7.7), `lower` / `||` / `substr` groups (5-8 vs 17-25), `LIKE` over 400 and 1M words (2.4 vs 5.5, 4.6 vs 8.4), the casts and the long `IN` (2.5 vs 6.1, 2.9 vs 6.5), `CASE` sums (5.0 vs 4.8, 6.4 vs 9.4); it loses `EXTRACT(year)` group (4.9 vs 3.6), `l_comment LIKE '%special%'` over 3.6M distinct words (23.7 vs 12.7) and `count(DISTINCT lower(h))` over 1M distinct words (336 vs 58: a text-making function builds each answer once, ~0.3 µs a word on one thread).
- Found on the way: DuckDB 1.5.1's optimizer answers wrongly when `INTERVAL '30' DAY` and `INTERVAL '1' MONTH` are in one SELECT (it takes them for one expression): the oracle runs with `pragma disable_optimizer`. A new expression op the planner did not know merged the NULL group into a word (fixed in `expr_nullable`, lessons).
- Not done / deferred: a `LIKE` pattern that is not a constant; `||` of a non-constant INT or DATE (CAST it first); `CAST(expr AS VARCHAR)` of an expression or of a FLOAT; two columns' `||` past 1,048,576 pairs of words; `%` on a FLOAT; `abs`, `round`, `floor`, `strftime`, regular expressions, window functions; ISO `week`; BC dates and years past 9999 as text; intervals in hours or less (no timestamp kind); GPU. Text functions give each distinct word once, so a column with millions of distinct words costs one pass over them (the 3.6M-word `%special%` is 20 ms, built in parallel).
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK, one silent wrong answer (a FLOAT cast to INT kept its float flag, so a negative halfway value grouped as a NaN), fixed at the root (the flag is cleared; every key emitter now supplies a NULL flag register where the planner says nullable), plus TRY_CAST scaling overflow is NULL (range checked before the multiplication), a constant failing cast in an untaken CASE branch no longer raises at bind time, AND / OR / WHERE evaluate the right side only where the left does not decide (a CAST error flag in a later conjunct cannot fire on excluded rows). Open (F-161 / F-167): text-making functions (`lower`, `replace`, ...) build their per-word results serially and dedup by hash (`count(DISTINCT lower(h))` over 1M words: 336 ms vs 58 ms): build in parallel slices and merge once, and skip the dedup for one-to-one functions. Seen, not from F-156: text order between two columns / a literal is the engine's case-insensitive order (`text_ranks`), DuckDB compares bytes (`s < s2`); BC dates print as `0000-12-01` for DuckDB's `0001-12-01 (BC)`.
- Review round 1: NEEDS WORK (a FLOAT cast to INT / DECIMAL kept its float flag: a negative value as a group key, DISTINCT key or min / median became a NaN pattern), fixed with the nullable-key flag register, TRY_CAST range checks, deferred constant casts and short-circuit AND / OR / WHERE; round 2: PASS (1,216 expression runs, the F-150 battery 1,104 and F-152 312 unchanged; differences only by decision: case-insensitive text comparison, BC dates printed as year 0000)

## F-157 M8: FLOAT64 as a column kind [done]
Needs: F-150 (parallel) · Size: L · real files and the repo's own TPC-H Parquet (`float` money columns) were refused before
- [x] FLOAT kind (float64 bits in the Int64 column); Parquet FLOAT / DOUBLE read and written; CSV exponents and decimals past 4 places as FLOAT when asked
- [x] comparisons, arithmetic, SUM / AVG in float64 with a defined order (reproducible, F-064), MIN / MAX, group and join keys with -0.0 = 0.0 and one NaN, NaN ordered as DuckDB
- [x] equal to DuckDB (1e-12 relative; keys exact) on the repo's TPC-H SF1 Parquet for single-table queries

Built (2026-10-18): rules in `docs/mxframe-mojo-api.md` ("FLOAT64 (F-157)"). Parquet DOUBLE / FLOAT (PLAIN, dictionary, BYTE_STREAM_SPLIT) read, FLOAT widened (its column keeps `scale == 32` so SQL compares it with a literal in 32 bits, as DuckDB does; without it TPC-H Q6 drops the 0.07 rows), written as DOUBLE; footer min / max of a float are never read. CSV: an exponent, more than 4 places or nan / inf in the first rows, or `CsvOptions(float_columns)`; correctly rounded (exact fast path, `strtod` for the rest; `_strtod` joins the libSystem list `check_mac_link.sh` prints). Builder (`Expr` float literals and `/`, `lit_float`, `lit_f32`, `Query.threads`, `QueryResult.floats / is_float`), SQL (exponent literals, row-level float arithmetic and compares, IN, SUM / AVG / MIN / MAX / median / quantile_disc / count distinct, hashed float keys with ORDER BY), Frame (`set_float`, float text, float formulas, sort, bake, save). SUM / AVG use the kernel's reproducible mode; a float-sum scan runs row group by row group so the order never depends on the cores; MIN / MAX leave NaN out of the kernel and count it. Changes you may not expect: a CSV column with more than 4 places is now FLOAT (it was a rounded DECIMAL); the Frame's null marker is a NaN (the old one, INT64_MIN + 1, is -5e-324); a float query never runs on the GPU.
- Review round 1 fixes: DuckDB's REAL model in SQL (REAL with INT / DECIMAL / REAL is 32-bit, with a DOUBLE or an exponent literal 64-bit, REAL arithmetic rounded to 32 bits, unmodelled forms raise by name; TPC-H is now compared with native DuckDB); a base cell is null by its validity bit only (any value, the old and the new marker included, is data); CSV: more than 4 places is FLOAT by default (as DuckDB: an exact DECIMAL(6) has no int128 behind it, so `sum(rate * qty)` would raise), exact DECIMAL is the opt-in `CsvOptions(decimal_columns)`, wider than int64 is FLOAT; BYTE_STREAM_SPLIT for INT32 / INT64; `strtod_l` in the C locale; FLOAT text is the shortest that reads back (`floatfmt.mojo`); the float-sum bits' dependence on plan and source layout is documented (not removed).
- Not done / limits: `//` and `%` on a FLOAT, `CAST` / `::` (F-156), float join keys (no joins yet: F-153; the key normalisation is `holistic.float_key` and the hashed group words), a float group key in the builder (dense integer keys only), an integer past 2^53 meeting a float raises, GPU. DuckDB does REAL arithmetic in 32 bits; MX frame widens first, so the tests cast to DOUBLE inside arithmetic.
- Tests: `mojo/tests/test_float.mojo`; `tests/test_mojo_float_parquet.py`, `test_mojo_float_csv.py`, `test_mojo_float_sql.py`, `test_mojo_float_sql_paths.py` (frames after 250 edits, threads, 32-bit column, TPC-H SF1 Q1 / Q6 / by discount / median), `test_mojo_float_builder.py` (table, scan, frame, frame over a scan; bit-identical sums over threads / morsels / block size).
- Reviews: round 1 NEEDS WORK (REAL compared by expression shape: silent wrong counts), round 2 (exact DECIMAL for 5-9 CSV places overflowed ordinary products without int128), round 3 (shortest text at powers of two), all fixed; round 4 PASS (1,999,738 doubles print exactly as Python repr; 15,600 + 3,000 fuzz runs; TPC-H SF1 equal to DuckDB). Final rules: REAL typed through expressions as DuckDB; CSV past 4 places or wider than int64 is FLOAT, `CsvOptions(decimal_columns=)` opts into exact DECIMAL; base nulls from validity bits; strtod_l in the C locale; float sum bits documented as depending on plan / streaming / grouping

## F-161 M8: join speed: m:n inside the fused probe, radix-partitioned probe, runtime join filters [todo]
Needs: F-155 · Size: L · engine-first; F-058's losses are joins
- [ ] one-to-many expansion inside the front stage (no materialized pairs for aggregates over m:n joins), a LEFT join with repeated build keys included (F-154 review, bench154: customer LEFT JOIN orders GROUP BY nation runs 34 ms and the top-10 per customer 42 ms, both on the `materialized: 1,500,000 pairs (unmatched rows included)` path, vs DuckDB 13 and 28; every fused or presence-only case beats DuckDB)

- [ ] write a probe side's filtered rows less (Q10, Q3 with a selective fact filter: evaluate a cheap filter before the probe in the front stage); teach the join order's cost model about fused stars and group-by-first strategies (F-155 review notes)
- [ ] the Python engine's joins call the same kernel (closing F-046a slice 1; `libmxhashjoin_cpu.so` and its C ABI exist since F-153, nothing in Python calls them yet: "shared with the Python engine" is open), suites green
- [ ] `Query.join` (F-153 review): it gathers every column of both tables and never fuses, so a big fact-to-dimension join hits the 3 GiB budget through the builder while the same join runs fused in SQL; carry only the columns the query after it reads and fuse where SQL does
- [ ] informal timings on dbgen SF1 / SF10 (Q3, Q12, Q18 shapes, the random suite's join shape) through `mx` vs DuckDB, A/B against F-155
